# 00_04 — Master Finding Register

**Menstrual Data Integrity & Active Bleeding Journey wave update (PR #4, `feat/menstrual-data-integrity`, unmerged, 2026-09-18/19):** a separate, charter-driven engagement (see `docs/menstrual-data-integrity-contract.md` for full architecture) building `bleeding_episodes`/`bleeding_observations`/`cycle_baselines` and the active-bleeding journey. Full requirement-level mapping lives in `00_12_TRACEABILITY_MATRIX.md`'s own new section. Three genuine, previously-undetected defects found and fixed during this wave are registered here:

| Finding | Audit | Native severity | Master class | Summary | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `MDI-001` | Hardening 5 SQL adversarial pass (live-verified) | Newly discovered | **MEDIUM — VERIFIED_CLOSED (2026-09-19)** | `REVOKE ALL ON FUNCTION ... FROM PUBLIC` on 8 new/converted RPCs (including the correction-authority RPC) did not actually block `anon` from executing any of them — Supabase's own canonical baseline runs `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES FOR ROLE postgres IN SCHEMA public GRANT ALL ON FUNCTIONS TO anon`, which grants directly to the named role, not via `PUBLIC`. Directly confirmed live (`has_function_privilege('anon', ...)` returned `true` for all 8 before the fix). Fixed by explicitly revoking from `anon` too; re-verified live (`false` for all 8, and a direct `anon`-role RPC call now returns `permission denied`). **The identical pattern (`W1-002`, already registered above) exists in the pre-existing, already-deployed `check_and_increment_ai_rate_limit`** — not touched this wave, since fixing it safely requires confirming nothing legitimately depends on pre-auth `anon` access to it | **VERIFIED_CLOSED** |
| `MDI-002` | Commit D9 reconciliation wiring (code-review, this session) | Newly discovered | **MEDIUM — VERIFIED_CLOSED (2026-09-19)** | `BleedingEpisodeRepositoryImpl.reconcilePendingOperations`'s switch statement called `endEpisode`/`recordObservation`/`correctObservation`/`saveBaseline` — each of which returns `null` on failure (including an ordinary RPC error already caught and reported internally) rather than throwing — without checking the return value before falling through to `clearPending`. A genuine RPC failure during reconciliation would therefore have been silently treated as success and the pending operation permanently dropped, losing the retry entirely — the exact class of silent data loss this charter exists to prevent. **Affected the pre-existing `endEpisode` case too**, not only the operation types added this wave. Fixed by checking each nullable result and throwing on `null`, routing it into the same "leave pending" path every other failure in that loop already uses; re-confirmed via the existing "leaves pending on failure" test pattern for every affected operation type | **VERIFIED_CLOSED** |
| `MDI-003` | Export/deletion audit (code-review, this session) | Newly discovered | **LOW — VERIFIED_CLOSED (2026-09-19)** | `data_export_builder.dart`'s `exportSections` never included `bleeding_episodes`/`bleeding_observations`/`cycle_baselines` — a real personal-data-export gap for every user of the new canonical model. Account **deletion** was never actually broken (verified: `auth.users`→`public.users`→these 3 tables all cascade via pre-existing `ON DELETE CASCADE` foreign keys in the canonical baseline) — only export was missing them. Fixed by adding the 3 sections; the existing generic per-section test loop (`data_export_resilience_test.dart`) picked them up automatically, 9/9 passing | **VERIFIED_CLOSED** |


**Wave 0 update (Remediation Session, 2026-09-04):** Full detail in `00_10_WAVE0_EXECUTION_REPORT.md`. Two new findings discovered via live-schema behavioral validation, added below with fresh IDs (native audit register did not and could not find these — they required live database access):

| Finding | Audit | Native severity | Master class | Summary | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `W0-001` | Wave 0 (Database, live-verified) | Newly discovered | **BLOCKER — VERIFIED_CLOSED (W0-003 remediation wave, 2026-09-05)** | Live production table is named `prayer_log`; `prayer_tracking_repository_impl.dart` queried `.from('prayer_entries')`, which does not exist live. Every remote prayer-tracking call failed and silently fell back to local-only data via the existing `ROOT-005` catch-all pattern. **The table-name fix (querying `prayer_log`) was made and is confirmed still in place.** Its own closure criteria required "a live remote read/write round-trip test to confirm `prayer_log` now resolves correctly" — not run until this wave. **That test has now been run and passed** (`00_09` §21 Phase D/F), against a live-schema-derived isolated environment, only possible now that `W0-003` and the newly-discovered `W0-004` (both blocking every write, independent of the table name) are also fixed | **VERIFIED_CLOSED** |
| `W0-002` | Wave 0 (Database, live-verified) | Newly discovered | **BLOCKER — CODE_COMPLETE / LOCALLY_VERIFIED (W0-002 wave, 2026-09-06), NOT YET DEPLOYED TO PRODUCTION** | Live production table is named `pregnancy_records`; `pregnancy_tracking_repository_impl.dart` queries `.from(_tableName)` where `_tableName='pregnancy_milestones'`, which does not exist live. **Deeper root cause found this wave, not previously documented**: `PregnancyTrackingScreen` (the only consumer of this code path) is unreachable from any navigation route in the entire app — confirmed via exhaustive grep, and via its own git history (present, unwired, since the very first commit). Separately, `pregnancy_records` itself — the table this finding originally assumed was the intended backing store — is **not queried by any current Flutter code at all**; it was superseded by `pregnancy_profile` (a later, correctly-designed, actively-used table) at some point, and left live but orphaned. **Resolved**: a new `pregnancy_milestones` table (`supabase/migrations/20260907090000_pregnancy_milestones.sql`) — a direct per-user child table (FK to `users(id) ON DELETE CASCADE`), matching this schema's existing `wellbeing_logs` convention, **not** nested under the orphaned `pregnancy_records` (the app's own `PregnancyMilestone` entity has never had a pregnancy-parent-id field, and `pregnancy_profile`'s own `UNIQUE(user_id)` confirms this app has no concept of multiple concurrent/historical pregnancies needing one). `pregnancy_records` is left completely untouched — not read from, not migrated from, not dropped; its own disposition (real historical data vs. genuinely vestigial) is an explicit, separate, deferred question requiring live production read access this session doesn't have. The repository was rewritten to the real table with proper `AppErrorReporter` reporting (previously silently swallowed all remote failures) and a local-authoritative-with-sync `PregnancySyncStatus` matching `CycleLog`'s established pattern. The ViewModel's hardcoded fake LMP (`DateTime.now() - 30 weeks`, regardless of any real user input) was also replaced with real `PregnancyStatusController` state. **Fully validated against a disposable isolated local Supabase stack**: 14/14 RLS/CRUD tests (own-row CRUD, cross-user read/update/delete denial, unauthenticated denial, spoofed-user-id denial, malformed-input rejection), plus a direct account-deletion cascade test (milestone rows correctly removed via the `users` cascade, zero orphans), plus a combined fresh-restore test confirming this migration applies cleanly alongside the AI Security wave's pending migration. **Unreachable-screen question resolved this wave (Product Integration wave, 2026-09-06)**: classified **DUPLICATED BY ANOTHER CURRENT FEATURE**, not an unwired bug, on strong, multi-source evidence — (1) the dashboard's `_PregnancyOverview` already provides a materially richer, embedded pregnancy-status experience (week-by-week baby-size stages, progress percent, days-to-birth, log-birth action) than `PregnancyTrackingScreen` ever did; (2) the design reference itself (`src/components/PregnancyTracker.tsx`) is embedded inside `Today.tsx`, not a standalone page — confirming dashboard-embedded was always the intended architecture; (3) `PrayerTrackingScreen` shows the identical unreachable-since-first-commit pattern, confirming this is a consistent app-wide consolidation, not a pregnancy-specific accident; (4) the screen's "daily hydration/movement/symptom tracker" concept has no basis in the design reference and no other product evidence anywhere. **Not force-fit into navigation, per explicit instruction not to expose confirmed-abandoned UX.** File deletion was attempted and blocked by this session's own safety tooling (a destructive action); resolved instead via the charter's own offered alternative — the screen/ViewModel/calculator are kept in place, unchanged in behavior, with a prominent header comment documenting the full evidence trail so this is never mistaken for an oversight again. The underlying data layer (`PregnancyMilestone`/`PregnancyTrackingRepositoryImpl`/the `pregnancy_milestones` migration) is deliberately preserved as correct, tested, dormant infrastructure — independently valid regardless of this specific UI's fate. A real, separate bug was caught and fixed in the same pass: the Data Export screen's own user-facing disclaimer text still claimed pregnancy-tracking data was excluded, even though this wave's own export fix (§26) had already started including it — corrected. **Superseded this wave (Dormant Pregnancy Tracking Retirement, 2026-09-06): the feature itself was retired rather than deployed.** Given the Classification-C evidence above was already conclusive that this UI was abandoned/superseded, deploying a new production table and schema surface for a feature no user can or should reach was judged the less safe option, not the more thorough one — carrying forward unused schema, unused RLS, and unused client code purely because it had already been built. **Retirement executed in full**: `lib/features/pregnancy_tracking/` (all 7 files: screen, ViewModel, repository interface/impl, entity, local data source, calculator), `test/pregnancy_tracking_test.dart` (10 tests), and the never-applied `supabase/migrations/20260907090000_pregnancy_milestones.sql` were all deleted outright (`git rm`), not merely commented out — explicitly authorized this wave, correcting the prior wave's blocked-deletion workaround. The local-storage cleanup registration (`local_sensitive_data_cleanup.dart`'s `'pregnancy_tracking'` entry) was also removed — safe with zero legacy-data risk, since the screen was never reachable from any navigation route in the app's entire history, meaning no real user could ever have written data under that local storage category to strand. **A real, live, previously-undetected production bug was found and fixed in the same pass**: `DataExportScreen` unconditionally queried `pregnancy_milestones` — a table that was never actually deployed to production — inside a single un-partitioned try block, meaning this one bad fetch was silently failing the **entire** data export for **every user**, not just the pregnancy-tracking portion (`PC-006` regression, now fixed by removing the fetch). **Closure criteria met**: no production code references `pregnancy_milestones`; no reachable product flow depends on it; no local cache remains for it; no pending sync mechanism remains for it (deleted along with the repository); the export no longer references it; no production migration is required (the pending one was deleted, never applied); `pregnancy_profile`/`PregnancyStatusController` behavior re-verified correct and unaffected (19/19 tests passing); `flutter test`/`dart analyze` re-run and confirmed clean of regressions. `pregnancy_records`' own disposition (Phase F, `00_09` §28) remains a separate, deferred, owner-level question — untouched by this retirement, exactly as it was untouched by the original migration this replaces | **VERIFIED_CLOSED — FEATURE RETIRED / DEAD PATH REMOVED (Dormant Pregnancy Tracking Retirement wave, 2026-09-06)** |
| `W1-001` | Gemini trust-boundary Operational Closure Checkpoint (live-verified) | Newly discovered | **BLOCKER — VERIFIED_CLOSED (re-remediated, URGENT PRODUCTION RECOVERY wave, 2026-09-09 — regressed once in production same-day and was recovered; see incident history in Summary)** | The in-memory, per-instance sliding-window rate limiter (`_shared/rate_limit.ts`, added to close `SEC-005`/`AB-002`/`AB-008`) was deployed to production and directly load-tested: 28 rapid authenticated requests against `ai-assistant-chat` produced **zero** `429` responses, because Supabase's Edge Runtime does not guarantee warm, single-instance reuse. **Replaced with a durable, Postgres-backed atomic counter** (`ai_rate_limit_counters` table + `check_and_increment_ai_rate_limit()` `SECURITY DEFINER` RPC), fully validated locally in the AI Security wave, then **deployed to production and independently re-verified end-to-end with real evidence, not assumed from the local pass**: migration checksum re-confirmed unchanged (`4b346d3f71...`) before applying; applied to production via the Management API's direct SQL-query endpoint (isolated to this one file only, `db push` never used); all objects confirmed correct in production (`ai_rate_limit_counters` + both indexes + the function, `SECURITY DEFINER` with explicit `search_path=public`, RLS enabled with zero policies, table grants restricted to `postgres`/`service_role` only). All 4 Edge Functions redeployed and confirmed via direct download-and-diff to be running the new RPC-backed code (previously still running the old in-memory version, verified before this deployment). **Production smoke tests, all real**: `ai-assistant-chat`/`dream-interpreter-chat`/`dr-niswah-chat` all returned real, coherent Gemini replies; malformed request → `400`; unauthenticated request → `401`; zero credential leakage. **Rate-limiter behavior directly re-proven in production, not re-derived from the local pass**: quota enforcement (3 allowed, then correctly rejected with a real `retry_after_seconds`); concurrency (10 parallel requests against a quota of 5 — exactly 5 `allowed`, exactly 5 rejected, every count value 1-10 appearing exactly once, zero race condition); identity isolation (two different users, same function/window, independent counters); `dr-niswah-chat` red-flag exemption re-confirmed (`urgent: true`, real reply, correctly bypassing the limiter). **Fail-closed behavior achieved genuine `PRODUCTION RUNTIME VERIFIED` status, not merely `CODE/LOCAL VERIFIED`**: `REVOKE EXECUTE` on the RPC from `authenticated`, then a real HTTP call correctly returned `503`/`"This feature is temporarily unavailable"` with no internal detail leaked and no Gemini call made, then `GRANT` immediately restored and normal operation re-confirmed with one more real successful call. All synthetic test accounts/threads/counter rows created for this verification were deleted immediately afterward — zero test artifacts remain in production. **🔴 REGRESSION DISCOVERED, AI User-State Context Layer wave, 2026-09-09**: while performing live synthetic verification for an unrelated wave (AICTX remediation), a real call to `ai-assistant-chat` returned `HTTP 503`/`"This feature is temporarily unavailable"` — the rate limiter's own documented fail-closed response. Investigated directly: `SELECT to_regprocedure('public.check_and_increment_ai_rate_limit(text,integer,integer)'), to_regclass('public.ai_rate_limit_counters')` **both return NULL** — the RPC function and its backing table are **completely absent from production**, confirmed via three independent methods (`pg_proc` lookup, `information_schema.tables` lookup, `to_regprocedure`/`to_regclass`), ruling out a query-syntax or schema-cache artifact. **This is not a full rollback**: `auth.users`/`cycle_entries` row counts and a synthetic account created seconds earlier in the same investigation were both present and current, proving the rest of the database is intact and up to date — only the `W1-001` migration's specific objects are gone. Root cause not yet determined by this session (no destructive action was taken by this session — investigated read-only, per this engagement's standing discipline). **Practical impact: all 4 AI features are currently returning 503 to every real user**, since every one of them calls this now-nonexistent RPC before reaching Gemini. **This finding is corrected from `VERIFIED_CLOSED` to `PARTIALLY_REMEDIATED`** — the evidence trail above remains accurate as a historical record of what was proven working on 2026-09-08, but does not reflect the current, live state of production, and per this engagement's own "never let a stronger contradictory signal go unresolved" evidence-hierarchy rule, the live 2026-09-09 evidence controls current status. **Not remediated by this session — flagged for immediate owner attention, re-applying the migration is a production DB mutation requiring explicit authorization, exactly as every prior W1-001-related change in this engagement has required** **🟢 RE-REMEDIATED, URGENT PRODUCTION RECOVERY wave, 2026-09-09**: explicit owner authorization received to re-apply the exact previously-verified migration (checksum re-confirmed unchanged, `4b346d3f71bfa87509139f81efd802877145032bbe16420b645ce195c99c2639`) against production. Applied via the Management API's direct SQL endpoint (`2026-09-09T07:05:22Z`–`07:05:24Z`, `HTTP 201`), isolated to this one file only — no other migration, no `supabase db push`, no ledger change. **Every object independently re-verified in production, not assumed**: table, function, both indexes, RLS (enabled, zero policies), table grants (`postgres`/`service_role` only), function grants (matching the known, unchanged `W1-002` quirk), `SECURITY DEFINER` with `search_path=public`. **All 4 AI Edge Functions re-verified live without redeploying any of them** — confirming the previously-deployed Edge Function code was already correct and only the DB layer needed restoring: `ai-assistant-chat` 200, `dream-interpreter-chat` 200, `dr-niswah-chat` normal 200/`urgent:false`, `dr-niswah-chat` red-flag 200/`urgent:true`, `fiqh-advisor-chat` 200 (known/approved degraded grounding-fallback state), malformed request 400, unauthenticated 401, zero credential leakage. **Rate limiter re-verified via direct RPC calls (zero Gemini cost)**: quota enforcement (3 allowed, 4th/5th correctly rejected with real decreasing `retry_after_seconds`) and identity isolation (independent per-user counters) both confirmed working again. All synthetic accounts/counter rows created for this verification were deleted and confirmed gone (`ai_rate_limit_counters` count = 0) immediately afterward. **Root cause of the 2026-09-09 disappearance — classified `PROBABLE_WITH_EVIDENCE`, not `CONFIRMED`, not `UNKNOWN`**: `postgres_logs` shows zero `DROP` (or any) statement of any kind referencing these objects, or at all, in the incident window — ruling out an explicit SQL drop through any logged channel. Direct log evidence instead shows a Postgres compute restart at `2026-09-08T07:41:32Z` ("received fast shutdown request") followed by WAL-archive-based recovery that explicitly logged **"starting point-in-time recovery to WAL location (LSN) 6/8E000460"** and stopped there, rather than replaying to the live pre-shutdown LSN (`6/97002440`, confirmed numerically later/larger). That recovery LSN is earlier than the migration's own objects (independently confirmed: the objects were absent immediately after this recovery completed), meaning an infrastructure-level restart-and-partial-WAL-replay event — not any statement this engagement or the app executed — silently reverted several minutes of production writes, including the `W1-001` migration, without ever issuing a logged `DROP`. The specific external trigger for the compute restart itself (planned maintenance, host event, or platform auto-restart) could not be attributed — no project/organization audit-log endpoint was reachable via the public Management API (`GET .../organizations/{id}/audit` → 404) — so the mechanism is evidenced directly from Postgres's own recovery log, but the ultimate cause of the restart is not. **Recurrence risk: structural, not eliminated** — this is platform/infrastructure behavior outside this repository's control (this project has `pitr_enabled: false`); any future compute restart could, in principle, similarly revert recent unarchived writes, not just this specific migration's objects. **Prevention added, detection-only per explicit instruction (no self-healing DB mutation)**: `scripts/check_ai_rate_limit_objects.sh` — a non-mutating, read-only production health check (verified working live against production, exit 0) that an owner can run manually or wire into their own monitoring/cron/CI to detect this specific failure mode before real users do. Recommended, not yet actioned: owner discussion with Supabase support about this compute tier's restart/WAL-archival behavior and whether enabling PITR reduces this risk. **Status corrected back to `VERIFIED_CLOSED`** — the 2026-09-09 regression and recovery are preserved above as incident history, not erased, per this engagement's own discipline of never erasing the fact that a production regression occurred. **🟢 AUTOMATED DETECTION ADDED, W1-001 Schema Sentinel wave, 2026-09-09**: the manual detection script above was wired to run automatically — a new hourly-scheduled GitHub Actions workflow (`.github/workflows/w1001-sentinel.yml`) runs `scripts/w1001_sentinel_check.sh`, which checks both objects' presence using only the project's public anon/publishable API key (no privileged Management API token or DB password stored in CI), distinguishing "missing" from "present but access-correctly-denied" via PostgREST's own distinct error codes (`PGRST205`/`PGRST202` = missing; `42501`/`28000` = present). Verified with three real, executed GitHub Actions runs, not just local reasoning: a healthy-state run passed; a simulated missing-table run and a simulated missing-function run (deliberately-wrong object names, never touching the real production objects) both correctly failed with the right diagnosis in the log. Confirmed non-mutating (production row count unchanged, `0`, before and after all three test runs) and confirmed no key/secret value appears in any of the three runs' logs. A failed run is the alert surface — visible in the GitHub Actions tab and covered by GitHub's default failure notifications — no additional integration was built. | **VERIFIED_CLOSED (re-remediated 2026-09-09 — regression + same-day recovery preserved as incident history above, not erased; root cause `PROBABLE_WITH_EVIDENCE`: infra-level compute-restart + partial WAL recovery, not a logged SQL statement; non-mutating detection added: `scripts/check_ai_rate_limit_objects.sh`)** |
| `W1-002` (**new**) | Production DB Change Safety Gate wave, Phase G local re-test (2026-09-06) | Newly discovered | **LOW — functionally safe, defense-in-depth gap** | `20260906090000_ai_rate_limit.sql`'s function-level `REVOKE ALL ON FUNCTION check_and_increment_ai_rate_limit(...) FROM PUBLIC` does not strip the canonical baseline's schema-wide `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES FOR ROLE postgres IN SCHEMA public GRANT ALL ON FUNCTIONS TO anon` default grant, because that grant is made directly to the `anon` role at function-creation time, not inherited via `PUBLIC` — unlike the same migration's table-level `REVOKE ALL ON ai_rate_limit_counters FROM PUBLIC, authenticated, anon`, which correctly names `anon` explicitly and does strip it. **Directly tested, not inferred**: against a fresh local restore, `anon` was confirmed to hold `EXECUTE` on the function (contradicting the migration's own comment, "keeps the function off the anon role's ... surface entirely"), but calling it as `anon` with no JWT claims set raised `ERROR: Not authenticated` from the function's own `auth.uid() IS NULL` check — the intended control still holds functionally, just via the wrong layer. Not a deployment blocker for `W1-001`; recommended follow-up (not yet made): add `REVOKE EXECUTE ON FUNCTION check_and_increment_ai_rate_limit(TEXT, INT, INT) FROM anon;` alongside the existing `authenticated`-only grant | OPEN — LOW severity, non-blocking, recommended follow-up not yet implemented |
| `W2-001` | Reliability + Observability Persistence-Recovery Remediation (analysis) | Newly discovered | **MEDIUM** | Evaluated per explicit instruction: does the new cycle sync-status/retry behavior itself create any additional reliability risk? One genuine, pre-existing (not newly introduced) residual risk identified: a `pending` entry has no durable recovery path beyond local device storage (`shared_preferences`) — if the app is uninstalled, local storage is cleared, or the device is lost/replaced before that entry ever successfully syncs, it is permanently lost with no way to detect or recover it, since it never reached the server. The new automatic-retry mechanism (`RR-001`'s fix) reduces how often this window is ever reached (a transient failure now gets multiple automatic retry chances instead of one terminal attempt) but does not eliminate the underlying local-only-until-synced window — that would require a fundamentally different architecture (e.g. a durable local outbox surviving reinstall, which `shared_preferences` cannot provide) | OPEN — inherent to the local-first design; mitigated, not eliminated, by this pass |
| `W0-003` | Backup/Recovery Remediation Wave, behavioral restore validation (2026-09-05) | Newly discovered | **BLOCKER — VERIFIED_CLOSED (2026-09-05)** | `prayer_tracking_repository_impl.dart` sent `PrayerStatus` enum values (`pending`/`completed`/`missed`/`excused`) that did not match the live database's `prayer_log_status_check` CHECK constraint (`prayed`/`qadha_required`/`lifted`/`missed`) — only `missed` overlapped, confirmed against the live-captured schema. **Fixed**: an explicit, bidirectional persistence mapping (`prayerStatusToDbValue`/`prayerStatusFromDbValue`) scoped to exactly the repository's remote boundary — `completed↔'prayed'`, `missed↔'missed'`, `excused↔'lifted'`; `pending` (never actually sent by the UI) throws defensively rather than silently writing an invalid value; unmapped database values (`qadha_required`) are reported via `AppErrorReporter`, not silently coerced. **Validated end-to-end** against a live-schema-derived isolated environment using the real repository code: all 3 real statuses round-trip correctly, updates work, no duplicates, the unknown-value path reports as designed, and the database's own constraint correctly rejects a truly invalid value. See `00_09` §21 for full evidence, including a real timezone bug caught and fixed in the same pass (see `W0-004`) | **VERIFIED_CLOSED** |
| `W0-004` (**new**) | W0-003 remediation, Phase D behavioral validation (2026-09-05) | Newly discovered | **BLOCKER — VERIFIED_CLOSED (2026-09-05)** | A second, distinct, currently-live production defect found while validating `W0-003`, in the exact same write path: `prayer_log.scheduled_time` is `timestamp with time zone` in the live schema, but `savePrayer()` sent `TimeOfDay.toJson()`'s `{hour, minute}` JSON object — every remote prayer_log write failed on this alone, independent of the status value, meaning `W0-003`'s fix could not have restored any real functionality without this also being fixed. **Fixed** with the same remote-boundary-only pattern; an initial version used `.toLocal()`, which made the round trip depend on the running machine's timezone — caught by its own test and corrected to a timezone-neutral UTC encoding (prayer times are a local wall-clock concept throughout this app, not a true cross-timezone instant). **Validated end-to-end** against the live-schema-derived isolated environment, confirmed on a second independent run after the timezone correction | **VERIFIED_CLOSED** |

Status changes to existing findings from Wave 0 (full evidence in `00_10`): `BR-007` resolved (Storage confirmed unused); `DI-012` upgraded from structural inference to behaviorally confirmed (account deletion cascades away the other conversation participant's own messages too); `DI-001`/`BR-002` further deepened (two new `UNKNOWN`-classified live-only objects: `chat_history`, `secret_vault`); `PC-002` scope further informed (`delete_my_account()` confirmed to work correctly and completely, but its shared-conversation cascade needs product/privacy sign-off before client-wiring).


Status: **ALL WAVES COMPLETE (1–6).** Wave 6 (Post-Launch Monitoring) plan authored at `production-readiness-results/post-launch/PL_monitoring_plan.md` — infrastructure to execute it does not yet exist (see that document's Part B). Final master consolidation is in `00_05` through `00_08`. **Final master verdict: 🔴 NO-GO** — see `00_08_FINAL_PRODUCTION_READINESS_REPORT.md`.

Status: **Wave 5 COMPLETE.** Final Pre-Launch User Journey (PJ) audit ingested below — **NO-GO**, with 4 new PJ0 (Critical) and 1 new PJ1 (High) finding discovered only through end-to-end cross-layer tracing, not visible to any single specialist audit.

**The single most consequential new discovery:** `PJ-001` traces the exact mechanism behind `DI-004`'s open critical unknown — 7+ core tables (including chat threads/messages, the `flagged_conversations` safety log, cycle entries, and community content) FK to a bare `public.users` table that no code path in the entire app ever writes to, while the codebase's own migration history is internally inconsistent about whether tables should reference `auth.users` or this bare `users` table. If live-unverified `DI-004`/`PJ-001` resolves unfavorably, this is very likely the same failure `BR-002` already proved by direct migration-replay trace, now identified as a *live, ongoing* write-path failure, not just a schema-recoverability one.

Chained onto that, `PJ-004` found that when a red-flag (urgent, safety-relevant) message's backend call fails, **three separate persistence failures stack**: the clinical audit-log entry, the user's own message, and even the reassuring safety banner shown to her are all lost — the entire exchange vanishes with zero trace on next app open. `PJ-006` found this isn't just a gap but an architectural guarantee: the Doctor's Report red-flag section cannot distinguish "nothing happened" from "every safety record failed to save," so a real doctor reading it has no way to know it's incomplete. `PJ-005` pinpointed the exact live trigger for the private-messaging demo-mode fallback (CQ-007): a per-screen-open session check that silently and repeatedly drops a user into fabricated conversations the moment her auth session dies mid-use, with no re-authentication prompt ever shown.

Status: **Wave 4 COMPLETE.** Privacy & Compliance (PC), Accessibility & UX (AU), and Analytics & Business Events (AE) ingested below.

**Wave 4 specialist verdicts:** Privacy & Compliance **NO-GO** · Accessibility & UX **NO-GO** · Analytics & Business Events **N/A** (independently re-confirmed with full evidence, not merely accepted from the provisional lean — see `AE-001`, a non-blocking observation about a dead, fully-priced "Niswah Plus" paywall UI that corroborates DI-004/DI-008's dormant premium-tier columns from the UI side).

Key new evidence: Privacy & Compliance found the sign-up consent checkbox is **purely cosmetic** — its state is never read by any sign-up button, so the app collects health/pregnancy/religious/location data from every user with no functioning consent gate at all (PC-001), and there is no account-deletion capability anywhere in the app despite the database being correctly designed to cascade it (PC-002). Accessibility & UX found 15 of 20 icon-only buttons have no accessible name for screen readers (AU-001, the audit's sole AU1), two of the app's four core fiqh-state colors fail WCAG AA contrast as text (AU-003), and — like Accessibility's own controlled-validation phase — could not execute any live interaction testing, which the template treats as blocking on its own (AU-009).

Status: **Wave 3 COMPLETE.** Observability (OB), Backup & Recovery (BR), and Release & Deployment (RD) audits ingested below (all three completed via subagent after the rate limit cleared).

**Wave 3 specialist verdicts:** Observability **NO-GO** (4 Critical findings — the most severe verdict of the audit) · Backup & Recovery **NO-GO** · Release & Deployment **NO-GO** (13 of 14 mandatory launch gates fail).

Key new evidence: Backup & Recovery independently *traced* (not inferred) that replaying the full migration sequence against an empty Postgres database halts partway through, directly confirming DI-001/DI-005's schema-recoverability concern by execution-order analysis rather than static comparison alone — the database genuinely cannot be rebuilt from this repository if lost. Release & Deployment found two new, purely mechanical release blockers independent of everything else: a static build number (`1.0.0+1`) with no increment process, which alone guarantees store rejection on any second upload; and a non-functional "Privacy Policy" text label with no actual link, a near-certain App Store/Play Store submission blocker for a health-data app. Observability confirmed, for four realistic failure scenarios (AI endpoint fully broken, an RLS data leak, a recurrence of the exact DI-002 incident, a launch crash), that **none would currently be detected by the team through any in-app or in-repo mechanism** — discovery would depend entirely on a user complaint in every case.

Status: **Wave 2 COMPLETE.** Functional QA (FQ), Performance (PF), and Reliability & Resilience (RR) audits are ingested below (performed directly by the lead auditor after all three delegated Wave-2 sub-agents failed on a session-wide rate limit before producing output — see note in each domain's findings file). Wave 1 status follows.

**Wave 2 specialist verdicts:** Functional QA CONDITIONAL GO · Performance CONDITIONAL GO · Reliability & Resilience **NO-GO** (two RR1/High findings, both confirmed to be the same systemic silent-failure pattern independently found by four other Wave 1 audits — see `ROOT-005` below, now the most cross-corroborated defect in the audit).

Status: **Wave 1 COMPLETE.** Security (SEC), Code Quality (CQ), Dependencies & Configuration (DC), API & Backend (AB), and Database & Data Integrity (DI) audits are all complete and ingested below. This file will be updated (not renumbered) after each subsequent wave. Finding IDs are never renumbered per master §16/§66.7.

**Wave 1 specialist verdicts:** Security NO-GO · Code Quality CONDITIONAL GO · Dependencies/Config NO-GO · API/Backend NO-GO · Database & Data Integrity NO-GO. Per master §42 (Specialist Verdict Propagation) and §19 (Blocker Propagation), any mandatory-audit NO-GO makes overall master GO impossible until resolved — four independent NO-GOs already guarantee the master verdict cannot be GO. Waves 2–6 continue per plan to produce a complete evidence baseline and remediation plan; the master verdict itself is finalized only in `00_08_FINAL_PRODUCTION_READINESS_REPORT.md`.

Native severities are preserved verbatim from each specialist register. The "Master class" column applies the master's normalization (§18): **BLOCKER** = specialist critical or explicit launch-blocking high; HIGH = serious/conditional; MEDIUM = bounded; LOW = backlog; INFO = observation.

| DI-001 | Database | DI1 High | **BLOCKER — VERIFIED_CLOSED (Migration Chain Reproducibility wave, 2026-09-06)** | `schema.sql` and `supabase/migrations/` had repeatedly, materially diverged — 10 of 19 documented tables had no `CREATE TABLE` anywhere in tracked migrations; migrations could not rebuild the schema from empty. Migration comments admit this already caused real production incidents (silent haid-entry write failures, missing `chat_threads` table). **This finding's own remediation category ("establish one schema source of truth") is now met**: `supabase/canonical_baseline/00_public_baseline_draft.sql` is that single verified source of truth (captured live, re-verified current this wave), the 12 historical migrations that caused this divergence are archived (`supabase/migrations_archive/`, no longer part of any replay path), and a real, tested, repeatable validation mechanism (`scripts/validate_migrations.sh`, integrated into CI) now exists specifically to prevent this exact failure mode from recurring on the next schema change — exceeding the finding's own literal ask ("a live `information_schema` dump... reconciled into a single authoritative file"). See `BR-002`/`00_09` §37 for the full evidence (two independent fresh rebuilds, both fully schema-contract-verified). Not retroactively claiming every historical write-failure incident this finding once listed was flawless — those were separately fixed in their own dedicated waves (prayer: `W0-003`/`W0-004`; community: `20260830140000`, already reflected in the baseline; cycle columns: already reflected in the baseline). **Regression found and fixed, BR-002 CI Migration-Reproducibility Repair wave, 2026-09-15**: the first migration added since this mechanism's original implementation to both alter a baseline-defined table and replace a baseline-defined function (`20260914120000_madhhab_authority_state.sql`, Fiqh Remediation Wave 1) exposed a real ordering defect in `validate_migrations.sh` itself — it started the local stack (which auto-applies `supabase/migrations/`) *before* applying the canonical baseline, backwards from the intended contract, previously invisible only because no earlier migration overlapped with a baseline-defined object. This is classified as a **validation-harness regression, not production schema corruption** — the actual production database was never affected (both migrations were already applied there directly and independently verified, unrelated to this local/CI reconstruction tooling); reproduced byte-for-byte locally (`psql:/tmp/baseline.sql:131: ERROR: function "create_user_profile" already exists with same argument types`, exit code 3, matching the real CI failure exactly) before being fixed by reordering the harness (hold migrations out → clean start → baseline → migrations applied explicitly in order → verify), with neither the two active migrations nor the baseline file itself modified. `verify_schema_contract.sql` extended (existing checks unchanged) with explicit Madhhab-authority-model coverage (column nullability/defaults, all 3 new constraints' presence *and* logic, the legacy constraint's absence, `create_user_profile()`'s applied body) so a future regression of this same kind fails loudly rather than silently reconstructing an incomplete schema. Verified end-to-end against a real local Docker/Supabase stack (not merely reasoned about) — original harness reproduces the failure, fixed harness exits 0 with zero FAIL rows — and independently reconfirmed on real GitHub Actions CI: all 4 required jobs (`Analyze & Test`, `Validate DB migration reproducibility (BR-002)`, `Build Android`, `Build iOS`) now pass on PR #1. **`DI-001`/`BR-002` remain `VERIFIED_CLOSED`** — this was a regression in the mechanism's own reconstruction ordering, now fixed and re-verified, not a reopening of the underlying finding | VERIFIED_CLOSED — process-level root cause resolved with a real, tested, CI-integrated prevention mechanism, not just a one-time fix; **reconstruction-order regression found and fixed, 2026-09-15, re-verified on real CI, classification unchanged** |
| DI-002 | Database | DI1 High (bordering DI0) | **BLOCKER — partially closed (Silent-Failure Remediation, 2026-09-05)** | Systemic pattern: cycle tracking, pregnancy tracking, and community repositories silently swallow remote write/read failures behind local-only or fabricated demo-data fallbacks. Migration comments confirm this already masked real data-loss incidents on core health-tracking data (haid/cycle logging). **Cycle tracking: fixed** — `saveCycleLog` now returns whether remote sync succeeded, reports failures via `AppErrorReporter`, and the UI shows a non-blocking "not backed up yet" indicator instead of the failure vanishing with zero trace; the `sync_status` column bug (successfully-synced rows stayed permanently `'pending'`) is also fixed. **Community (read paths): fixed** — `getPosts`/`getComments` no longer substitute fabricated fake content on a real `PostgrestException`; they report and propagate to the UI's already-correct error state (see `AB-010`). **`pregnancy_profile`: confirmed already correctly designed** — deliberately throws rather than swallows, per its own code comment; no change needed. **`pregnancy_tracking`/milestones: retired in full (Dormant Pregnancy Tracking Retirement wave, 2026-09-06)** — no longer deferred, no longer exists; the dead-code silent-catch pattern this finding originally flagged for this feature is moot because the feature itself is gone, not because it was fixed in place | OPEN — materially narrowed; the pregnancy-tracking portion of the original scope no longer exists to remediate |
| DI-004 | Database | UNKNOWN (could be DI0) | ~~BLOCKER~~ **RESOLVED FAVORABLY (Phase 0, 2026-09-04)** | No *tracked* code path ever populates the `users` table, the FK target for 14+ core tables. **Live `auth` schema dump reveals two live-only triggers on `auth.users`**, including `auth_users_create_profile` → `create_user_profile()`, which does populate `public.users` on every signup — this function/trigger exists **only in the live database**, absent from every tracked migration and `schema.sql`. Production is not currently broken. **But this is simultaneously the clearest proof of `DI-001`: critical, safety-relevant provisioning logic exists nowhere in version control** — a rebuild from `supabase/migrations/` alone would silently omit it and reintroduce the exact 100%-write-failure scenario this finding originally worried about, for every new signup, going forward. See `00_05` Phase 0 §UNK-006. | **CLOSED (favorable outcome; underlying drift now feeds `DI-001`/`ROOT-007` instead)** |
| DI-005 | Database | DI1 High | **BLOCKER** | `20260830140000_community_schema_reset.sql` contains `DROP TABLE ... CASCADE` guarded only by a code comment ("confirmed empty"), no enforced precondition — a landmine if ever replayed against non-empty data | OPEN |
| RR-001 | Reliability | RR1 High | **BLOCKER — PARTIALLY_REMEDIATED (Reliability+Observability Remediation, 2026-09-05; Reliability/Resilience Final Closure wave, 2026-09-06)** | No systematic retry/backoff or connectivity-awareness anywhere in the app — a single transient network failure is terminal, no automatic recovery, compounds DI-002. Per the native finding's own definition, the correct closure bar is **not** "does every operation automatically retry" but "for every active critical operation, is failure detected, surfaced truthfully, recoverable in a semantically-correct way, observable, and protected from duplicate/corrupt behavior." **Explicit reliability contract established this wave, applied per authority model** (see `00_09` §29 Phase C for full detail): `LOCAL_AUTHORITATIVE_WITH_SYNC` → local durable save, truthful pending/failed state, `AppErrorReporter`, bounded automatic retry, no duplicate remote row; `REMOTE_AUTHORITATIVE` → failure surfaced, input preserved, user-controlled retry, no false success, no background replay required. **Full active-write-path inventory performed** (dormant paths — the retired pregnancy tracker, and `PrayerTrackingScreen`'s `savePrayer`, itself confirmed unreachable and excluded per instruction, unchanged from the prior wave's observation, still not acted on) — every currently-active path now meets its model's contract: (1) **Cycle/haid** — `LOCAL_AUTHORITATIVE_WITH_SYNC`, automatic retry, unchanged. (2) **Pregnancy profile** (`RR-005`) — `REMOTE_AUTHORITATIVE`, correctly non-silent, manual retry, unchanged. (3) **Profile/account edits** — `REMOTE_AUTHORITATIVE`, correctly non-silent, unchanged. (4) **Community posts/comments** — **real gaps found and fixed this wave**: `createPost`/`addComment` used a bare `.insert()` with no client-supplied id, meaning a manual retry after a client-side timeout could create a duplicate row if the first attempt had actually reached the server; now upsert-safe via a stable, caller-generated id reused across retries (`CommunityFeedViewModel._pendingPostId`/`PostDetailViewModel._pendingCommentId`), matching the pattern already correctly used by `DreamInterpreterViewModel`. Registered as **`RR-006` (`VERIFIED_CLOSED`)**. (5) **AI chat persistence** (Fiqh Advisor / general assistant / dream interpreter, `ChatRepositoryImpl`) — **a real, previously-undocumented silent-failure gap found and fixed**: `_showAssistantReplyAndPersist`'s assistant-message catch and `persistUser`'s `onError` handler both silently discarded `chat_messages` persistence failures with zero observability (the DUAL/SPLIT AUTHORITY contract's response-delivery half was already correct — the live reply is never hidden or blocked by a persistence failure — but the persistence half was invisible). Both now report via `AppErrorReporter`; `sendMessage`/`createThread` also gained the same stable-id/upsert idempotency protection as community. Registered as **`RR-007` (`VERIFIED_CLOSED`)**. (6) **Private messaging** (`PrivateMessagingRepository.sendMessage`) — same two defects (silent `AppErrorReporter` gap + bare-insert duplicate risk), same fix applied. Folded into **`RR-007`**. (7) **Wellbeing check-in** (`WellbeingRepository.upsertToday`, called from `DashboardScreen._showWellbeingCheckIn`) — failure was already honestly surfaced to the user but never reported via `AppErrorReporter`; fixed. Registered as **`RR-008` (`VERIFIED_CLOSED`)**. (8) **Account deletion** — audited, already correctly designed (RPC failure blocks cleanup and signOut; cleanup failure is best-effort/reported/retried at next start and never blocks signOut or claims a false failure) — no code change needed, confirmed via direct code review, not freshly unit-tested this wave (no existing Supabase-client mock harness for this repository). (9) **Data export** (`PC-006`) — the exact "one bad table aborts everything" architecture that caused last wave's live regression was still structurally present for all 8 remaining datasets; redesigned so every section fetches independently, failures are isolated per-section, and the export now carries an explicit `export_complete`/`sections_unavailable` marker rather than ever silently omitting a section or aborting the whole export. (10) **Notifications/preferences** — device-local, `RR-002`/`RR-003` already non-silent, unchanged. **Conclusion, updated this wave (Reliability Evidence Closure, 2026-09-06)**: every currently-active write path now meets its authority model's contract **with executable, passing test evidence**, closing the exact gap the prior wave left open. Testability seams were added (all `@visibleForTesting`/interface-typed, none changing production behavior): `ChatViewModel.persistUserMessageForTesting`/`showAssistantReplyAndPersistForTesting` (public, `@visibleForTesting`, so the AI-persistence fix is directly testable without mocking `AiAdvisorService`/Supabase Functions); `lib/features/legal/domain/data_export_builder.dart`'s `ExportSectionFetcher` abstraction (so the per-section-isolation redesign is testable without a live/mocked `SupabaseClient`); `lib/features/auth/domain/account_deletion_orchestrator.dart`'s `performAccountDeletion` (so the deletion sequencing contract is testable without mocking Supabase Auth/RPC); `ProfileViewModel` retyped to the existing `AuthRepository` interface instead of the concrete `AuthRepositoryImpl` (a fake was already possible in principle, just blocked by an unnecessarily narrow type). **New tests, all passing**: `test/ai_chat_persistence_resilience_test.dart` (7 — id-reuse-on-retry, `AppErrorReporter` invocation, response-delivery independent of persistence failure), `test/data_export_resilience_test.dart` (9 — scenarios A–G plus JSON validity and stable section ordering), `test/account_deletion_resilience_test.dart` (6 — RPC-failure-blocks-cleanup, cleanup-failure-doesn't-block-signout, no false success, redundant-attempt handling), 3 new tests in `test/secure_local_store_test.dart` (startup-retry actually re-invokes previously-failed cleanup; a still-failing category safely remains pending across repeated restarts; independent users retried independently), 4 new tests in `test/profile_update_observability_test.dart` (a real, previously-unreported observability gap found and fixed in the same pass: `ProfileViewModel.updateProfile`/`setAnonymousMode` discarded the real error before wrapping it in a generic `AuthFailure`, with no `AppErrorReporter` call — fixed), 3 new tests in `test/private_messaging_test.dart` (retry-reuses-id, fresh id per new message, `AppErrorReporter` invocation). **Every active path in the Phase B inventory now has passing executable evidence of its own model's contract** — cycle tracking (prior waves' live forced-outage test), pregnancy profile (`RR-005`'s existing tests), profile/account edits (new), community (`RR-006`'s existing tests), AI chat + private messaging (`RR-007`'s new tests), wellbeing (`RR-008`, simple enough that code review was already sufficient evidence), account deletion (new), data export (new). Verified locally first (a synthetic garbage artifact, proving the script completes and correctly leaves cert fields empty for unsigned input) before pushing, avoiding a second blind CI cycle. **Second real run (`34216345677`, commit `448f0d0b...`, `build_number=5`) completed `success` end-to-end** — all 14 real steps passed, including the now-fixed checksum/manifest step and artifact upload. Independently re-verified, not merely trusted from the workflow's own self-report: downloaded the artifact (`GET .../actions/artifacts/10052388181/zip`), re-confirmed its SHA-256 against GitHub's own reported digest (`b1371f6cd275e2c45532ed83c126c0591382ec00fbaa0e50ef19a9f394b90822`, exact match), independently recomputed the APK's own SHA-256 (`47d66c9f1949876481288ee66fff9c45683655ff62a0aae659b09b80b7716b99`, exact match to the manifest), independently re-ran `apksigner verify` locally (real `CN=Niswah, OU=Mobile, O=Niswah` certificate, SHA-256 `6d888f0166f7897098fbdd3229ca7c441ec80428741ac0592e5504ab51726ecd` — identical across both runs, the real production signing key), and independently ran `aapt dump badging` (`package: name='com.niswah.niswah' versionCode='5' versionName='1.0.0'` — correct application ID, `versionCode` strictly greater than every prior known value: pubspec baseline `+2`, an earlier local drill's `3`, this same wave's failed first attempt's `4`). Retention independently confirmed via the artifact's own API metadata: `created_at: 2026-09-08T10:50:15Z`, `expires_at: 2026-12-07T10:36:28Z` — exactly 90 days, matching the workflow's configured `retention-days: 90`; `expired: false`. No Play Store/App Store publication occurred at any point (no such step exists in this workflow, by design). **`RD-009` = `VERIFIED_CLOSED`** — a real signed emergency artifact was produced, independently verified at every layer (checksum, signature, package ID, version, retention), and no release signing was weakened in the process (the same real release keystore/certificate was used throughout, never a debug or ad hoc key) | **VERIFIED_CLOSED (RD-009 Emergency Release Verification wave, 2026-09-08)** — real signed emergency artifact produced on live GitHub Actions infrastructure, independently re-verified (checksum, signature, package ID, versionCode, 90-day retention) outside the workflow's own self-report; one genuine CI-portability defect found and fixed along the way (`scripts/generate_release_manifest.sh`) |

| PJ-001 | Final User Journey | PJ0 Critical | **BLOCKER — VERIFIED_CLOSED (favorable; resolved Wave 0, 2026-09-04, but this row was never updated to reflect it until the Final User Journey / Upstream Write-Reliability Reconciliation wave, 2026-09-06)** | Traces the exact mechanism behind `DI-004`: 7+ core tables FK to a bare `public.users` no *tracked* code path ever writes to; migration history is internally inconsistent about which "users" table is correct. **This row sat stale for two months of remediation waves** — the underlying question was actually resolved, favorably, back in the very first Wave 0 remediation session (2026-09-04, see `00_05_UNKNOWN_ASSUMPTION_REGISTER.md`'s `UNK-006` resolution and `DI-004`'s own already-corrected row), but nobody had gone back to update `PJ-001`'s own dedicated row to match — this is exactly the kind of contradiction this wave existed to catch and fix. **Evidence** (live, not inferred): a read-only `supabase db dump --linked -s auth` (schema-only, no row data) revealed two live, active triggers on `auth.users` — `auth_users_create_profile` → `create_user_profile()` (a live-only function absent from every tracked migration and `schema.sql`) inserts into `public.users` on every signup, `ON CONFLICT (id) DO NOTHING`. `public.users` **is** populated for every real account today. This satisfies the finding's own native closure bar exactly ("cannot be elevated to Confirmed without one live query") — a schema-level trigger-definition dump is at least as strong as the row-count query originally specified. **Re-confirmed fresh this wave**: an authenticated, read-only `supabase functions list --project-ref jkmjobvxfrmuwafczvtw` was run directly (not assumed from the two-day-old record) to independently corroborate live tooling access still works and nothing has silently drifted since (see `PJ-004`'s row for that check's own primary purpose). **What remains open, but is tracked elsewhere, not here**: this provisioning trigger exists *only* in the live database, in no tracked migration — if the database were ever lost and rebuilt from `supabase/migrations/` alone, this exact 100%-write-failure scenario would newly, silently occur for every future signup. That risk is `DI-001`/`ROOT-007`'s territory (both already `OPEN`, unaffected by this correction) — a disaster-recovery/schema-drift risk, not a present-day write-correctness one, which is what `PJ-001` itself specifically claims and what this closure is scoped to | **VERIFIED_CLOSED — present-day write correctness for `public.users` confirmed live; the untracked-provisioning-logic risk this uncovered is separately tracked under `DI-001`/`ROOT-007`, not reopened here** |
| PJ-002 | Final User Journey | PJ0 Critical | **BLOCKER — PARTIALLY_REMEDIATED (2026-09-05)** | Full data-loss chain traced end-to-end: local-first UI + silent remote-write failure + no cross-device sync + zero "not backed up" warning anywhere — haid history is plausibly local-device-only. **"Zero warning" is fixed**: the UI now shows an accurate, status-specific, bilingual message distinguishing "will back up automatically" (genuinely true now — see `RR-001`) from "could not be backed up, please try again later" (non-retryable). **"Silent remote-write failure" is fixed** for the retryable case (real automatic retry now exists). **"No cross-device sync"** is unchanged — this finding is about real-time multi-device state, a different, larger architectural question, not attempted here | OPEN — the data-loss-with-no-warning defect is closed; cross-device sync remains a separate, unaddressed concern |
| PJ-004 | Final User Journey | PJ0 Critical | **BLOCKER — VERIFIED_CLOSED (deployed + live-verified 2026-09-05; re-confirmed live this wave, 2026-09-06 — a later wave's "not yet deployed" claim was a documentation error, corrected)** | On red-flag chat backend failure, three persistence failures used to stack: audit log, user's own message, AND the reassuring banner shown to her were all lost — the entire safety exchange vanished on next app open. **Fixed, deployed, and verified three separate ways, all on record**: (1) each of the three writes (`flagged_conversations` insert, user message insert, assistant message insert) independently try/caught and logged; the computed reply — including the safety banner — is returned to the client regardless of whether any of the three persistence writes succeeds; a correlated zero-tolerance log line fires if any part of an urgent exchange didn't fully persist. (2) `supabase functions deploy dr-niswah-chat` was run and confirmed via `supabase functions list` showing `version: 8`, matching the exact commit containing this fix (2026-09-05). (3) A live forced-failure test against an isolated, production-equivalent Supabase stack proved all four required checks: reply delivered, failure logged, independent safety-audit write behavior confirmed, no false success claim (2026-09-05) — then an **authenticated production recheck**, once the owner confirmed a synthetic QA account, verified `dr-niswah-chat`'s normal and red-flag/urgent paths directly against production with no regression from this fix and full account-deletion cleanup confirmed (2026-09-05). **Re-confirmed fresh this wave**: an authenticated, read-only `supabase functions list --project-ref jkmjobvxfrmuwafczvtw` was run directly this wave and shows `dr-niswah-chat` still `status: ACTIVE`, still `version: 8` — unchanged since the fix was deployed, confirming nothing has silently regressed or been redeployed over since. The current local source (`supabase/functions/dr-niswah-chat/index.ts`) was also re-read directly and still contains all three independent try/catch blocks. **Correction**: a later wave (`PJ-006`, 2026-09-06) incorrectly stated this fix was "code-complete but not yet deployed" — this was a genuine documentation error (the deployment/production-recheck evidence above already existed on record at the time that claim was written, and was simply not cross-referenced) — corrected here, with fresh evidence rather than merely restoring the old claim | **VERIFIED_CLOSED — deployed to production (function version 8), live-forced-failure-tested, production-recheck-verified, and freshly re-confirmed still live this wave** |
| PJ-005 | Final User Journey | PJ1 High | **BLOCKER — VERIFIED_CLOSED (Final Application Code Blockers wave, 2026-09-06)** | Pinpointed `CQ-007`'s exact live trigger: a per-screen-open session check silently and repeatedly dropped a user into fabricated conversations the moment her session died mid-use, no re-auth prompt ever shown. **Root cause**: `community_board_screen.dart`'s `_messagingRepository` getter and `profile_screen.dart`'s equivalent branch both checked `NiswahSupabase.clientOrNull?.auth.currentUser?.id == null` — a live, per-access read of the current auth session — and branched to `MockPrivateMessagingRepository()` (fabricated conversations with named fake contacts) whenever true, with zero distinction between "never signed in" and "a session that was active a moment ago just died." **Key insight that shaped the fix**: both screens are only reachable from inside `NiswahHomeShell`, which `main.dart`'s root router already gates behind `AuthController.isAuthenticated` — a genuinely unauthenticated user is shown `SignInScreen` before ever reaching either screen. A null user id here therefore can only mean a dying/dead session, never deliberate pre-login browsing — so the mock substitution was never actually serving its stated "explore the demo" purpose for a real user in the shipped app. **Fixed**: both getters now always resolve the real, Supabase-backed repository; the two call sites (`_openPrivateMessages` in both files, `_messagePostAuthor` in `community_board_screen.dart`) now check for a signed-in user *before* navigating, and show an honest, bilingual "Your session has ended. Please sign in again to use messaging." message instead of ever constructing fake content. `private_messaging_locator.dart`'s own separate, legitimate SDK-not-configured fallback (a local-development/config-time concern, not reachable by any real user post-auth-gate) was deliberately left untouched — `MockPrivateMessagingRepository` is still used there and nowhere else, confirmed via `grep`. **Tested**: `test/private_messaging_no_demo_fallback_test.dart` — tapping "Messages" with no session shows the honest prompt, never navigates to a conversations screen, and no fabricated contact name (`Umm Sara`/`Huda`/`Maryam`) appears anywhere | **VERIFIED_CLOSED — the live trigger no longer exists; a dead/absent session now surfaces an honest re-auth prompt, never fabricated content** |
| PJ-006 | Final User Journey | PJ0 Critical | **BLOCKER — VERIFIED_CLOSED (Doctor's Report Data Completeness + Truthfulness wave, 2026-09-06)** | Doctor's Report red-flag section is architecturally incapable of distinguishing "nothing happened" from "every safety record failed to save" — a reviewing doctor cannot know the report is incomplete, because an empty `flagged_conversations` read is structurally indistinguishable from a silently-failed write into that same table. **Root-caused, not just patched at one section**: this wave traced the entire report pipeline (cycle/haid history, pregnancy profile, wellbeing logs, flagged conversations) and found the exact same class of gap present in three of the four sources, not only the one the native finding named — `getCycleLogs()` (the required source) silently falls back to local-only data on a remote failure with no signal to the caller; `getForUser()`/`getLogs()`/`getRecent()` all had their exceptions completely uncaught at the call site, meaning a real fetch failure would have crashed report generation outright rather than being classified at all. **Fixed with a general completeness model**, not a one-off patch: `ReportSourceStatus` (`available`/`empty`/`unavailable`/`failed`) and `ReportCompleteness` (`complete`/`partial`/`insufficient`/`loadFailure`) — every source is now loaded independently, its real outcome captured, and that outcome travels with the report all the way into the rendered PDF export (so a partial report can never lose its own disclosure by being printed/shared out of the app). The red-flag section specifically is now **never silently omitted** — it always renders, with an honest, permanent caveat ("this reflects only conversations successfully recorded... does not rule out a concern that went unsaved") replacing the previous silent gap-when-empty. A required-source (cycle) failure now blocks generation with a retry UI instead of producing a report that would misrepresent cycle/fiqh status; a source with genuinely nothing to report (a brand-new account) is now honestly labeled "insufficient," never "complete." **Executable evidence, not just a warning widget**: 16 new tests directly verify the state originates from real load outcomes, not a cosmetic addition — `test/services/report_completeness_test.dart` (8, the computation logic itself), `test/services/doctor_report_insights_engine_test.dart`'s new group (4, integration through the real `analyze()` entry point), `test/doctor_report_pdf_builder_test.dart` (2 new, the notices actually render), `test/doctor_report_source_loading_test.dart` (2, the new cycle-log-for-report status classification). **Correction (Final User Journey / Upstream Write-Reliability Reconciliation wave, 2026-09-06): the claim below about `PJ-001`/`PJ-004` was wrong and has been corrected at their own rows.** ~~`PJ-001` (the `public.users` FK gap, a genuinely separate, still-`OPEN` database defect) and `PJ-004` (the chat-persistence fix, code-complete but not yet deployed) are unaffected and unresolved by this wave.~~ Both were actually already resolved before this wave was even written — `PJ-001` favorably since the very first Wave 0 remediation session (2026-09-04), `PJ-004` via deployment + live production recheck (2026-09-05) — this wave's claim was written without cross-referencing that existing evidence and has been corrected at their own rows, with fresh, independently-reconfirmed evidence, not merely restored. What genuinely remains true and unaffected: an upstream write into `flagged_conversations` could still, in principle, silently fail for some *other*, not-yet-identified reason (this report-layer fix cannot rule that out, and does not need to). What this wave's fix actually changed is that the report itself no longer silently assumes writes succeeded — it explicitly, permanently discloses this residual limitation instead of being blind to it, which is the exact "truthfulness" bar this finding's own text sets | **VERIFIED_CLOSED — the report can no longer silently represent incomplete/failed source data as complete, proven by 16 passing tests exercising real load-outcome classification, not a cosmetic warning; see `PJ-001`/`PJ-004`'s own rows for their corrected, independently-`VERIFIED_CLOSED` status** |

This does not change `RR-004`'s own closure, since `CQ-007` is a *read-time content-substitution* concern (what a screen shows), not a *write-path reliability* concern (the actual subject of the two-model contract this finding closes on) — but the incorrect supporting claim is removed rather than left standing. **Closed this wave**: the contract is now (1) **explicit** — written up as a standalone product-facing document, `docs/reliability-contract.md`, mapping every active write path to a model with success/failure semantics, retry expectations, idempotency, observability, and user-facing state defined per model; (2) **implemented** — confirmed against current code, not aspirational; (3) **tested** — every claim in the document is backed by a passing automated test (`test/community_repository_idempotency_test.dart`, `test/ai_chat_persistence_resilience_test.dart`, `test/data_export_resilience_test.dart`, `test/account_deletion_resilience_test.dart`, `test/secure_local_store_test.dart`, `test/profile_update_observability_test.dart`, `test/private_messaging_test.dart`). | RR-005 | Reliability | RR2 Medium (newly discovered, 2026-09-05) | **VERIFIED CLOSED (2026-09-05; corrected id, re-confirmed live in code, Dormant Pregnancy Tracking Retirement wave, 2026-09-06)** | `PregnancyProfileRepository` had two of three write call sites (`profile_screen.dart::_startNifas`, `dashboard_screen.dart`'s `onLogBirth`) silently discarding persistence failures the repository's own design treats as meaningful — a failed sync to `pregnancy_profile` left the "طبيبة" chat context stale with no trace and, in `onLogBirth`'s case, an unconditional "success" SnackBar shown even when the sync had failed (a false-success bug). **Fixed same day**: all 3 call sites (`_startNifas`, `onLogBirth`, `_showPregnancySetupSheet`'s `upsert`) now wrap the remote call in try/catch, report via `AppErrorReporter`, keep the local-first toggle authoritative (reverting it would be a worse UX regression than an unpersonalized chat), and show an honest, non-false-success message when the sync fails. **Re-verified directly against current code this wave** (not assumed from the old narrative): all three call sites inspected line-by-line, confirmed still correct and unchanged since the original fix; `test/pregnancy_profile_repository_test.dart`'s three tests (including one explicitly named for `RR-003`, using the id that was live in the codebase's own test-comment at the time — left as historical text in that file, cross-referenced here rather than edited, since renaming a passing test's docstring is out of this wave's scope) still pass | **VERIFIED_CLOSED** |
| RR-006 | Reliability | RR2 Medium (newly discovered, 2026-09-06) | **VERIFIED_CLOSED (Reliability/Resilience Final Closure wave, 2026-09-06)** | `CommunityRepositoryImpl.createPost`/`addComment` used a bare `.insert()` with no client-supplied id — a manual retry after a client-side timeout (the user tapping "post"/"comment" again after seeing an error, with no way to know whether the first attempt actually reached the server) could create a duplicate row. `likePost`/`unlikePost` were already correctly idempotent via a natural-key unique-violation catch, not affected. **Fixed**: `CommunityFeedViewModel`/`PostDetailViewModel` now generate a stable id once (`_pendingPostId`/`_pendingCommentId`) and reuse it across retries of the same logical submit, reset only after a successful submit; the repository now `.upsert()`s on that id instead of blind-inserting — matching the pattern already correctly used by `DreamInterpreterRepositoryImpl`/`_activeEntryId`. **Tested**: `test/community_repository_idempotency_test.dart` (4 new tests) — a fake repository that fails once then succeeds proves a retry reuses the same id, and a second genuinely-new post/comment after a success gets a fresh one. `dart analyze`: clean. `flutter test`: all 4 new tests pass, zero regressions | **VERIFIED_CLOSED** |
| RR-007 | Reliability | RR2 Medium (newly discovered, 2026-09-06) | **VERIFIED_CLOSED (found and fixed, Reliability/Resilience Final Closure wave, 2026-09-06; test-proven, Reliability Evidence Closure wave, 2026-09-06)** | Two related, previously-undocumented gaps in `ChatRepositoryImpl` (Fiqh Advisor / general assistant / dream-interpreter chat threads) and `PrivateMessagingRepository`: (1) `sendMessage`/`createThread` used a bare `.insert()` with no client-supplied id — same duplicate-on-retry class as `RR-006`. (2) `ChatViewModel._showAssistantReplyAndPersist`'s assistant-message persistence catch and its `persistUser` future's `onError` handler both **silently discarded** `chat_messages` persistence failures with zero `AppErrorReporter` call — a message could vanish from persisted chat history forever with no trace anywhere, while the correct part of the design (the live AI reply is never hidden or blocked by a persistence failure — a genuine DUAL/SPLIT AUTHORITY contract, response-delivery vs. durable-history) was unaffected. `ChatDetailViewModel.sendMessage` (private messaging) had the identical silent-`AppErrorReporter`-gap. **Fixed**: both repositories' `sendMessage`/`createThread` gained an optional stable `messageId`/`threadId` parameter and switched to `.upsert()`; `ChatViewModel` generates and reuses a stable id per persist attempt via a new `_persistUserMessage` helper; both view models' previously-silent catches now call `AppErrorReporter.report()`. **`VERIFIED_CLOSED` this wave (Reliability Evidence Closure, 2026-09-06)**: the two production methods responsible for the AI-chat half (`persistUserMessageForTesting`/`showAssistantReplyAndPersistForTesting` — public, `@visibleForTesting`, real production code paths, not test doubles) are now directly exercised by `test/ai_chat_persistence_resilience_test.dart` (7 tests: successful persist reports nothing; a persistence failure is reported via `AppErrorReporter`, not swallowed; each call gets its own stable id; the assistant reply is shown immediately regardless of persistence outcome — the DUAL/SPLIT AUTHORITY contract; a persistence failure doesn't remove the already-shown reply; distinct ids per attempt; the interface-level upsert-id contract). The private-messaging half is now exercised by 3 new tests in `test/private_messaging_test.dart` (failure reported via `AppErrorReporter`; a retry after a failure reuses the same id; a new message after success gets a fresh id). Both halves — idempotency and observability — are fully proven for both affected repositories, not just one | **VERIFIED_CLOSED — both defect classes (duplicate-on-retry, silent persistence swallow) fixed and test-proven for both `ChatRepositoryImpl` and `PrivateMessagingRepository`** |

| RR-009 (**new**, AUTH-004 E4 Retest wave, 2026-09-11; root-caused and remediated, Onboarding Language-Transition wave, 2026-09-12; **owner iOS E4 acceptance PASS, Wave 1 Evidence Reconciliation, 2026-09-12 — `VERIFIED_CLOSED` in full**) | Reliability / UI Rendering | HIGH (§6 — reachable production flow: fresh-account onboarding, language selection → login step) | **`VERIFIED_CLOSED`** (E4 — code-level E2 regression evidence plus a real live owner E4 acceptance journey on iOS, 2026-09-12: "onboarding language → SignInScreen transition rendered correctly," "no white/empty body," "no RenderAnimatedOpacity/infinite-size recurrence," "full onboarding completed through dashboard," "no empty onboarding step encountered" — all owner-reported PASS on commit `72204b28eca759509230318184caf79d8da3c282`) | **Original report (2026-09-11)**: owner saw a near-blank render (progress bar + back chevron only) while attempting the `AUTH-004` E4 retest, described as happening on "Profile," with a log excerpt naming `RenderAnimatedOpacity object was given an infinite size during layout`, ancestor `_RenderSingleChildViewport`. That pass's investigation (Profile Settings flow driven live, twice) could not reproduce it and left the finding open, root cause unproven. **Actual root cause, found and reproduced 2026-09-12**: a second, owner-reported live failure — onboarding's language-selection step, tap Continue, next step renders blank — was traced to the exact same exception signature, this time fully reproduced on demand: `onboarding_screen.dart`'s shared step shell wraps every step in `SingleChildScrollView` → `AnimatedSwitcher` → a custom `FadeTransition`/`SlideTransition`/`ScaleTransition` chain; step 3 is `SignInScreen`, which — until this fix — rendered a full nested `Scaffold` containing a `Stack` with a `PositionedDirectional` child (the language toggle). Neither a `Scaffold` nor a `Stack` with `Positioned` children can be laid out inside `SingleChildScrollView`'s inherently unbounded child slot; the instant the switcher transitions into step 3, Flutter throws exactly the reported cascade (`RenderAnimatedOpacity`, `RenderCustomMultiChildLayoutBox` — confirmed via full stack trace to be `_ScaffoldLayout.performLayout` itself — down through `RenderStack`'s own "requires bounded constraints" assertion). **This retroactively explains the original Profile-attributed report too**: a fresh test account with `onboarding_completed=false` is routed through onboarding from step 1 before ever reaching Profile, so the owner most likely hit this exact onboarding defect and, being unfamiliar with the app's internal step numbering, described it by the destination they expected rather than the screen actually showing — treated as the same root cause (Outcome A: expanded/reclassified, not a new separate finding), not decided on visual-similarity alone but on an identical, independently-reproduced exception signature at the one and only place in the entire codebase with this widget combination. **Remediation**: an initial attempt bounded the shared shell's switched-content height directly (`LayoutBuilder` + `ConstrainedBox`) — this fixed step 3 but was caught, via the new regression tests below, to break scroll-safety for other, legitimately-tall steps (4 and 7 began overflowing at small viewports, since they no longer had the original unbounded `SingleChildScrollView` growing room). **Corrected to the actual minimal, surgical fix**: reverted the shared shell to its original, unmodified form, and instead fixed `SignInScreen` itself — added an `embedded` constructor flag (default `false`, so the standalone top-level usage in `main.dart` is completely unaffected); when `true` (onboarding's step 3 only), the screen returns a plain `Column` (toggle + content, no `Scaffold`, no `Stack`/`Positioned`) — a `Column` has no bounded-height requirement of its own, so it is safe under both a bounded (top-level `Scaffold`) and unbounded (onboarding's scroll view) ancestor alike. **E2 evidence**: 18 new regression tests (`test/onboarding_ui_test.dart`) — both language transitions (English/Arabic), a locale-toggle-before-continuing case, back/forward navigation, small viewport, 200% text scale, semantics enabled, and a full 10-step state-machine sweep asserting every reachable onboarding step renders its real content (never just the shared shell) — all pass; full suite re-confirmed with zero new failures (same 8 known, unchanged golden-image diffs). **Live verification, this pass**: the exact failure was reproduced live on a real Android emulator (fresh synthetic account, real UI taps, language step → Continue → confirmed blank body + full exception cascade captured from the live Flutter log), then, after the fix, the identical live sequence was re-run twice (English and Arabic) on a fresh rebuild and completed cleanly with zero exceptions — real E4-grade evidence for Android. iOS: interactive UI automation remains unavailable in this environment (no `idb`, no System Events access to the Simulator window, documented precisely rather than glossed over) — a clean iOS build was confirmed instead; since this is a pure Dart/Flutter rendering defect with no platform-specific code involved, and the fix was verified via the framework's own real constraint-solving behavior (not emulator-specific), Android's live evidence is treated as representative of iOS too, not as a substitute claimed to be equivalent without qualification. | `lib/features/auth/presentation/screens/sign_in_screen.dart` (`embedded` flag, restructured `build()`); `lib/features/onboarding/presentation/screens/onboarding_screen.dart` (step 3 now passes `embedded: true`; shared shell itself reverted to unmodified); `test/onboarding_ui_test.dart` (18 new tests) | **`VERIFIED_CLOSED`** — root-caused with a full stack trace, fixed at the actual source rather than papered over at the shared shell, regression-tested exhaustively including the state-machine sweep that would have caught the original bug and the viewport/text-scale sweep that caught this fix's own first, incorrect attempt, and now confirmed by a real owner E4 acceptance journey on iOS against production. Canonical closure threshold (proven root cause + remediation + automated regression + Android live verification + owner iOS E4 verification) fully satisfied. |
