One release/staging build emitting a real event (Release Engineering wave, item 8) remains the most rigorous remaining path if the above isn't accepted as sufficient. **Re-attempted, OB-006 Sentry Deployed/Staging Verification wave, 2026-09-08**: native closure bar re-read directly from `OB_remediation_plan.md` R2-1's own retest column — "confirm the new structured log appears in **the chosen tool**" — confirming server-side visibility in Sentry itself is the correct reading, not merely a local SDK-reported success. Code/config re-confirmed unchanged and correct by direct inspection (no defect found, nothing modified). **Remote Sentry access checked directly, confirmed absent**: no `sentry-cli` installed; no Sentry auth token/env var anywhere in this session; no Sentry-related GitHub Actions secret (re-confirmed via API, `total_count: 3`, unrelated). The only Sentry credential present anywhere is the write-only DSN already wired into the app — structurally incapable of querying events back. **Result: `OB006_SENTRY_OWNER_CONFIRMATION_REQUIRED`**, stopped per the charter's own explicit instruction — no remote Sentry access existed in this session to confirm the event independently. **Closed — OB-006 Owner Confirmation wave, 2026-09-09**: the owner opened the Sentry dashboard directly and confirmed the staging verification event is genuinely present server-side, not merely accepted locally by the SDK. Observed evidence, as reported by the owner: Sentry issue `FLUTTER-2`, an intentional Niswah reliability/staging verification event, event ID beginning `10b520e6...` (matching the event id this engagement's own prior wave generated and cited), `environment: staging`, event count `1`. This is exactly the native closure bar's own requirement — the event "appears in the chosen tool" (Sentry), confirmed by the one party capable of confirming it (dashboard access this session never had). **`OB-006` = `VERIFIED_CLOSED`.** No Sentry integration code was touched to reach this closure — the existing, already-correct integration (re-confirmed unchanged in the immediately prior wave) is what produced the event the owner just verified |

| PC-006 | Privacy | PC2 Medium | MEDIUM — **PARTIALLY_REMEDIATED (2026-09-05; live-breaking export bug fixed, Dormant Pregnancy Tracking Retirement wave, 2026-09-06; architecture redesigned for resilience, Reliability/Resilience Final Closure wave, 2026-09-06)** | "Data Export" only produces 4 curated PDF reports, not a full personal-data export. **A real, working raw JSON export now exists** alongside the PDFs — RLS-verified own-data-only. **The underlying architectural pattern that caused the `pregnancy_milestones` live regression (one shared try block around all fetches, so any single table's failure aborted the entire export for every user) was still structurally present for all 8 remaining datasets even after that specific fetch was removed — redesigned this wave, not just patched**: every section (`account`, `profile`, `pregnancy_profile`, `cycle_entries`, `prayer_log`, `community_posts`, `chat_threads`, `chat_messages`) now fetches and fails independently; a transient failure on any one table can no longer hide data the user could otherwise have exported. | PC-010 (**new**) | Privacy | Newly discovered | **HIGH — VERIFIED_CLOSED (Local Sensitive Storage wave, 2026-09-05)** | Cross-user local data leak: cycle/prayer/pregnancy `SharedPreferences` caches used a fixed **global** key with zero per-user scoping — a second person signing in on the same device after the first signed out could read the first person's cached health data. Cycle tracking had zero filtering at any layer (worst case); prayer tracking had only a fragile client-side filter over a still-shared cache; pregnancy tracking had zero filtering. **Fixed**: every local storage key is now scoped by the resolved current-user id internally (`SecureLocalStore.currentUserId()`), so a different signed-in user resolves an entirely different key — no explicit wipe-on-logout required, the leak is structurally impossible. **Validated via a mandatory dedicated test**: User A writes local data → simulated logout → User B signs in → User B reads back empty; User A's own data unaffected. Run for both cycle and prayer tracking. See `00_09` §23 Phase A/G | **VERIFIED_CLOSED** |

| AUTH-002 (**new**, Critical Auth/Signup Lifecycle wave, 2026-09-10; E4-verified, Wave 1 Closure Preparation, 2026-09-10) | Authentication / Onboarding | HIGH (§6, onboarding bypass / incorrect user-state routing) | **`ADVERSARIAL_VERIFIED` (canonical status per `NISWAH_PRELAUNCH_ADVERSARIAL_VALIDATION_MASTER.md`) — root cause confirmed, remediated, E3-verified, now E4-verified on a real device** | Owner acceptance testing found a genuinely new, confirmed email account was treated as an existing/returning user and skipped onboarding. **Root cause, E1 (direct code trace, conclusive)**: `AuthController._isNewSignUp` — an in-memory-only flag — was the sole signal gating onboarding vs. dashboard in `main.dart`'s root router. Critically, `markSignedUp()` is called ONLY on the immediate-session signup branch (`needsConfirmation == false`) — for the real production email/password path, where `mailer_autoconfirm: false` (confirmed live) makes confirmation mandatory, this flag is **never set at all, in any scenario**, not merely lost across a process restart. **Fix (code, no production mutation needed — the column already existed)**: `public.users.onboarding_completed` (already live in production, already correctly defaulted to `false` by the existing `create_user_profile()` signup trigger, but never read or written by any app code before this fix — confirmed via exhaustive `grep`) is now the sole durable, server-side gate. `AuthController` gained a tri-state (`bool?`) `onboardingCompleted`, re-fetched on every genuine sign-in transition, never trusted stale; `main.dart`'s routing rewritten as an explicit 4-state contract (unauthenticated / checking / onboarding-incomplete / onboarding-complete) with a real loading state for "checking" rather than a guess; `AuthRepositoryImpl` gained `fetchOnboardingCompleted()`/`markOnboardingCompleted()`; `OnboardingScreen._completeOnboarding()` now writes the flag server-side (best-effort, with an immediate local optimistic update) as the real, sole completion signal — no other event (session creation, profile-row existence, email confirmation) is treated as completion. The old `isNewSignUp` flag is retained only as a UX hint for which onboarding step to start at, never as an authority over whether onboarding shows at all. **8 new automated widget tests** (`test/auth_onboarding_routing_test.dart`) cover all 5 routing states plus 2 explicit regression tests proving the old flag alone can never resurface as the gate, either direction. **Elevated to E3 this wave**: two real synthetic users against production, using the exact REST query shapes the repository code issues — confirmed the signup trigger sets `onboarding_completed=false`; confirmed a user's own fetch/update of the flag persists correctly; confirmed cross-account isolation (RLS) — a second user can neither read nor write the first user's flag, verified via a direct attack attempt that left the first user's data unchanged. Full scenario records: `production-readiness-results/adversarial-validation/WAVE_1_AUTH_IDENTITY_SESSION_ONBOARDING.md` §2. **E4 update (Wave 1 Closure Preparation, 2026-09-10)**: a real on-device journey was executed against a fresh synthetic account (`w1s03-device-test@niswah-internal-test.invalid`, `onboarding_completed=false` confirmed in production) — signed in through the real Android UI (`adb input tap`/`input text`, not REST), confirmed landing on `OnboardingScreen` (not the dashboard) immediately after authentication; then force-stopped the app and fired the `niswah://login-callback` deep link cold, confirmed via `dumpsys` a fresh process reopened directly to `OnboardingScreen`, not the dashboard. This does not require `AUTH-001` (it uses direct sign-in, not the confirmation-email click-through, which remains separately blocked on `AUTH-001`). Full scenario record: `WAVE_1_AUTH_IDENTITY_SESSION_ONBOARDING.md` `W1-S03`, Status: PASS, Evidence level E4. The confirmation-email click-through specifically remains blocked on `AUTH-001`. |
| AUTH-003 (**new**, Wave 1 Closure Preparation, 2026-09-10) | Onboarding | HIGH (§6, onboarding bypass / incorrect user-state routing class) | **`ROOT_CAUSE_CONFIRMED`** | Prior wave's own follow-up note ("full durable step-level resume flagged as a follow-up") formalized into its own tracked finding, per explicit instruction not to let it disappear from governance. **Traced precisely**: the onboarding step index is pure in-memory `State`, never persisted anywhere; force-close/logout/restart/reinstall/another-device all correctly re-show onboarding from the top (never the exact abandoned step) because `onboarding_completed` is re-checked fresh from the server every time (the `AUTH-002` fix) — **the mandatory safety invariant (incomplete onboarding can never silently become complete; dashboard can never be reached before real completion) holds in every traced case**, verified by the same automated regression tests added for `AUTH-002`. Full reasoning below supersedes the superseded "recommended for owner reconsideration" framing — this session was explicitly instructed not to leave this as only a recommendation, and the underlying evidence (unchanged since 2026-09-14 earlier the same day) genuinely meets the CRITICAL bar for the Fiqh feature specifically: an undisclosed default silently substitutes for a user's actual religious choice and reaches live worship-obligation-determining output. | **`OPEN` — CRITICAL FIQH-FEATURE LAUNCH BLOCKER**, reclassified 2026-09-14 (Fiqh Authority / Knowledge-Base Governance Correction wave). **Formal reassessment, 2026-09-14**: the governing question is "can Niswah safely launch Fiqh guidance while a user's explicitly selected madhhab can disappear and be replaced by an undisclosed Hanbali default?" The answer is **no** — for the Fiqh feature specifically. Reasoning: (1) the four madhahib are confirmed, via the fiqh engine's own golden test dataset (`FIQH-CASE-010`), to produce genuinely different classifications on identical facts — a 30-hour bleeding episode is `needsAdvisory` under Hanafi but `haid` under the other three schools — so a silent madhhab substitution is not a cosmetic label change, it can change whether a woman is told she may pray; (2) this default is confirmed (see the code trace preserved below) to reach both the live deterministic fiqh-calculation path and the Fiqh Advisor AI's context, with zero disclosure to the user at any point; (3) the affected population (any already-onboarded user who reinstalls or changes devices) is a real, ordinary usage pattern, not a contrived edge case. **This is distinct from a whole-app blocker**: Niswah's non-fiqh functionality (cycle logging, pregnancy tracking, community, wellbeing) is entirely unaffected by this gap and works correctly regardless — the CRITICAL classification applies specifically and only to launching the Fiqh-guidance-bearing surfaces (dashboard fiqh-status card, Fiqh Report, Fiqh Advisor AI chat) with this gap unresolved. See the Fiqh Feature Blocker List in `00_11_REQUIREMENTS_LEDGER.md`'s governance-correction section for the full two-layer blocker model. Original governance-correction analysis (2026-09-14, earlier the same day) preserved verbatim below as the evidentiary basis for this reclassification: **Governance correction, 2026-09-14**: the Full Implementation Reconciliation pass (2026-09-14) independently rediscovered this exact gap and drafted it as a new ledger entry, `REQ-FIQH-002`, without cross-referencing this already-open finding — a real duplication error, corrected here. **`REQ-FIQH-002` maps directly and entirely to `AUTH-005`; they are the same requirement, not separate ones.** That same pass also traced the silent-default mechanism precisely: `MadhhabController.load()` falls back to `Madhhab.hanbali` whenever no value is stored, with no user-facing notice. In the *normal* onboarding flow this is harmless (the Madhhab step gates progression — `Continue` stays disabled until a real choice is made, so no genuine user ever reaches the dashboard/fiqh-calculation/AI-context code paths on the silent default). The confirmed-live-risk scenario is narrower and specific: an **already fully-onboarded** user (`onboarding_completed = true`, so the root router sends her straight to the dashboard, never back through the Madhhab step) whose local `SharedPreferences` is lost (reinstall, new device, or any local-storage clear) silently reverts to Hanbali with no warning, and — confirmed via this pass's own code trace — that value *does* flow into the live haid-duration/fiqh-calculation path (`MadhhabRuleEvaluator`) and the Fiqh Advisor's `UserAiContext`. This is the exact mechanism this finding's own original summary already described ("changing which fiqh haid-duration thresholds and rulings she is shown") — this pass adds no new mechanism, only a sharper, independently-re-derived confirmation that it is real and reachable, not merely theoretical. | Selected madhhab is `LOCAL_ONLY_UNSAFE`: the value actually read by the Fiqh engine, dashboard, and (via `UserAiContext`) the Fiqh Advisor comes exclusively from `MadhhabController` (`SharedPreferences`, device-local) — confirmed via exhaustive `grep`, no server read exists anywhere in the live consumption path. Two server-side columns already exist, live, unused — `profiles.selected_madhhab` (default `'shafii'`, written only by the orphaned, unreachable `SettingsScreen`) and `users.madhhab` (default `'HANBALL'`, set once by the signup trigger and never read/written again) — but neither is wired to the actual runtime read path, so this is functionally a **NO** on "does the server already persist this," despite the columns' existence. A reinstall or new device silently reverts a user to the hardcoded default madhhab with no warning, changing which fiqh haid-duration thresholds and rulings she is shown. Minimal correct design prepared (Wave 1 Final Blocker Remediation, 2026-09-11): designate `users.madhhab` as the single canonical field; `MadhhabController` becomes `LOCAL_CACHE_OF_SERVER` (server wins on sign-in, write-through on explicit user change); reconcile the two live tables' disagreeing defaults/casing as part of that same future work. **Not remediated** — implementing this is a real feature addition (touches sign-in flow, `MadhhabController`, and indirectly the Fiqh engine's consumption path), explicitly out of `AUTH-004`'s scope (`AUTH-004` concerned the broken `anonymous_mode`/`display_name` write path, not madhhab), and is not being classified as resolved merely because it was out of scope for that fix. | `lib/core/preferences/madhhab_controller.dart`; `lib/core/models/user_profile.dart`/`lib/core/services/user_profile_repository.dart` (writes `profiles.selected_madhhab`, unreachable path); live `public.users.madhhab`/`public.profiles.selected_madhhab` (both confirmed via canonical baseline + live schema, unused by the real runtime read path) | **`REMEDIATED — E3_SERVER_VERIFIED, pending owner E4 retest`** (Fiqh Remediation Wave 1, 2026-09-14). A canonical, durable, three-state Madhhab authority model (UNSET/UNKNOWN/SELECTED — never collapsed) is now implemented: `public.users.madhhab`/`madhhab_selection_state` (new columns, migration `20260914120000_madhhab_authority_state.sql` + a same-day follow-up `20260914120100_...` that dropped a conflicting, previously-undocumented legacy uppercase-value constraint found live in production during verification), with `MadhhabController` rewritten as `LOCAL_CACHE_OF_SERVER` (server-authoritative, local cache only for offline/startup). Every silent-Hanbali-default location found this pass was removed, including the single most authoritative one — the `create_user_profile()` signup trigger, which had hardcoded `'HANBALI'` into every new user's row since the column existed; it now inserts `NULL`/`'unset'`. Every existing user's row was migrated to UNSET, not inferred as Hanbali (per explicit governance instruction). `MadhhabRuleEvaluator`/`CycleStatusEngine`/`FiqhReportInsightsEngine` now return an explicit `FiqhCycleState.madhhabUnresolved` (never a guessed ruling) when no madhhab is SELECTED; the AI context layer (`ai_user_context.ts`, both `fiqh-advisor-chat` and `ai-assistant-chat`) now carries an explicit `madhhab_state` distinguishing unknown/unset/selected, and `fiqh-advisor-chat` refuses gracefully (never a fabricated-madhhab answer) when state is not `selected`. **Live-verified this pass**: schema/constraint changes confirmed via direct Management API read-back; a real lowercase write (`madhhab='hanafi', madhhab_selection_state='selected'`) proven to succeed against the live table inside a rolled-back transaction (no real data touched). This is `E3` (server persistence + constraints + calculation/context integration proven) — **not yet `E4`**, per this finding's own closure standard, which explicitly requires owner real-device evidence that an explicit selection survives a genuine reinstall/account-switch journey and that the unknown option behaves correctly on a real device. See the owner acceptance script in `docs/final-owner-launch-checklist.md`'s new Fiqh Remediation Wave 1 handoff. **Not a whole-app blocker** — scoped to the Fiqh-guidance surfaces only, unchanged. **Update, Fiqh Remediation Wave 1 — Pre-E4 Verification Completion, 2026-09-14 (later still) — every engineering-verifiable gap ahead of the owner's E4 retest is now closed.** A real two-account (and separately, a real UNKNOWN-vs-SELECTED account) live isolation test was run directly against production using 4 synthetic, disposable Supabase accounts (created via the Admin API, deleted immediately afterward, zero trace remaining, confirmed via a post-cleanup row count of 0): each account's real JWT, obtained via a genuine password sign-in, could read only its own `madhhab`/`madhhab_selection_state` row and never another account's, including across a full "A → B → A again" sequence proving no cross-session leak or mutation. The full 6-case constraint matrix (selected+valid/accepted, selected+NULL/rejected, unknown+NULL/accepted, unset+NULL/accepted, unknown+Madhhab/rejected, unset+Madhhab/rejected) was proven live via rolled-back transactions — all 6 behaved exactly as designed. The signup trigger was re-confirmed unchanged and correct. Two real accessibility defects were found and fixed during this pass (tracked under `AUTH-010`, not a new finding): neither the onboarding Madhhab grid nor the Settings Madhhab grid exposed a `selected` semantics flag (screen-reader users could not hear which school was currently chosen), and the Settings grid overflowed at 200% text scale (a real, previously-untested `RenderFlex` overflow) — both fixed and retested, 17/17 semantics tests and 17/17 text-scale/viewport tests now passing. The dead `MadhhabType.fromValue`/Shafi'i-fallback code path was exhaustively re-confirmed unreachable from any live code path (Fiqh engine, AI context, or persistence) and correctly left untouched, per explicit instruction not to expand scope. **Existing-user migration rationale (permanent record, per explicit instruction not to reinterpret these rows later without new evidence)**: all 25 pre-existing production users were migrated to `UNSET` rather than any specific madhhab, including Hanbali, because the prior architecture's only value for this field (`'HANBALI'`, written unconditionally by `create_user_profile()` for every account) was never the product of an explicit user choice — it was an automatic default assigned identically to every signup regardless of that user's actual beliefs. Treating that default as if it were a real, explicit selection would mean inferring and persisting a specific religious classification for a real person with zero evidence she ever made that choice — exactly the harm this whole remediation wave exists to prevent. This determination should stand unless and until new evidence specifically establishes that a given user's pre-migration value reflected a real, explicit choice (e.g. a corroborating client-side record from that user's own device) — it must not be silently revisited or reversed by a future wave absent such evidence. Full detail: see `00_09_PHASE1_ROOT_CAUSE_REMEDIATION_PLAN.md` §79. |

Classified this pass (Wave 1 Final Blocker Remediation, 2026-09-11) as **(B) account state that should survive reinstall/device changes**, not a device preference — a woman's prayer location is tied to her identity/location, not the specific phone she is using at a given moment. live `public.users.prayer_city`/`prayer_country`/`prayer_lat`/`prayer_lon`/`prayer_city_ar`/`prayer_country_ar` (confirmed via canonical baseline + live schema, unused by any app code) | **`OPEN`** — real, live, currently-unresolved gap; tracked explicitly so it is never mistaken for resolved by `AUTH-004`'s adjacent fix. **Reassessment, Live Onboarding Contradiction Investigation wave, 2026-09-15** (triggered by the owner's live report that the "Use Current Location" control appeared to do nothing): `lib/core/preferences/prayer_location_controller.dart` was re-read in full and re-confirmed byte-identical between `main` and `terminal` (`git diff main terminal -- lib/core/preferences/prayer_location_controller.dart` — zero lines of output), so this finding's persistence gap predates and is independent of this wave's separately-discovered `main`/`terminal` branch divergence (see `AUTH-010`'s reopening note). `PrayerLocationController` remains a `SharedPreferences`-only singleton with no server read/write path — confirmed again this pass by direct source read, not inferred from UI behavior alone, satisfying the explicit instruction not to close this finding from UI tests. **This finding is NOT the same defect as the owner's "Current Location does nothing" report** — that report's actual, distinct root cause (the onboarding screen advancing to the next step immediately on success with zero visible confirmation, making a fully-working detection+persistence call look identical to a dead tap) is tracked separately as `AUTH-011`, now fixed; `AUTH-006`'s gap (no server-side survival of a reinstall/new device) is unrelated to that UI symptom and remains genuinely unresolved. Status remains `OPEN` — this pass added confirming evidence and a formal separation from `AUTH-011`, not a remediation. |
| AUTH-007 (**new**, Wave 1 Final Auth/Localization Closure wave, 2026-09-13; **owner fresh-install iOS E4 retest PASS, Post-Reconciliation Governance Correction wave, 2026-09-14**) | Authentication / Onboarding | HIGH (§6 — real live E4 defect affecting the app's own default-Arabic user base across the entire post-language onboarding journey and the embedded auth screen) | **`VERIFIED_CLOSED` / `E4`** — owner ran a real fresh-install iOS retest of the exact prescribed script; "Arabic onboarding remained Arabic" reported PASS, directly satisfying this finding's own closure criterion. Root-caused, fixed, and regression-tested; now also live-device-proven. | Owner E4 evidence: selected Arabic during onboarding, but the Madhhab step displayed in English; separately, the Sign In / Sign Up screen appeared "flipped/mirrored" under Arabic RTL. Investigation proved both symptoms share **one root cause**: `_OnboardingScreenState` (`onboarding_screen.dart`) kept its own local `bool _arabic` field, hardcoded to `false` at construction, completely disconnected from `AppLocaleController.instance.isArabic` — the real, `SharedPreferences`-persisted, app-wide source of truth already used by the language-selection step itself and by `SignInScreen`. Unlike its sibling field `_isMarried` (deliberately restored from `MaritalStatusController` in `initState()` for exactly this "returning user re-routed through onboarding" scenario, per the code's own comment), `_arabic` was never restored the same way. Any time a fresh `OnboardingScreen`/`_OnboardingScreenState` was created after Arabic had already been selected and persisted in an earlier attempt (the realistic trigger: the app process being recreated while the user leaves to confirm their email, then returns and is re-routed through onboarding) — every step driven by that local field (Madhhab, Married, Location, Period, Privacy, Welcome) silently fell back to English. The embedded `SignInScreen` at step 3 showed the SECOND symptom of the identical bug: its own text is always correct (it reads `AppLocaleController` directly), but its ambient `Directionality` is inherited from the onboarding shell's wrapper — which used the same stale field — so genuinely-Arabic text rendered inside an LTR-mirrored layout, exactly matching "flipped/mirrored." A second, independent, minor defect was found during the same investigation: the onboarding back-navigation icon (`Icons.chevron_left_rounded`) is a non-directional glyph that does not mirror for RTL even when `Directionality` is correct (its `PositionedDirectional` placement was already correct — only the glyph itself was fixed-left). Tab-order/tap-semantics of the Sign In / Sign Up toggle itself (item I in the charter) were audited and found already correct — `_modeTab`'s displayed label, active-highlight, and tap handler are all driven by the same `signUp` parameter per call, so no mismatch between visible label and resulting mode is possible; Flutter's own `Row` RTL auto-reversal already places the tabs in a semantically correct reading-order in both directions (charter's own "use semantic RTL behavior, do not blindly mirror" was already satisfied there — locked in with new test coverage, not changed). **Fix**: `_arabic` converted from a stored field to a getter reading `AppLocaleController.instance.isArabic` directly (single source of truth, no possible desync regardless of how many times the State is recreated); the language-selection callback simplified to just call the controller and `setState(() {})`; the back-chevron glyph now swaps between `chevron_left_rounded`/`chevron_right_rounded` based on the same source. **Regression coverage added**: 13 new tests in `onboarding_ui_test.dart` (`AUTH-007` group) construct a fresh `OnboardingScreen` at every one of its 10 steps with `AppLocaleController` already set to Arabic *before* the widget is built — exactly reproducing the real trigger — and assert Arabic content with no English fallback at every step, explicit Arabic madhhab-name coverage, correct chevron-glyph direction, and correct embedded-step-3 RTL `Directionality`; none of these are vacuous — the equivalent assertions demonstrably fail against the pre-fix code (the existing state-machine test never caught this because it always reset `AppLocaleController` to English before construction). 7 new tests in `sign_in_rtl_test.dart` cover standalone + embedded Sign In/Sign Up RTL/LTR correctness, tab-tap semantics, small viewport, 200% text scale, and semantics-tree validity. Full regression suite clean. | `lib/features/onboarding/presentation/screens/onboarding_screen.dart` (`_arabic` getter, language-select callback, back-chevron icon); `lib/features/auth/presentation/screens/sign_in_screen.dart` (added `Key`s to `_modeTab` for reliable, unambiguous test targeting — no behavior change); `test/onboarding_ui_test.dart` (`AUTH-007` group, 13 tests); `test/sign_in_rtl_test.dart` (new file, 7 tests) | **`VERIFIED_CLOSED` / `E4`** — root cause proven with code-level evidence, minimal fix applied, comprehensive automated regression coverage passing, and now confirmed on a real fresh-install iOS device by the owner running the exact prescribed retest script. Closed. |
| AUTH-008 (**new**, Wave 1 Final State-Machine Closure wave, 2026-09-13; **owner E4 retest FAILED, REOPENED, Wave 1 Owner E4 Retest Failure wave, 2026-09-13, later the same day**; **owner fresh-install iOS E4 retest PASS, Post-Reconciliation Governance Correction wave, 2026-09-14**) | Authentication / Onboarding | HIGH (§6 — downgraded from CRITICAL/LAUNCH BLOCKER now that a fresh-install owner E4 retest confirms the fix; the reopening episode itself is preserved as historical evidence of why this was ever a launch blocker) | **`VERIFIED_CLOSED` / `E4`** — the owner ran a real fresh-install iOS retest of the exact prescribed script and reported PASS on every item this finding covers: no duplicate Sign In/Sign Up during onboarding, Sign Out correctly returns to Sign In, subsequent Sign In goes directly to the dashboard. This directly confirms the reopening episode's own leading hypothesis — the prior FAIL was very likely a stale (pre-fix) build on the owner's device, not a surviving code defect — without needing to assert that as fact; the fresh-install retest settles it either way. The prior `E4_FAIL`/`REOPENED` history below is preserved verbatim as historical evidence, per this engagement's standing rule never to erase a prior finding's evidence trail. Re-investigation this pass (exhaustive re-audit of every `SignInScreen`/`OnboardingScreen` construction site in `lib/`, plus new root-router-level tests reproducing the owner's exact real sequence — pre-auth language selection, then a simulated real sign-in) found **no code path in the current source matching the reported "language again → Sign In again" transition**; the current code's only two `SignInScreen` construction sites remain the root router and the delete-account flow, and the new tests pass showing zero `SignInScreen`/language-screen reappearance across that exact sequence. This is consistent with — but does not prove on its own — the owner having retested a build compiled before the AUTH-008 fix landed (the same stale-build risk `DC-010` already documents elsewhere in this engagement). A live device re-verification of the current build was attempted this pass and could not be completed: the Android emulator entered a persistent "System UI isn't responding" state that reproduced even on a from-scratch, fully wiped AVD before the app was ever installed — proving it is a host/infrastructure condition, not an app or code issue — after four escalating, genuine remediation attempts (clean restart, process kill+relaunch, direct SystemUI force-stop, full data wipe). Per explicit instruction, this is not claimed as live verification and does not by itself close `AUTH-008`. A genuinely new, distinct defect was found and fixed in the same pass — see `AUTH-009` — which the owner's report also correctly identified (the redundant Language step) and which was real and current regardless of the stale-build question. | Owner E4 evidence: a real production journey (signup → email confirmed → returned → signed in → language → Madhhab) was followed by Niswah presenting Sign In/Sign Up again. **Proven root cause** (via a deterministic, reproducible widget test, not inference): onboarding step 3 was a full embedded `SignInScreen` (`onAuthenticated: _next, embedded: true`) — present since before this repository's own tracked history (confirmed via `git log --follow`, already in the very first commit). `main.dart`'s root router (`_buildHome`, introduced by `AUTH-002`, commit `409ef5d`) already requires `auth.isAuthenticated == true` before `OnboardingScreen` is ever constructed at all — meaning a login step *inside* onboarding was redundant for every real path from that commit onward, but was never removed when that routing contract was introduced; `RR-009` (commit `dc55a95`) later fixed a rendering crash *inside* that same already-redundant step without questioning its necessity. Both **forward navigation** (any fresh `OnboardingScreen` instance starting at `initialStep: 1`, which is the default for every case except an in-process signup) and **back navigation** (tapping Back from Madhhab, step 4, decremented `_step` straight onto step 3) passed an already-authenticated user through a live, interactive Sign In/Sign Up screen — reproduced directly in a widget test before any fix was applied. **A second, independent, more severe instance of the same anti-pattern was found during this investigation**: `ProfileScreen._signOut()` manually pushed `OnboardingScreen()` (via `Navigator.push`, bypassing the root router entirely) as an ad hoc way to let a signed-out user log back in through onboarding's own embedded step 3 — exactly the "hard-code a login step to cover session loss" pattern the canonical methodology doc explicitly prohibits (root auth guard must own this, never an embedded step). Removing step 3 without also fixing this call site would have left sign-out completely broken (no way to re-authenticate at all, landing on Madhhab with no session). **Fix**: onboarding step 3 (embedded `SignInScreen`) removed entirely; steps renumbered (10 → 9 steps: 1 Splash → 2 Language → 3 Madhhab → 4 Married → 5 Location → 6 Last Period → 7 Period Length → 8 Privacy → 9 Welcome); `main.dart`'s `initialStep: auth.isNewSignUp ? 4 : 1` → `? 3 : 1` (and the debug-preview flag likewise); `ProfileScreen._signOut()`'s manual `Navigator.push(OnboardingScreen(...))` removed entirely, now relying on the already-correct, already-tested reactive root router (`AuthController`'s `onAuthStateChange` listener already flips `isAuthenticated` false on a real sign-out, which `_buildHome` already reacts to on its own). A genuinely new, unrelated defect was also discovered and fixed while extending 200%-text-scale coverage to the (now more prominently reached) Madhhab step: its `_SelectCard` choice-grid cells (fixed `childAspectRatio`) overflowed at large OS text-scale settings — fixed with the same `FittedBox(fit: BoxFit.scaleDown)` treatment already established for this exact class of bug by `AU-006`. **Regression coverage**: `auth_onboarding_routing_test.dart` gained 16 new tests exercising the root router directly (`AuthController.setStateForTest`) for all 4 required app-restart scenarios, both logout/login journeys, and both returning-user journeys — each asserting `find.byType(SignInScreen), findsNothing` at every authenticated state. `onboarding_ui_test.dart`'s state-machine sweep and `AUTH-007` sweep both renumbered and extended with an explicit `SignInScreen` absence assertion per step, plus a new full forward-and-backward walk asserting strictly-increasing step order (no cycle) and zero `SignInScreen` occurrences throughout. The obsolete `RR-009`-titled transition suite (which tested entering the now-removed step 3) was replaced with an equivalent-coverage `language -> Madhhab` transition suite (small viewport, 200% scale, semantics, back navigation) rather than deleted outright. `sign_in_rtl_test.dart`'s now-impossible "embedded step 3" test was removed; its standalone-screen coverage (the only way this screen is shown at all now) is unchanged and still passing. A pre-existing documentation inconsistency (a stale "AUTH-008" reference inside `sign_in_rtl_test.dart` left over from the prior wave, which actually belongs entirely to `AUTH-007`) was also corrected. Full regression: 451 tests, 443 passing, same 8 known pre-existing golden-image diffs, zero new failures. | `lib/main.dart` (`_buildHome`'s `initialStep` branches); `lib/features/onboarding/presentation/screens/onboarding_screen.dart` (step switch, `_totalSteps`, `_SelectCard`'s `FittedBox` fix, doc comments); `lib/features/auth/presentation/screens/profile_screen.dart` (`_signOut()` simplified, unused import removed); `test/auth_onboarding_routing_test.dart` (16 new tests); `test/onboarding_ui_test.dart` (renumbered + extended); `test/sign_in_rtl_test.dart` (obsolete test removed, header corrected) | **`VERIFIED_CLOSED` / `E4`** — a real fresh-install iOS owner retest, exactly the closure condition the reopening episode itself specified ("either a successful owner retest on a confirmed-fresh install, or successful live device verification in a working environment"), reported PASS on every item. Closed. | AUTH-009 (**new**, Wave 1 Owner E4 Retest Failure wave, 2026-09-13; **owner fresh-install iOS E4 retest PASS, Post-Reconciliation Governance Correction wave, 2026-09-14**) | Authentication / Onboarding | HIGH (§6 — a real, reproducible, unnecessary duplicate screen shown to every authenticated user on the primary signup journey; not launch-blocking on its own the way a circular-auth defect is, but a genuine UX regression the owner directly experienced) | **`VERIFIED_CLOSED` / `E4`** — the owner's fresh-install iOS retest reported both "language selected pre-auth: PASS" and "no duplicate Language screen after login: PASS," directly confirming this finding's own closure criterion on a real device. Owner E4 evidence: having already selected a language via the pre-auth toggle on the standalone `SignInScreen` (which sets `AppLocaleController` directly — the app's single, real language authority, already relied on everywhere including `AUTH-007`'s fix), the owner signed in and was shown onboarding's own Language-selection step again — a genuine, reproducible duplication, not a misreading of `AUTH-008`. **Root cause**: onboarding's own step 2 was a full `_Language` selection screen, structurally identical in purpose to the pre-auth toggle but with no awareness that a choice (explicit or default) already existed. Unlike `AUTH-007`'s locale-desync bug, this was not a state-sync defect — the language VALUE was always correct; the screen asking for it a second time was simply redundant. **Fix**: onboarding's step 2 (`_Language`, now fully unused and removed as dead code) deleted from the step switch entirely; remaining steps renumbered (Madhhab now step 2, the first real content step). `main.dart`'s two `initialStep` computations updated (`auth.isNewSignUp ? 2 : 1`; debug-preview flag likewise). The back-button visibility condition (`_step > 2`) already correctly excludes the new step 2 (Madhhab) without any additional code change — Madhhab, now the very first onboarding content step, has no Back button at all, the safest possible choice per the canonical methodology's explicit "do not accidentally expose auth" requirement (there is nothing before it to go back to, so no path can ever reach auth from within onboarding via Back). **Regression coverage**: `onboarding_ui_test.dart` fully renumbered (8 steps) with new tests asserting the splash → Madhhab transition shows no language screen, in both Arabic and English, across small viewport/200% text scale/semantics, plus a dedicated test confirming Madhhab has no Back button. `auth_onboarding_routing_test.dart` gained 3 new root-router-level "real journey" tests setting `AppLocaleController` to Arabic/English *before* simulating the auth transition (the exact scenario neither file previously covered, since existing tests always reset locale to English in `setUp` and never touched `AuthController` from within `onboarding_ui_test.dart`) — proving pre-auth language selection survives sign-in with zero `SignInScreen` or language-screen reappearance in either language, and that a killed-and-reopened app with a restored session preserves the pre-auth Arabic choice. It is not. A woman who does not actually know her madhhab, forced to pick one of 4 to proceed, may pick incorrectly; the app then produces confident, deterministic fiqh classification output (and AI guidance) under a school she does not actually follow, with no signal to her or the app that the underlying selection is a guess rather than a fact. `FIQH-CASE-010` (the golden dataset's own madhhab-disagreement case) confirms this is not cosmetic: identical facts genuinely classify differently across the 4 schools, meaning a wrong guess can produce a wrong haid/tahara determination, not just a differently-worded one. **Distinguished from `AUTH-005`**: `AUTH-005` is the system silently choosing with zero user input or disclosure; `AUTH-010` involves an explicit (if uninformed) user choice, which is why it is classified HIGH rather than `AUTH-005`'s CRITICAL — some degree of user agency exists, even though the agency is exercised without adequate information. **Not a whole-app blocker** — same reasoning as `AUTH-005`, scoped to the Fiqh-guidance-bearing surfaces only. | **`REMEDIATED` — `E2_AUTOMATED_VERIFIED`**, reverted from the 2026-09-15 `REOPENED`/`E4_FAIL` status by the Release Branch Reconciliation wave (2026-09-15, same day) once the owner's failing E4 retest was proven — not merely hypothesized — to have been collected against a stale `main`-derived build lacking this fix entirely, rather than against the actual current implementation; see the governance-correction note appended at the end of this row for the full evidentiary basis. Owner E4 retest still required for closure, now against a build proven to originate from the reconciled release branch. (The underlying HIGH FIQH-FEATURE LAUNCH BLOCKER classification from the 2026-09-14 reassessment below is unaffected — that classification concerns severity if unresolved, not evidence-tier status.) | The Full Implementation Reconciliation pass (2026-09-14) confirmed the owner's own reported example precisely: `FIQH_ENGINE_ACCURACY_AUDIT_MASTER.md`'s onboarding spec explicitly requires a 5th choice — "I don't know my madhhab" — alongside Hanafi/Maliki/Shafi'i/Hanbali, with a hedged, geography-based suggestion path (never a declaration) and mandatory explicit confirmation before any suggestion becomes authoritative. The shipped onboarding Madhhab step (`onboarding_screen.dart`) offers only the 4 fixed choices, and `Continue` is disabled until one is picked — no escape hatch exists, unlike the Last-Period step's real "I'm not sure" pattern in the same file. Item B (the silent default-to-Hanbali behavior) is **not** a separate root cause — it is the exact mechanism `AUTH-005` already tracks, and is documented there, not duplicated here. Remains `E2`, still pending the owner's real-device E4 retest per this finding's own standing evidence discipline. **Reopening note, Live Onboarding Contradiction Investigation wave, 2026-09-15**: the owner's first real-device retest reported a live screenshot showing only the original 4 Madhhab choices, with no 5th "I don't know my Madhhab" option — a direct E4 FAIL, treated per explicit instruction as authoritative over the prior E2 automated evidence (the E2 result is preserved above verbatim as historical evidence only, not current truth). This was investigated end-to-end rather than dismissed as a stale build: `git show <Fiqh Remediation Wave 1 commit>:lib/features/onboarding/presentation/screens/onboarding_screen.dart` on the `terminal` branch's HEAD confirms the 5-choice grid and the full UNKNOWN sub-flow are genuinely present in source, and the full `onboarding_ui_test.dart` Madhhab suite (including the "all 5 choices are visible" tests, EN+AR) was re-run against that exact HEAD and passes. No other reachable Madhhab-selection UI exists anywhere in the codebase (`settings_screen.dart`'s legacy 4-option dropdown was independently re-confirmed unreachable — zero construction sites outside its own declaration). The root cause is external to the application code: `git merge-base main terminal` and `git log --oneline main..terminal` show `main` (GitHub's own default branch, per `gh repo view --json defaultBranchRef`) has been frozen at commit `ebd201256f7b85741457bd44be1df8f26804b7fa` since before this entire engagement began, 97 commits behind `terminal`, with zero pull requests ever opened (`gh pr list --state all` empty) and zero of those 97 commits merged by any means. `main`'s own copy of `onboarding_screen.dart` (read directly via `git show main:...`) contains only the original 4-choice grid — byte-for-byte the same defect shape the owner's screenshot shows. This means whatever build/install the owner tested was built from `main` (or an equivalently stale source), not from `terminal` — the fix has never reached that pipeline, for any finding in this entire engagement, not only this one. This is a deployment/branch-management gap, not a regression in the remediation itself. **AUTH-010 is correctly classified `REOPENED`/`E4_FAIL` per the owner's authoritative live evidence** — the fix's non-arrival on a real device is exactly what E4 failing means, regardless of cause — but the engineering root cause is now conclusively identified and requires an owner decision (merge/deploy `terminal`, not a further code fix) before any real E4 retest can be meaningful. See `00_09_PHASE1_ROOT_CAUSE_REMEDIATION_PLAN.md`'s Live Onboarding Contradiction Investigation wave section for the full evidence trail. **Governance correction, Release Branch Reconciliation wave, 2026-09-15 (same day)**: the owner's own 4-option screenshot is reclassified **`STALE_BUILD_EVIDENCE` / `INVALID_FOR_CURRENT_RELEASE`** — not because the owner's report was wrong (it accurately showed what her tested build rendered) but because that build is now proven, not merely suspected, to have been sourced from `main` at a commit that predates every Fiqh Remediation Wave 1 change, rather than from `terminal`'s current HEAD. This is a stronger evidentiary basis than a "probably stale build" hypothesis: direct content comparison (`git show main:lib/features/onboarding/presentation/screens/onboarding_screen.dart` at the time) showed the exact same 4-choice-only `_madhhabChoices` list the screenshot rendered, and `git merge-base --is-ancestor` confirmed no Fiqh Remediation Wave 1 commit was ever an ancestor of `main` at that time. Per this instruction: **the historical `REOPENED`/`E4_FAIL` entry immediately above is preserved verbatim, not erased** — it remains the accurate record of what happened and why it was the correct call with the evidence available at that moment (owner live evidence over automated evidence is the right general principle; it was the *premise* that the tested build reflected current `terminal` that has since been disproven, not that principle itself). Status reverts to **`REMEDIATED` — `E2_AUTOMATED_VERIFIED`**, its technically-justified pre-owner-test tier, pending a new E4 retest — this time against a build whose commit SHA is confirmed contained in the reconciled release branch (`main`, once PR #1 merges), per the new release-branch policy recorded in `00_09`'s §82. **Assistance-flow robustness completed, Madhhab Resolution Gate wave, 2026-09-16**: the owner tested the "I don't know my Madhhab" flow this remediation shipped and found the assistance itself genuinely too weak — a single free-text residence-country question that frequently returned "لا يتوفر اقتراح لهذه المنطقة بعد" with no further path forward once that happened. This is the completion of this same finding's original assistance-quality gap, not a new, unrelated defect — reconciled here rather than opened as a duplicate finding, per explicit instruction. Delivered: (1) a single canonical entry gate — every "Log my Haidh" action in the app (6 call sites, all already funneling through one function, `showCycleLogSheet`) now checks the real Madhhab state first, opening a full guided resolution flow before real blood/flow logging (not the separate, non-Fiqh symptoms-only logging path) whenever state is UNSET or UNKNOWN, and continuing straight through when already SELECTED; (2) a real signal hierarchy replacing the old single-question, single-country model — a "where did you learn most of your religious practice?" question outranks a "what does your family mostly follow?" question, both backed by a searchable, full-world (~195-country) picker (ISO-coded internally, never matched on translated display strings) rather than free text or a small hard-coded list, with current residence never consulted at all (the charter's own weakest-signal ranking, satisfied trivially by omission rather than by a weighting formula that could be gamed or misjudged); (3) the underlying country→madhhab evidence dataset was **not** expanded or invented from model knowledge, per explicit instruction — the existing, already-reviewed-status-disclosed 10-country/1-region seed mapping (`MadhhabSuggestionService`) is reused as-is, now carrying its full source metadata (`mapping_id`, `evidence_source_ids`, `reviewer_status`, a dataset version) instead of a bare country→madhhab list; any country outside that seed set honestly reports insufficient evidence, never a guessed suggestion; (4) explicit confirmation remains the sole authority for a SELECTED state — a suggestion, single or multiple candidates, never mutates state on its own, matching this finding's own original governing principle; "still not sure" persists UNKNOWN, never a default. New reusable component: `lib/features/madhhab_resolution/presentation/madhhab_resolution_screen.dart`, wired into both the Log-my-Haidh gate and Settings (which now also surfaces "Not yet determined" alongside a "Help me choose" action reusing the identical resolver, rather than a second, separate decision path) — onboarding's own existing assistance step was deliberately left untouched this wave, out of scope per explicit instruction. 19 new automated tests (`test/madhhab_resolution_test.dart`) cover the gate (SELECTED/UNSET/UNKNOWN, symptoms-only never gated), direct selection, guided selection (single/multiple/insufficient-evidence candidates, the upbringing-outranks-family hierarchy, the Canada/unsupported-country cases proving residence and unreviewed geography never assign a school), "still not sure," Settings' shared resolver, account-switch isolation, Arabic/English, 200% text scale, and the searchable picker's filtering — all passing, alongside a full project regression (505/524 passing pre-existing-baseline-identical, zero new failures) that also caught and fixed one real design defect before it ever reached a device: a `PopScope(canPop: false)` gate, added to intercept hardware-back for internal step navigation, was found to also silently block this same screen's own intentional `Navigator.pop(true)` confirmation calls — fixed by moving internal back-navigation to the AppBar's own back button, entirely separate from the Navigator's pop machinery. |
| AUTH-011 (**new**, Live Onboarding Contradiction Investigation wave, 2026-09-15; **remediated in code, same wave — `E2_AUTOMATED_VERIFIED`, owner E4 retest required for closure**) | Onboarding / UX | MEDIUM (§6 — a fully-working detection-and-persistence mechanism was indistinguishable, from the user's perspective, from a broken one; no incorrect data was ever persisted, but the UX made a working feature look dead) | **`REMEDIATED — E2_AUTOMATED_VERIFIED, pending owner E4 retest`** | Discovered while investigating the owner's live report that tapping "استخدام موقعي الحالي" (Use Current Location) on the onboarding Location step "is tappable but doesn't appear to detect location" and "onboarding appears to continue/skip internally, with no clear confirmation a location was obtained." Traced end-to-end: `PrayerLocationController.useDeviceLocation()` (`lib/core/preferences/prayer_location_controller.dart`) was confirmed, by direct code read, to already correctly check location-service-enabled, check/request real OS permission, call `Geolocator.getCurrentPosition()`, and persist the result via `select()` — this part of the mechanism was never broken. The actual defect was entirely in the onboarding screen's UI layer: `_useDeviceLocation()` in `onboarding_screen.dart` called `_next()` immediately on success with **zero** visible confirmation of what (if anything) had just happened — success and a silent no-op were visually identical to the user, and the same immediate-advance-with-no-confirmation pattern existed for tapping any of the 6 popular-city buttons too. This is **distinct from `AUTH-006`** (formal separation recorded in that finding's own row): `AUTH-006` is that prayer location never survives a reinstall/new device because it is `SharedPreferences`-only with no server sync; `AUTH-011` is that even on the very same device, in the very same session, a successful detection or city selection was never visibly confirmed to the user. Fixing one does not fix the other. | `lib/features/onboarding/presentation/screens/onboarding_screen.dart` (`_useDeviceLocation`, `_selectCityLocation`, `_Location`, new `_LocationStatusBanner`) | **Remediated this wave**: introduced an explicit `_LocationStatus` state machine (`idle` → `detecting` → `selected`, plus a distinct `error` state) with a single always-visible status banner shown directly on the Location step — a real animated indicator + "Detecting your location…"/"جارٍ تحديد موقعكِ…" while in flight, a checkmark + "Location confirmed: [city or "Current location"]"/"تم تحديد موقعكِ: ..." on success (held visibly for 700ms before auto-advancing), and a real on-screen error message (matching the pre-existing SnackBar wording, so failure is visible even if the SnackBar is missed) for service-disabled/permission-denied/generic failure. All controls (both city chips and "Use current location") are disabled while a detection is in flight, so no competing tap can race an in-flight request. "Skip for now" (`تخطي الآن`) was verified to remain the only path that advances without ever touching `PrayerLocationController` — it does not fake a selected/confirmed state, and does not persist or overwrite any existing selection. All 6 popular-city buttons (Riyadh, Jeddah, Mecca/"Makkah", Medina/"Madinah", Dubai, Cairo — `PrayerLocationController.presets`) were individually confirmed to call `.select()` and show their own confirmation, so none is decorative-only. iOS `Info.plist`/Android manifest permission declarations were not re-checked this pass (pre-existing, out of the code path actually changed) — flagged as a remaining gap, not asserted as correct. **Tests**: a new `GeolocatorPlatform`-mocked test group (`test/onboarding_ui_test.dart`, "Live Onboarding Contradiction Investigation — Location UX state machine") covers, without any device or emulator (per this wave's explicit allowance for a mocked location service): the idle state shows no confirmation banner; a successful device-location fix shows `detecting` then a visible `selected` confirmation before advancing (using a completer-backed fake `GeolocatorPlatform` to deterministically freeze at each state); location-service-disabled and permission-denied both show a real visible error and never advance; a city-chip tap persists the exact tapped preset and shows its own confirmation (English and Arabic); Skip advances immediately and never alters an existing selection; and city chips/Skip are inert while a detection is genuinely in flight. 8/8 new tests passing; full existing `onboarding_ui_test.dart` suite (39 pre-existing tests) re-run and unaffected; full project regression (`flutter test`) shows only the same 10 pre-existing golden-image/screenshot-parity failures already tracked elsewhere in this engagement, none newly introduced. This is `E2` (automated-verified) — **not yet `E4`**; genuine live-device location detection was not exercised (no physical device/emulator with real GPS available this pass), so the owner's own real-device retest — including a real successful GPS fix, not only the mocked path — remains the closing evidence this finding needs. |
| AUTH-012 (**new**, Startup Auth-Gate Infinite-Spinner Investigation, 2026-09-15; **remediated in code, same wave — `E2_AUTOMATED_VERIFIED`, owner E4 retest required for closure**) | Authentication / Onboarding / Reliability | **CRITICAL — WHOLE-APP LAUNCH BLOCKER** — an authenticated returning user can be permanently unable to enter the app at all, on any device, with no in-app recovery path | **`REMEDIATED — E2_AUTOMATED_VERIFIED`, pending owner E4 retest** | The owner reproduced live, on a clean rebuild from the just-reconciled `main` (commit `27e53f50bd8145babba6e129e4abf3e31522944a`, all CI green): the app launches to a white screen, then a correctly-rendering Niswah loading indicator (confirmed this is **not** a `UI-001` regression — that finding concerned spinner rendering only and remains `VERIFIED_CLOSED`/`E4`), which spins indefinitely — the app never reaches Sign In, Onboarding, or Dashboard. Traced to an architectural defect in `AuthController.refreshOnboardingStatus()` (`lib/core/auth/auth_controller.dart`), proven by direct code reading, not guessed: the pre-fix contract used `bool? _onboardingCompleted`, where `null` meant both "still checking" and "check failed" — indistinguishable — and the update guard `if (completed != _onboardingCompleted) { ...; notifyListeners(); }` meant that whenever a check failed (any exception, or a `maybeSingle()` call returning no row) it set `completed = null`, which compared **equal** to the already-`null` in-flight value, so `notifyListeners()` was never even called — the root router (`main.dart`'s `_buildHome`) had no error/timeout/retry state to fall into regardless, only an unconditional full-page spinner for `onboardingCompleted == null`. This produces an indefinite hang whenever the server check resolves to `null` for **any** reason: a thrown exception (network failure, malformed response), a genuine RLS denial, or — proven the most likely trigger for this specific live case — an authenticated Supabase session (its JWT/refresh-token pair persisted in the device's secure storage, which a "clean rebuild" does not clear) referencing a user ID that no longer resolves to any row, e.g. a stale session left over from one of this engagement's own many synthetic disposable test accounts, created and later deleted via the Admin API on the same project this simulator points at. **Live database checks performed this pass rule out data corruption as the general-population cause**: all 24 current `auth.users` rows have a matching `public.users` row (zero orphans), `onboarding_completed` is genuinely `NOT NULL DEFAULT false` in production with zero NULL values among real accounts (8 `false`, 16 `true`), and the `users_read_own` RLS policy (`can_access_user(id)`: `auth.uid() = row_user_id or is_admin()`) correctly permits any authenticated user to read their own row — meaning the mechanism is real and live-database-consistent, but the exact triggering session on the owner's specific device could not be directly inspected (no live simulator/device access from this environment); this is disclosed honestly as the limit of remote diagnosis, not glossed over. | `lib/core/auth/auth_controller.dart` (`OnboardingStatus` enum replacing the ambiguous `bool?` contract; `refreshOnboardingStatus`/`_resolveOnboardingStatus` rewritten with a request-generation guard, a 15-second timeout, and explicit error classification); `lib/main.dart` (`_buildHome`'s router switches on the 4 explicit states; new `_AuthGateErrorScreen` widget) | **Remediated this wave**: `AuthController`'s `bool? _onboardingCompleted` contract replaced with an explicit `enum OnboardingStatus { loading, incomplete, complete, error }` — a genuine backend failure is now always distinguishable from "still loading," closing the exact architectural gap the code trace proved. `refreshOnboardingStatus()` now: (a) applies a 15-second timeout to the server call, so a hung network request can never wait forever; (b) treats a `maybeSingle()` `null` response (no matching `public.users` row) as a distinct, reported error — never silently treated as "onboarding complete" (would skip onboarding for someone who never did it) and never silently client-side-repaired (this controller cannot safely guess what a fabricated row's other fields should honestly contain — per this engagement's own standing "never guess, never fabricate" discipline, most recently applied to Madhhab's UNSET-not-Hanbali migration); (c) reports every failure (exception or missing row) through `AppErrorReporter`/Sentry with safe, opaque metadata (`context: 'AuthController.refreshOnboardingStatus'`, `feature: 'auth'` — no email, token, or health data), replacing the prior silent `catch (_) { completed = null; }`; (d) uses a monotonic request-generation counter, incremented on every refresh call **and** on every sign-out, so a slow/stale request from a previous session or a previous account can never overwrite a newer, more current result — proven via dedicated tests for both logout-in-flight and account-switch-in-flight. `main.dart`'s router now switches explicitly on all 4 states: `loading` shows the existing spinner (unchanged, still the correctly-rendering `UI-001`-verified component); `error` shows a new, real, bilingual recovery screen (`_AuthGateErrorScreen`) with a **Retry** button (re-runs the exact same check) and a **Sign out** button (the honest path forward for a session this device can never resolve on its own, e.g. one referencing a deleted account) — never routes to Dashboard or Onboarding by guessing. **12 new automated tests** (`test/auth_gate_test.dart`) cover: server returns incomplete/complete (routes correctly); a thrown exception (explicit error, reported, never stuck loading); a missing `public.users` row (same); a slow response (loading, then resolves correctly); a 15s timeout (explicit error, verified via `tester.pump`'s fake-clock advance past the bound); a retry after failure succeeding; logout mid-flight discarding a stale result; account-switch mid-flight preventing cross-account contamination; plus 3 widget-level tests confirming the router renders the real error screen (English and Arabic) for `OnboardingStatus.error` and the loading indicator (not the error screen) for `OnboardingStatus.loading`. All 12 pass; the pre-existing `auth_onboarding_routing_test.dart` suite (19 tests) re-run unchanged and passing, since its `setStateForTest(onboardingCompleted: bool?)` call sites remain fully backward-compatible with the new enum internally. Full regression: `flutter analyze` clean (same 43 pre-existing baseline info-level issues, 0 errors); `flutter test` 505/515 passing, the same 10 pre-existing golden-image failures, 0 new failures. This is `E2` (automated-verified) — **not yet `E4`**: no live device/simulator access was available to this session to directly reproduce the owner's own exact stuck session before or after the fix; the owner's own real-device retest, on the same simulator that currently hangs, is the closing evidence this finding needs. **Not classified as a Fiqh-feature-scoped blocker like `AUTH-005`/`AUTH-010`/`AUTH-011`** — this is a whole-app blocker, since it can strand ANY authenticated returning user, regardless of which features are enabled. |
| PJ-007 (**new**, Post-Reconciliation Governance Correction wave, 2026-09-14) | Final User Journey | MEDIUM (§6 — a feature is actively advertised to every single new user at the exact moment of highest trust, onboarding's own Welcome screen, but is unreachable; not a safety/correctness issue, a promise-not-kept issue) — **not launch-blocking** (owner decision: wire in or remove the promise) | **`OPEN` — newly formalized, not previously assigned a finding ID** | The Full Implementation Reconciliation pass (2026-09-14) found that onboarding's Welcome screen (`onboarding_screen.dart`, `_Welcome`/`_Feature` widgets) lists "Journeys" as a delivered feature to every new user. The screen implementing it, `GuidedJourneysScreen` (`lib/features/education/presentation/screens/guided_journeys_screen.dart`), is confirmed fully built but **orphaned** — zero references anywhere outside its own file, unreachable from any navigation path in the current app. **Governance classification (item 6 of the correction charter)**: this is **not** intentionally deferred in the way the founder dashboard's GRAY items are (retention period, DPA terms, age-gate, scholar review) — none of those advertise the missing feature to users while withholding it; this one does, which is a materially different, worse pattern (a kept promise's absence is invisible; a broken promise is visible to every user). Classified as approved-but-undelivered launch functionality, not a deliberate deferral, per the charter's own explicit instruction. | `lib/features/onboarding/presentation/screens/onboarding_screen.dart` (`_Welcome` advertises "Journeys"); `lib/features/education/presentation/screens/guided_journeys_screen.dart` (built, unreachable) | **`OPEN`** — real, confirmed gap; not launch-blocking per this pass's severity assessment; recommended fix is either wiring in the screen or removing the Welcome-screen promise before launch, owner's call. |
| UI-001 (**new**, Global Loading/Spinner Remediation wave, 2026-09-15; **owner E4 acceptance completed, 2026-09-15 — `VERIFIED_CLOSED`**) | UI/UX / Reliability | **MEDIUM** — launch-relevant (per the charter's own suggested classification, adopted as-is): a loading indicator that fails to render as a recognizable spinner leaves a real user unable to tell whether her action (creating an account, saving her Madhhab, sending a message) is still running or has silently failed — a trust/usability defect, not merely cosmetic. Not escalated to HIGH: every reachable, live code path this pass could inspect and empirically measure was already correctly sized (see investigation below), so the confirmed, reproducible blast radius is narrower than the owner's report initially suggested. **Governance note**: confirmed distinct from `AU-014` (loading-state *semantics/screen-reader* labels, `VERIFIED_CLOSED`) — this finding is about *visual rendering size*, a different defect class; `AU-014` is not reopened. | **`VERIFIED_CLOSED` / `E4`** (owner acceptance, 2026-09-15) — canonical component + representative replacement done; full-app migration to the canonical component remains a tracked, **non-blocking** cleanup task | **Owner live evidence**: repeated observation that loading indicators inside buttons can collapse into a tiny white dot/speck rather than a visible animated spinner, concretely on the Create Account (`sign_in_screen.dart`) submit button. **Inventory (Section A)**: 25 genuine `CircularProgressIndicator` call sites across 19 files (plus 2 comment-only references to the same pre-existing `AU-014` no-label exceptions, not new), zero `CupertinoActivityIndicator`, 8 `LinearProgressIndicator` sites (all confirmed determinate data/stat visualizations, not loading states, per `AU-014`'s own prior sweep — not re-investigated this pass). Every one of the 25 sites was individually inspected for widget/size/color/stroke-width/parent-constraint/shared-component status; results: 17 sites already wrap the indicator in an explicit `SizedBox`/`SizedBox.square` with a concrete dimension (15-22px for button/inline contexts); 8 sites are page-level `Center(child: CircularProgressIndicator())` with no explicit size, which is *correct* default Material behavior for a page loader (renders ~36-40dp), not a defect. **Zero of the 25 live, reachable sites use a bare, unconstrained `CircularProgressIndicator` inside a compact/button context** — the one site that does (`lib/features/settings/settings_screen.dart:261`) is confirmed orphaned/unreachable dead code (the `SettingsScreen` class has zero references anywhere outside its own file, per this engagement's own prior exhaustive-grep confirmation). **Root cause (Section B) — investigated empirically, not from static reading alone**: a dedicated widget test harness reproducing the exact `sign_in_screen.dart` Create Account button code (22×22 `SizedBox` wrapping `CircularProgressIndicator(strokeWidth: 2.4, color: Colors.white)`, `onPressed: null` while loading, matching the disabled-button state exactly) was pumped and directly measured via `tester.getSize`/`tester.renderObject` — result: renders at the intended 22×22 with `BoxConstraints(w=22, h=22)`, no ancestor `Opacity`/`Transform`/`FittedBox` found via `visitAncestorElements`, no `ProgressIndicatorThemeData` override exists anywhere in `AppTheme`, `year2023` (the flag governing Material 3's redesigned "growing arc" indicator style, which *does* have a small-arc-at-animation-start visual phase) defaults to `true` (the classic, non-growing indicator style) and is never overridden in this app. `git log` confirms the 22×22 `SizedBox` wrapper on this exact button has been present since this repository's first tracked commit (`6d59bfe`, 2026-09-03) — never introduced as a fix for a prior regression. **This pass could not reproduce the reported collapse from the current, live, reachable code** — the honest conclusion is that the specific code inspected is structurally sound, and the mechanism behind the owner's observation remains formally unconfirmed (candidate explanations, none proven: a build predating this session, or a device/OS-level rendering behavior — e.g. real GPU/Impeller rendering at fractional-pixel sizes with a thin stroke — outside what static analysis or a widget-test environment can observe). **Remediation performed regardless (Sections C/D/H), per the charter's explicit instruction to introduce a canonical component independent of pinpointing a single historical cause**: a new canonical `NiswahLoadingIndicator`/`NiswahLoadingButton` (`lib/core/widgets/niswah_loading_indicator.dart`) with 3 explicit, non-parent-dependent size variants (small=15, medium=22, large=40) and light/dark contrast, stable button dimensions between normal/loading states, and built-in double-submit protection (`onPressed` nulled while loading) now exists. Applied at the exact reported site (`sign_in_screen.dart`'s Create Account/Sign In button, OTP verify, resend-email) plus 6 further representative sites covering the charter's "at minimum audit" list: profile save (pregnancy-tracking activation), account deletion, one AI/network send button and its conversations-loading spinner (`dr_niswah_chat_screen.dart` — this screen is shared by all 3 AI modes including the Fiqh Advisor, so this single fix covers that surface too), and the app's root page-level loader (`main.dart`, which previously had **no** `semanticsLabel` at all — a genuine, separate `AU-014`-class gap found and fixed in the same pass). **The remaining ~15 already-correctly-sized sites (private messaging, community, data export, notification settings, resource library, dream interpreter) were not mechanically migrated to the canonical component this pass** — each already renders correctly today (confirmed via the same inventory), so migrating them is a recommended consistency improvement for a future pass, not a correctness requirement. Two deliberately-unlabeled `AU-014` exceptions (`dr_niswah_chat_screen.dart`'s `_TypingIndicator`, `dream_interpreter_screen.dart`'s equivalent) were confirmed unaffected and correctly left untouched. **No double-submission, race, or dispose-after-setState issues were found** during this audit (Section I) — every `onPressed` gate already correctly nulls itself while its own loading flag is true, at every site checked. | `lib/core/widgets/niswah_loading_indicator.dart` (new); `lib/features/auth/presentation/screens/sign_in_screen.dart`, `lib/features/auth/presentation/screens/profile_screen.dart`, `lib/features/ai_assistant/presentation/screens/dr_niswah_chat_screen.dart`, `lib/main.dart` (remediated); `test/niswah_loading_indicator_test.dart` (new, 9/9 passing — button/page spinner visibility, non-zero dimensions, no-overflow at 200% text scale in both languages, small viewport, stable button size, double-submit protection) | **`VERIFIED_CLOSED` / `E4`** — owner completed the prescribed retest on the build at commit `211ee76d535f3090cf6c250c5d6faeea672d2e2a` and reported all 4 items PASS: the Create Account loading indicator clearly visible; rendered as an actual animated indicator, not a dot/speck; button dimensions remained stable while loading; an additional loading state (profile save) also tested successfully. **Historical note, preserved per explicit instruction**: the original tiny-dot/collapsed-spinner mechanism was never conclusively reproduced against the audited source (see the root-cause investigation above, unchanged) — the canonical loading components and representative migrations nevertheless passed both automated verification and this owner live acceptance. The ~15 already-correctly-sized, not-yet-migrated sites identified in the inventory above remain a tracked, **non-blocking** cleanup task — recommended for consistency, not required for correctness, since none of them exhibited the defect. |
| AU-014 (**new**, AU-009 Acceptance wave, 2026-09-10) | Accessibility | Newly discovered | **LOW — VERIFIED_CLOSED (AU-014 Remediation wave, 2026-09-10)** | Originally discovered during AU-009's Phase B automated recheck: no loading spinner anywhere in the app carried a `semanticsLabel` — a screen-reader user waiting on any loading/saving/sending state would hear nothing about it. First fixed only for `sign_in_screen.dart` (3 sites, `UXJ-001`); the remaining 14 files were an explicit, tracked remainder, not silently assumed fixed. **This wave closes that remainder comprehensively.** Full inventory re-run (`CircularProgressIndicator` + `LinearProgressIndicator` + custom skeleton/loader widgets): 22 genuine loading-state instances found across 16 files (14 `CircularProgressIndicator` files + 2 more `LinearProgressIndicator`-only files not in the original count), all now carrying a contextual, localized `semanticsLabel` (e.g. "Loading messages"/"جارٍ تحميل الرسائل", "Sending message"/"جارٍ إرسال الرسالة", "Deleting account"/"جارٍ حذف الحساب" — not identical wording everywhere). **One additional, previously-undiscovered real defect found and fixed in the same sweep**: `community_board_screen.dart`'s loading state used 3 purely-decorative `_SkeletonCard()` placeholders with zero semantics of any kind — not even a spinner — so a screen reader announced nothing at all while the community board loaded; fixed with a single wrapping `Semantics(label: 'Loading posts'/'جارٍ تحميل المنشورات')` around the placeholder group (converted from `SliverList.list` to `SliverToBoxAdapter`+`Column` to make the wrapper possible — visually identical, since it's a fixed 3-item list, not a lazily-scrolling one). **2 instances deliberately left unlabeled, documented in code**: `dr_niswah_chat_screen.dart`'s `_TypingIndicator` and `dream_interpreter_screen.dart`'s equivalent "Reflecting…" indicator both sit directly next to already-visible status text ("Niswah is thinking…"/"Reflecting…") — confirmed via a real test that a bare, unlabeled `CircularProgressIndicator` contributes zero semantics nodes of its own, so adding a label would only duplicate the adjacent text's announcement. **5 other progress-bar instances confirmed out of scope, not touched**: `ghusl_guide_screen.dart`, `onboarding_screen.dart` (step-progress bars, not loading), and `insights_screen.dart`/`cycle_tracking_screen.dart`/`guided_journeys_screen.dart` (determinate data/stat visualization bars with their own adjacent percentage text, not loading states) — confirmed via direct code read that none are gated on any loading boolean. | 16 files remediated (see `00_09` §60 for the full list); `dr_niswah_chat_screen.dart`/`dream_interpreter_screen.dart` each have 1 documented no-label exception; 5 files' progress bars confirmed non-loading and untouched | **VERIFIED_CLOSED** |
| PJ-003 | Final User Journey | PJ2 Medium | MEDIUM — **VERIFIED_CLOSED (Final Application Code Blockers wave, 2026-09-06)** | A correctly-firing safety banner could render simultaneously with a generic/alarming error state on the same chat screen for the same failed request. **Root cause**: `_sendViaDrNiswahBackend`'s catch block appended a reassuring banner message to `messages` when a red flag was detected locally, then unconditionally `rethrow`s — which the outer `sendMessage`'s catch block always turned into `errorMessage = error.toString()`, a second, contradictory signal for the exact same failure. **Fixed**: when the local red-flag fallback has already shown the banner, the method no longer rethrows — it reports the failure directly via `AppErrorReporter` (so observability is unaffected) and returns normally, meaning `errorMessage` is never also set for this case. A non-red-flag message's failure is completely unchanged — it still rethrows and still surfaces a generic `errorMessage`, which is the correct, coherent behavior for an ordinary (non-urgent) failure. `_sendViaDrNiswahBackend` was made public/`@visibleForTesting` (renamed `sendViaDrNiswahBackendForTesting`, no behavior change) so the fix is directly testable without mocking the hardcoded `DrNiswahBackendService` singleton. **Tested**: `test/dr_niswah_red_flag_dual_state_test.dart` (3 tests) — a red-flag failure shows only the banner (`errorMessage` stays `null`); a non-red-flag failure still throws normally, unchanged; two separate urgent exchanges get distinct message ids, no collision | **VERIFIED_CLOSED — one coherent signal per failed request, proven by 3 passing tests exercising the real production code path** |

**Note on RD-001/RD-002/RD-003/RD-005:** These are the Release/Deployment audit's formal, re-verified adoption of DC-005/SEC-003, DC-010, DC-006, and DC-001/DC-003 respectively — same underlying defects, not additional distinct blockers. They are not re-listed as separate rows above to avoid double-counting; see `ROOT-003`/`ROOT-004` below, which now also list RD as an owning domain.

The Observability audit independently confirmed that for four realistic failure scenarios (AI provider outage, an RLS data leak, a DI-002 recurrence, a launch crash), **none would currently be detected by any mechanism in the app** — not even the exact code path that already caused the documented incident, which is unchanged today (OB-006). The Final User Journey audit then traced this root cause all the way to its real-world, user-visible consequences: haid data plausibly lost with no warning (PJ-002), an urgent chat exchange that self-erases entirely including the safety banner (PJ-004), and a doctor's report structurally unable to reveal it's missing safety data (PJ-006, `VERIFIED_CLOSED` as of the Doctor's Report Data Completeness + Truthfulness wave, 2026-09-06 — the report itself can no longer silently misrepresent incomplete data as complete, though the underlying `flagged_conversations` write-reliability question this root cause also names remains open via `PJ-001`).
