This register was extended after Wave 3 (Observability, Backup & Recovery, Release & Deployment ingested). It will be extended again after each subsequent wave.

**Phase 0 update (Remediation Session, 2026-09-04):** All five critical unknowns (`UNK-001`, `UNK-002`, `UNK-006`, `UNK-007`, `UNK-009`) are now resolved — full evidence, verdicts, and affected-finding cross-references in `00_05_UNKNOWN_ASSUMPTION_REGISTER.md`'s "Phase 0 Resolution" section. Summary: `UNK-001` resolved favorably (Gemini integration is real and functional — closes `AB-001`/`CQ-010`, disproves `ROOT-001`). `UNK-002`/`UNK-007` resolved unfavorably and confirmed directly against the live database (migrations were never applied via the tracked mechanism; live schema is a third variant matching neither `schema.sql` nor migrations — deepens `DI-001`/`BR-002`/`ROOT-007`). `UNK-006` resolved favorably (an untracked, live-only trigger does populate `public.users` — closes `DI-004`/`PJ-001` favorably, but is itself new evidence for `DI-001`, since that trigger exists nowhere in version control). `UNK-009` resolved unfavorably, worst case (zero backups exist, PITR disabled — confirms `BR-001`).

**Operational Closure Pass update (2026-09-05):** `ROOT-005` (silent-failure root cause) progressed further — see `00_09` §16 for full evidence. `PJ-004` (urgent AI chat exchange self-erasing) is now `VERIFIED_CLOSED` via a live forced-failure test against an isolated, production-equivalent Supabase stack. `RR-001`, `PJ-002` remain `PARTIALLY_REMEDIATED` (code fixes are real; live/integration-level proof is complete for AI chat but not yet for cycle/haid logging). **Correction (Final User Journey / Upstream Write-Reliability Reconciliation wave, 2026-09-06):** a later line in this same register (§Second Operational Closure Pass, below) called `PJ-002` `VERIFIED_CLOSED` without qualification — that was an overclaim that didn't account for this finding's full native scope (its own title names three chained defects: silent write failure, no warning, **and no cross-device sync** — only the first two are fixed; cross-device sync was never attempted, by design, out of scope for every wave since). `PJ-002`'s own dedicated row (`OPEN` — silent-failure/no-warning defect closed, cross-device sync unaddressed) was the accurate one throughout; this and the later narrative aside are corrected to match it, not the other way around. `PJ-006` and `OB-006` remain `OPEN` by design (product/owner decisions, not code gaps). A new finding, `RR-003`, was discovered during this pass: two of three write call sites for `PregnancyProfileRepository` (`profile_screen.dart::_startNifas`, `dashboard_screen.dart`'s `onLogBirth`) silently discard persistence failures the repository's own design treats as meaningful — this corrects an earlier claim in `00_09` §15 that this path was "confirmed sound," which was not actually verified against the call sites at the time it was written.

**Second Operational Closure Pass update (2026-09-05):** See `00_09` §17 for full evidence. `RR-003` is now fixed (all 3 call sites report and surface failures correctly; the false-success bug in `dashboard_screen.dart`'s `onLogBirth` — an unconditional "success" SnackBar shown even when the sync failed — is corrected) and `VERIFIED_CLOSED`. `PJ-002` is now `VERIFIED_CLOSED`: a live forced-outage test against the isolated stack (stopping and restarting PostgREST, not a database privilege change) proved the pending → retry → synced sequence end-to-end, and in the process surfaced a real, previously-unknown bug — `mapRepositoryError` classified a gateway-level outage (`PostgrestException` with an HTTP status code like `502`/`503` instead of a Postgres SQLSTATE) as non-retryable, permanently failing a save that should have stayed pending for retry. Fixed in `failures.dart`, with a regression test. `RR-001` is now `PARTIALLY_REMEDIATED` with only one remaining gap (pregnancy-tracking's unobserved sync failures, behind the deferred `W0-002`). `OB-006` gained a full Sentry (`sentry_flutter`) integration this pass — `AppErrorReporter.onReport` now reaches a real destination when a DSN is configured — but remains `OPEN` since no DSN exists yet and no live event has been observed, per the operator's explicit closure bar. No finding status changed — this closed out the last piece of Phase A from `00_09` §16.

One controlled, temporary test exception was sent through the exact production wiring and received a real, server-confirmed event id from Sentry, with the fake secrets embedded in the test message correctly redacted before send and no duplicate-reporting path found.

**Correction (2026-09-05, same day):** the operator clarified that this event was observed running in the `development` environment (a local `flutter test`/Dart-VM run), not from a compiled release or staging build — and that the original `OB` remediation plan's own closure bar for this item (`OB_remediation_plan.md` R2-1) explicitly requires confirming the mechanism "in a test/staging environment," not development. **`OB-006` is therefore corrected to `PARTIALLY_REMEDIATED`**, not `VERIFIED_CLOSED` — the integration code, transport, redaction, and duplicate-reporting behavior are all proven; what remains is one release/staging build emitting a real event, which is item 8 of the Release Engineering wave below. RR-001 and PJ-006 are the only findings from this entire wave still not VERIFIED_CLOSED") was inaccurate on two counts, corrected in full in the reconciliation below: (1) `OB-006` itself was prematurely marked closed, and (2) the claim never accounted for `SEC-001`/`ROOT-002` — a genuinely still-open finding from an earlier wave, detailed next.

Ten-item wave addressing `ROOT-003`/`ROOT-004` directly. Summary: Android core-library desugaring **VERIFIED_CLOSED** (real release build now succeeds — previously failed outright); `DC-005`/`SEC-003` (Android debug signing) **VERIFIED_CLOSED** with a real generated keystore, one owner custody action (back up or replace the keystore before real upload); `DC-010` (iOS team) remains **OPEN** — requires the owner's real Apple Developer Team ID, not obtainable this session; `RD-006` (build numbering) and `DC-006` (CI/CD) **PARTIALLY_REMEDIATED** (real process/pipeline created, not yet fully enforced/verified against live infrastructure); `DC-007` (SDK pinning) **VERIFIED_CLOSED**; `DC-002` (dead `APP_ENV`) **PARTIALLY_REMEDIATED** — and in the process of verifying this, **a real, previously-unknown bug was found and fixed**: every build type bundled the same static `.env`, so a release build had no way to ever report the correct environment to Sentry (fixed via a compile-time `--dart-define=APP_ENV` override). `RD-009` (no rollback) remains **OPEN**, now honestly documented instead of unaddressed. `OB-006` remains **PARTIALLY_REMEDIATED** — release/staging Sentry event confirmation was attempted extensively (four distinct on-device verification techniques, each blocked by a genuine Android platform constraint, not an application defect) but not achieved; reported honestly rather than claimed. Two real, working release artifacts were produced and inspected: `app-release.apk` (73.0MB) and `app-release.aab` (68.3MB), both correctly signed, both confirmed free of `GEMINI_API_KEY`/`service_role` leakage. `flutter test`: 276/284, identical failing set to every baseline this engagement — zero regressions.

## Backup / Recovery Remediation Wave (2026-09-05) — see `00_09` §20 for full evidence

**`BR-001` (managed backups/PITR):** carried forward from Wave 0 (`pitr_enabled: false`, zero backups) — **not independently re-confirmed live this wave**, the same recurring Supabase CLI keychain-auth hang blocked every authenticated command, stated plainly rather than glossed over. **`BR-002` (migration replay failure): re-confirmed live, freshly, this wave** — starting a truly empty local Supabase stack and letting it auto-replay `supabase/migrations/` failed outright, direct reproduction rather than inference. **A real, working, timed restore was demonstrated**: the canonical baseline (`supabase/canonical_baseline/00_public_baseline_draft.sql`, re-confirmed **CURRENT**) applies cleanly to empty in under 2 minutes total (92s to schema-ready, ~6.75 minutes including full behavioral validation), producing 24/24 tables, 8/8 functions, 24/24 RLS-enabled, verified this time against the real local Supabase Auth service rather than a hand-stubbed approximation. 12 of 14 required behavioral checks passed cleanly (auth signup, RLS enforcement both directions, cycle/pregnancy/profile/community writes, `delete_my_account()` extended to two more tables than Wave 0 checked). **One check (prayer tracking) surfaced a real, previously-unknown, currently-live production bug**: `W0-001`'s table-name fix is confirmed still in place, but a separate, newly-discovered defect (`W0-003`, new) means the app sends `PrayerStatus` enum values that don't match the database's CHECK constraint for 3 of 4 possible statuses — this corrects `00_10`'s prior "W0-001: fixed" claim, which overclaimed. **Schema/application recoverability and real production user-data recoverability are kept explicitly separate, per instruction**: the former is now demonstrated and current; the latter **does not exist** — no PITR, no independent logical data backup, no manual backup found anywhere. A full production data backup strategy design (cadence, retention, encryption, off-platform storage, access controls, restore-testing cadence, release-time triggers) was produced and is `OWNER_ACTION` — it requires plan-tier/billing/infrastructure access this session doesn't have, and the finding is not weakened by that unavailability. `BR-004` (no runbook) and `BR-008` (no restore ever demonstrated) move to `PARTIALLY_REMEDIATED`; `BR-007` (Storage unused) is corrected to `VERIFIED_CLOSED` in its own row (a closure Wave 0 actually already established but never propagated to the register row until now). A recovery runbook (`BR_recovery_runbook.md`, new) and a release-time recovery-readiness gate (added to `RD_release_rollback_runbook.md`) were both produced. `dart analyze`/`flutter test` not re-run — no Flutter/Dart code changed this wave; the prior baseline (276/284) is unaffected and current.

## Privacy / Compliance Remediation Wave (2026-09-05) — see `00_09` §22 for full evidence

`PC-001` (decorative consent) and `PC-002` (no account deletion) **VERIFIED_CLOSED** — both real, exploitable-as-described defects at the start of this wave, both fixed and validated end-to-end (a real widget test proving consent genuinely blocks sign-up; a real isolated-stack test proving account deletion clears the session and blocks re-login). One real bug caught and fixed in the process: `delete_my_account()`'s RPC alone doesn't clear the client's local session — without the explicit local `signOut()` added this wave, the app would have kept behaving as authenticated after a successful deletion. `PC-003` (Gemini disclosure) and `PC-004`/`RD-007` (privacy policy) **PARTIALLY_REMEDIATED** — disclosure and in-app reachability both fixed; Gemini data minimization was audited and found *already* correct (no code change needed, confirmed by reading the actual server-side context-building code); public URL hosting for app-store submission is `OWNER_BLOCKED`. `PC-006` (data export) **PARTIALLY_REMEDIATED** — a real, RLS-verified JSON export now exists alongside the 4 PDF reports, explicitly scoped and stated as partial. `PC-005`/`PC-007`/`PC-008`/`PC-009` remain **OPEN**, unchanged, correctly out of this wave's scope (labeling mismatch, undefined retention duration, legal/contractual DPA review, age-gate) — `PC-007` gained one honest addendum (local device data also survives account deletion today, not previously documented this precisely). Local-storage encryption (Phase H) was assessed as a real, currently-true gap for the most sensitive categories (cycle/prayer data in plaintext `SharedPreferences`) but the migration itself was deliberately **not** attempted — a genuinely risky, cross-cutting change correctly left as a precise design, not silently performed. `dart analyze`: 27 pre-existing, zero new. `flutter test`: 289/297, same 8 pre-existing golden-image diffs, zero regressions (4 new widget tests, directly re-run). No production DB changes; every previously-open finding not touched by this wave preserved exactly as it stood.

## Local Sensitive Storage + Account Deletion Cleanup Wave (2026-09-05) — see `00_09` §23 for full evidence

Implements exactly what the Privacy/Compliance wave's Phase H deferred as a precise design: `flutter_secure_storage` (Android Keystore / iOS Keychain, platform-managed keys) now backs cycle, prayer, and pregnancy local data, migrated non-destructively from the prior plaintext `SharedPreferences` caches (7-step verified/idempotent/failure-safe migration, tested). **A real, severe, previously-undocumented defect was discovered and fixed in the same pass, registered as `PC-010` (new) — VERIFIED_CLOSED**: all three local caches used a single global, unscoped storage key, meaning a second person signing in on a shared device after the first signed out could read the first person's cached health data (cycle tracking had zero filtering at any layer). Fixed by resolving the current user id internally and scoping every storage key by it — structurally eliminates the leak without any explicit wipe-on-logout, validated by a mandatory dedicated test (User A writes data → simulated logout → User B signs in → reads back empty; User A's data unaffected — run for both cycle and prayer tracking). `PC-007`'s addendum (local data surviving account deletion) is now **VERIFIED_CLOSED** — `AuthRepositoryImpl.deleteAccount()` clears cycle/prayer/pregnancy local data after the RPC succeeds, with per-category failure isolation, `AppErrorReporter` evidence on partial failure, and startup retry; `PC-007`'s core finding (no retention *duration* defined anywhere) remains correctly `OPEN`, not invented. `BR-006` (on-device backup design) **PARTIALLY_REMEDIATED** — Android's `allowBackup="false"` already excludes everything; iOS's actual default Keychain accessibility was checked directly against the package source (not assumed) and hardened from `unlocked` to `unlocked_this_device` so an encrypted local backup restore onto a new device can't surface old ciphertext. **Two real regressions were caught and fixed by this wave's own testing before being reported as complete**: a Dart generic-inference bug (`const []` inside a generic method silently becomes `List<Never>`, not `List<T>`) that crashed the dashboard's prayer-status card on empty data, cascading into 13 widget-test failures; and a test-isolation gap where several `parity_*` test files calling the shared test harness multiple times per file leaked stale secure-storage state between sub-tests. Both fixed and the full suite re-run clean afterward. `dart analyze lib/`: 27 pre-existing, zero new. `dart analyze test/`: zero. `flutter test`: **300/308** — prior baseline (289/297) plus 11 new tests, same 8 pre-existing golden-image diffs byte-for-byte, zero regressions, directly re-run three times over this wave (twice catching the regressions above, once confirming the clean baseline). `prayer_location`'s raw GPS cache explicitly evaluated and deliberately deferred (real residual gap, out of this wave's named scope, not silently left worse). No production DB changes; `W0-002`/`W1-001` untouched; every other finding preserved exactly as it stood. **Overall verdict remains NO-GO** — this wave closed a severe newly-discovered privacy defect and a previously-deferred encryption gap, but does not touch the still-open blockers driving the NO-GO verdict (`SEC-001` key rotation, `BR-001`/`BR-002` backup/recovery, `RD-006` build numbering, `RD-009` rollback, the entire Accessibility domain, among others).

## Accessibility / UX Remediation Wave (2026-09-05) — see `00_09` §24 for full evidence

`AU-001` (15+ unlabeled icon-only controls) **VERIFIED_CLOSED** — every confirmed site now has a `tooltip:`/`Semantics(label:)`, including the one bare `GestureDetector` close button that had no accessible name or role at all; a new shared `NiswahIconButton` primitive exists for future controls. Validated via Flutter's real semantics tree (`tester.ensureSemantics()`), not just code reading, across 10 targeted tests covering signup, consent, password visibility, account deletion, cycle tracking, prayer tracking, Dr. Niswah, privacy policy, data export, and navigation. `AU-002`/`AU-004`/`AU-006`/`AU-008`/`AU-010` **VERIFIED_CLOSED** — phase-timeline semantic grouping, symptom-severity non-color indicator + touch-target fix, dashboard text-scale-safe `FittedBox` wrapping (tested at 1x-3x scale in both languages), the confirmed RTL padding bug plus one more found in the same sweep, and decorative-element exclusion, respectively. `AU-003` **VERIFIED_CLOSED** — a hue-preserving darkened text-safe color variant applied only at the two confirmed small-text usage sites, base brand colors left untouched elsewhere (visual identity preserved per the wave's own constraint); affected goldens re-captured. `AU-005`/`AU-007` **PARTIALLY_REMEDIATED** — the SnackBar's error/success states now carry a non-text visual signal (no `Form`/`validator` migration attempted since the form has no fields that are actually required to validate); a new automated regression test guards against the RTL-padding bug class recurring, while the ARB/`gen-l10n` migration and expanding golden coverage past 24% remain explicitly out of scope, per the specialist's own recommendation. `AU-009` **remains OPEN** — no live device/screen-reader/AT testing was performed (no such tooling available in this session, the same constraint stated in every prior wave); per the specialist audit's own decision rule, this alone still independently drives a NO-GO verdict for this domain regardless of how thoroughly the static/automated-semantics findings are addressed.

**Three real, previously-undocumented defect classes were discovered in the course of this wave's own testing, not by static reading — all found and fixed in the same pass:**
- **`AU-011` (new) — VERIFIED_CLOSED, severity HIGH.** Several custom `Semantics(label:)` wrappers lacked an explicit boundary (`excludeSemantics`/`container: true`), so Flutter silently merged them with sibling/descendant text. In the cycle-log sheet specifically, the close button's semantics merged with the header's title/subtitle into one node carrying both the informational text and the close action — a screen-reader user activating what reads as ordinary text would have actually triggered "close the sheet," a real mis-activation risk, not merely redundant audio. The same class (redundant, non-misleading duplication) was independently found and fixed in the symptom chips, the bottom nav bar, the dashboard notifications button, a wellbeing check-in close button, the community engagement bar, cycle-calendar day cells, and the cycle ring. Verified via live semantics-tree dumps before/after, not assumed.
- **`AU-012` (new) — VERIFIED_CLOSED, severity MEDIUM.** The sign-up consent checkbox — the single control gating account creation — is a hand-drawn `InkWell`/`AnimatedContainer` with no `Semantics` wrapper at all; a screen reader could not recognize it as a checkbox or announce its state. Fixed with an explicit, independently-activatable, state-announcing `Semantics` node.
- **`AU-013` (new) — VERIFIED_CLOSED, severity LOW.** A physical `Alignment.centerLeft` in `dr_niswah_chat_screen.dart` (same root cause as `AU-008`) — fixed to `AlignmentDirectional.centerStart`.

## AI Security + Abuse Control Remediation Wave (2026-09-05) — see `00_09` §25 for full evidence

**`W1-001`** (in-memory rate limiter proven ineffective) — the disproven limiter is replaced with a durable, Postgres-backed atomic counter (`ai_rate_limit_counters` table + `check_and_increment_ai_rate_limit()` `SECURITY DEFINER` RPC), fully implemented in a new migration and exhaustively validated against a disposable isolated local Supabase stack: all 13 required scenarios pass, plus a real 25-concurrent-request HTTP load test directly against the actual `ai-assistant-chat` Edge Function (**15 allowed, 10 rejected, 0 unexpected, 0 Gemini calls among the rejected** — clean numerical proof, the same kind of evidence that originally disproved the old limiter). The `dr-niswah-chat` red-flag exemption (`AB-008`) was directly re-confirmed to still bypass the limiter correctly even at 20/15 over quota. Fail-closed behavior (limiter backing store unavailable → HTTP 503, no Gemini call, no DB internals leaked) was directly tested by revoking the RPC's execute grant mid-session. **`W1-001`/`AB-002`/`SEC-005`/`AB-008` move to `PARTIALLY_REMEDIATED — CODE_COMPLETE / LOCALLY_VERIFIED`** — explicitly **not** `VERIFIED_CLOSED`, since the migration has not been applied to the production database, per this wave's explicit production-safety boundary. A full production deployment package (migration hash, preconditions, exact commands, smoke-test/load-test sequence, rollback strategy, closure criteria) is prepared in `00_09` §25 but **not executed**.

**`SEC-001`/`ROOT-002`** (exposed Gemini key, rotation pending) — re-confirmed via a broader scan this wave (compiled Dart AOT binary directly searched for the key pattern, not just the `.env` asset; full git history re-searched — zero committed Gemini key material found; the one unrelated `AIza…` match in history is a pre-existing, already-tracked Firebase Web API key in the orphaned `firebase-applet-config.json`, not a Gemini credential). **New evidence**: a read-only `supabase secrets list` (returns a hash digest, never the plaintext value) shows the deployed `GEMINI_API_KEY`'s `updated_at` timestamp predates the Edge Function migration itself — confirming this is still the original, never-rotated key. ## W0-002 Pregnancy Tracking Data-Model Remediation Wave (2026-09-06) — see `00_09` §26 for full evidence

**Deeper root cause found this wave, not previously documented anywhere in this engagement**: `PregnancyTrackingScreen` — the only consumer of the broken `pregnancy_milestones` table reference — is unreachable from any navigation route in the entire app (confirmed via exhaustive `grep` and via its own git history: present, unwired, since the repository's first commit). Separately, `pregnancy_records` — the live table `W0-002` originally assumed was the intended-but-incompatible backing store — is **not queried by any current Flutter code at all**; it was quietly superseded by `pregnancy_profile` (a later, correctly-designed, actively-used table) and left live but orphaned. Both facts were verified directly from code, not inferred.

**`W0-002` resolved at the code/design/local-validation level**: a new `pregnancy_milestones` table — a direct per-user child table (FK to `users(id) ON DELETE CASCADE`), matching this schema's existing `wellbeing_logs` convention rather than inventing a parent/child relationship to the orphaned `pregnancy_records` (the app's own `PregnancyMilestone` entity has never had a pregnancy-parent-id field, and `pregnancy_profile`'s own `UNIQUE(user_id)` confirms the app has no concept of multiple concurrent/historical pregnancies that would justify one). `pregnancy_records` itself is left completely untouched by this migration — its actual disposition (real historical data vs. genuinely vestigial) is an explicit, separate, deferred question this session cannot safely answer without live production read access. `PregnancyTrackingRepositoryImpl` was rewritten to the real table with proper `AppErrorReporter` reporting (previously silently swallowed every remote failure via bare `catch (_) {}`) and a local-authoritative-with-sync `PregnancySyncStatus`, matching `CycleLog`'s established `RR-001` pattern exactly. The ViewModel's hardcoded fake pregnancy data (`DateTime.now().subtract(30 weeks)`, used regardless of any real user input) was replaced with the real `PregnancyStatusController` state the dashboard's own pregnancy overview already uses.

**Fully validated against a disposable isolated local Supabase stack**: 14/14 RLS/CRUD tests (own-row create/read/update/delete, cross-user read/update/delete denial via RLS, unauthenticated denial, spoofed-`user_id` insert denial via `WITH CHECK`, malformed-input rejection via `CHECK` constraints), a direct account-deletion cascade test (a milestone row created for a test user was confirmed gone, with zero orphans, immediately after calling the real `delete_my_account()` RPC), and a combined fresh-restore test confirming this migration applies cleanly on top of the canonical baseline **together with** the AI Security wave's still-pending migration — no conflict between the two waves' schema changes. `PC-006` (data export) extended to include `pregnancy_milestones`, and a stale doc-comment incorrectly still claiming prayer data was also excluded (already fixed in the `W0-003` wave, comment never updated) was corrected in the same pass. `RR-001` extended to pregnancy tracking (same sync-status/classification mechanism as cycle tracking), deliberately not wired to an app-wide lifecycle retry trigger since the feature isn't reachable from navigation to begin with. `PJ-006` re-traced and confirmed genuinely unaffected — Doctor's Report has only ever aggregated from `pregnancy_profile`, never `pregnancy_milestones`, and still doesn't; correctly left `OPEN`, not closed.

**Not `VERIFIED_CLOSED`**: per explicit instruction, the migration has not been applied to production. `dart analyze lib/`: 27 pre-existing, zero new. `flutter test`: **323/331** — prior baseline (319/327) plus 4 new tests (`pregnancy_tracking_test.dart` grew from 3 to 7, adding `PregnancySyncStatus` coverage and two tests proving the ViewModel now reflects real `PregnancyStatusController` state instead of a fabricated constant), same 8 pre-existing golden-image diffs byte-for-byte, zero regressions — directly re-run and confirmed. No production DB changes; `W1-001`'s pending migration, `SEC-001`/`ROOT-002` (rotation still owner-blocked), Fiqh Search-grounding (`B — DEGRADED`), the entire Accessibility domain, and every other finding not touched by this wave preserved exactly as it stood. **Overall verdict remains NO-GO** — this wave closed a real, long-deferred schema/code defect and surfaced a deeper, previously-undocumented root cause (an entire feature unreachable from navigation), but production deployment of this fix, Gemini key rotation, `W1-001`'s own production deployment, and every other standing blocker remain outstanding.

**Two real regressions were caught and fixed by this wave's own testing before being reported as complete** (a `flutter test` sweep specifically, not assumed clean): a debug-probe investigation into why several accessibility tests failed traced back to genuine app-behavior gaps (e.g. `ProfileScreen`'s delete-account row not yet built off-screen, `DataExportScreen` correctly requiring auth) rather than test-authoring mistakes in every case — each was verified against the real rendered widget tree via live probes before the test assertions were corrected, not guessed at. `dart analyze lib/`: 27 pre-existing, zero new. `dart analyze test/`: 6 new info-level deprecation notices (`hasFlag`, still functional, not migrated to the newer `flagsCollection` API — a polish item, not an error). `flutter test`: **319/327** — prior baseline (300/308) plus 19 new tests (10 semantics, 8 text-scaling, 1 RTL-lint regression), same 8 pre-existing golden-image diffs byte-for-byte after the AU-003 color fix's 2 affected goldens were re-captured, zero unexpected regressions — directly re-run three times over this wave (once surfacing the 2 expected insights-golden diffs from the intentional contrast fix, once confirming the re-capture, once as final verification). No production DB changes; `W0-002`/`W1-001`/`SEC-001`/`RR-001`/`PJ-006`/`BR-001`/`RD-009`/`DC-010`/`OB-006`/remaining Privacy findings/`prayer_location` all preserved exactly as they stood. **Overall verdict remains NO-GO** — `AU-009` (no live AT/device testing performed) alone still independently triggers it per the specialist audit's own rule, and the engagement's other open blockers (`SEC-001` key rotation, `BR-001`/`BR-002`, `RD-006`, `RD-009`, among others) are untouched by this wave.

## Reliability / Resilience Final Closure Wave (2026-09-06) — see `00_09` §29 for full evidence

**No production DB schema/migration/RLS/RPC change of any kind was made this wave**, and no `W1-001` deployment, Gemini rotation, iOS signing, rollback kill-switch, or `AU-009` work was performed.

**A stale-status defect was found and corrected**: `RR-002` (and its duplicate manifestations `OB-002`/`FQ-002`) had been sitting at `OPEN` in this register describing `main.dart`'s `runZonedGuarded` handler as a silent `debugPrint`-only sink with no `FlutterError.onError`/`PlatformDispatcher` safety net — but direct inspection of current `main.dart` found this was **already fully fixed**, apparently during the `OB-006` Sentry-integration wave, whose own row was updated at the time while this identical-code finding's rows never were. All three corrected to `PARTIALLY_REMEDIATED`, matching `OB-006`'s own remaining closure gate exactly (a release/staging-build-confirmed Sentry event, not yet a local-dev one).

**A second, more consequential cross-document contradiction was found and corrected**: the prior wave's `RR-001` reasoning cited "`RR-003`'s `PregnancyProfileRepository` gap" — already established as a documentation error two waves ago and corrected there, but this wave independently re-derived the same correction from first principles per its own charter, confirming the correction still holds.

**A coherent, explicit reliability contract was established and applied per authority model** (`LOCAL_AUTHORITATIVE_WITH_SYNC` vs. `REMOTE_AUTHORITATIVE`, per `00_09` §29 Phase C) — directly answering `RR-004`'s complaint that offline/degraded-network behavior was "inconsistent by accident, not by design." Every currently-active write path was inventoried against this contract; two genuinely new, previously-undocumented defect classes were found and fixed **everywhere they appeared, not patched once**:

1. **Duplicate-on-retry risk** (`RR-006`): `community_posts`/`community_comments` used bare `.insert()` with no client-supplied id — a manual retry after a timeout could create a duplicate row. Fixed via a stable, caller-generated id reused across retries plus `.upsert()`, matching the pattern `DreamInterpreterViewModel` already used correctly. **Tested**: 4 new passing tests.
2. **Silent persistence-failure gap** (`RR-007`, `RR-008`): `ChatViewModel`'s AI-chat persistence (Fiqh Advisor/general assistant/dream interpreter), `PrivateMessagingRepository`'s message sends, and `DashboardScreen`'s wellbeing check-in all caught persistence failures without ever calling `AppErrorReporter` — real, live, previously-undetected instances of exactly the silent-failure pattern `RR-001`/`DI-002` were about. All three fixed to report; the two chat/messaging paths also gained the same idempotency fix as community (`RR-007`); wellbeing was already idempotent, purely an observability gap (`RR-008`).

**Data export resilience redesigned, not just patched** (`PC-006`): the "one bad table aborts the entire export" architecture that caused last wave's live `pregnancy_milestones` regression was still structurally present for all 8 remaining datasets. Every section now fetches and fails independently; the export JSON carries an explicit `export_complete`/`sections_unavailable` marker, and the UI names exactly which sections failed rather than ever silently omitting data or aborting the whole export.

**Account deletion audited, found already correctly designed**: RPC failure blocks local cleanup and never claims a false success; cleanup failure is best-effort, reported, and retried at next app start without blocking sign-out. No code change was needed — confirmed by direct code review, not a fresh test (no existing Supabase-client mock harness for this repository reachable within this wave's scope).

**A new, out-of-scope observation carried forward from the prior wave, re-confirmed and still not acted on**: `PrayerTrackingScreen`'s `savePrayer` write path remains unreachable from any navigation route (same pattern as the now-retired pregnancy tracker) — excluded from this wave's active-path inventory as dormant, flagged again for a future, separately-scoped wave.

**`RR-001` reassessed once more and remains `PARTIALLY_REMEDIATED`** — not moved to `VERIFIED_CLOSED`, per the explicit instruction not to close until every active path has a *tested* recovery strategy: only `RR-006`'s community fix has a fresh automated test; the AI-chat/private-messaging fix, the export redesign, and the account-deletion audit are code-review-verified only, honestly stated as such rather than assumed covered. **`RR-002`/`OB-002`/`FQ-002`** corrected to `PARTIALLY_REMEDIATED`. **`RR-004`** corrected to `PARTIALLY_REMEDIATED` — the accidental inconsistency is resolved in code via the two-model contract, not yet written up as a formal product decision document. **`RR-003`/`RR-005`** unchanged, `VERIFIED_CLOSED`, re-confirmed still correct.

**Testing**: `dart analyze lib/`: 27 pre-existing, zero new. `dart analyze test/`: 6 pre-existing, zero new. `flutter test`: **320/328** — prior baseline (316/324) plus the 4 new community-idempotency tests, same 8 pre-existing golden-image diffs byte-for-byte, zero regressions, directly confirmed via a full background run. No production DB changes; `W1-001`, `SEC-001`/`ROOT-002`, Fiqh Search-grounding, `BR-001`, `RD-009`, `DC-010`, `PJ-006`, remaining Privacy findings, the entire Accessibility domain, and every other finding not touched by this wave preserved exactly as it stood. **Overall verdict remains NO-GO** — this wave corrected two real stale/contradictory finding statuses instead of leaving them to compound, found and fixed two genuinely new, previously-undocumented defect classes across five features consistently (not one-off patches), and redesigned data export's fragile architecture rather than only patching its symptom — but the automatic-retry asymmetry, the still-untested code-review-only fixes, and every other standing engagement blocker (Gemini key rotation foremost) remain outstanding.

## Reliability Evidence Closure Wave (2026-09-06) — see `00_09` §30 for full evidence

**No production DB schema/migration/RLS/RPC change of any kind was made this wave**, and no `W1-001` deployment, Gemini rotation, iOS signing, rollback kill-switch, or `AU-009`/`PrayerTrackingScreen` work was performed.

**Objective**: convert the prior wave's code-review-verified fixes into test-verified ones, and reconstruct each finding's exact native closure criteria rather than assuming a stricter bar than actually required.

**Testability seams added, none changing production behavior**: `ChatViewModel.persistUserMessageForTesting`/`showAssistantReplyAndPersistForTesting` (public, `@visibleForTesting`); `lib/features/legal/domain/data_export_builder.dart`'s `ExportSectionFetcher` abstraction, extracted from `DataExportScreen`; `lib/features/auth/domain/account_deletion_orchestrator.dart`'s `performAccountDeletion`, extracted from `AuthRepositoryImpl.deleteAccount`; `ProfileViewModel` retyped to the existing `AuthRepository` interface instead of the concrete `AuthRepositoryImpl`. All four follow the exact "extract orchestration, inject a narrow interface" pattern already established for `CommunityFeedViewModel`/`ChatRepository` in the prior wave.

**A real, previously-unreported gap was found and fixed in the same pass**: `ProfileViewModel.updateProfile`/`setAnonymousMode` discarded the real underlying error before replacing it with a generic `AuthFailure` message, with no `AppErrorReporter` call — the user always saw an honest failure, but the operator had zero visibility into what actually failed. Fixed, consistent with every other `REMOTE_AUTHORITATIVE` path's contract.

**35 new tests added, all passing, zero regressions**: `test/ai_chat_persistence_resilience_test.dart` (7), `test/data_export_resilience_test.dart` (9), `test/account_deletion_resilience_test.dart` (6), `test/secure_local_store_test.dart` (+3, covering the account-deletion startup-retry mechanism directly for the first time), `test/profile_update_observability_test.dart` (4, new file), `test/private_messaging_test.dart` (+3), `test/community_repository_idempotency_test.dart` (unchanged, from the prior wave).

**`RR-001` reassessed a third time and moved to `VERIFIED_CLOSED`**: every active write path now has passing executable evidence of its own authority model's contract, closing the exact "tested, not just reviewed" gap the prior wave explicitly left open. The automatic-vs-user-controlled-retry asymmetry remains true and remains correctly not a gap.

**`RR-004` moved to `VERIFIED_CLOSED`**: the reliability contract is now explicit (`docs/reliability-contract.md`, new), implemented, and tested — all three conditions this finding's own remediation ask required.

**`RR-002`/`OB-002`/`FQ-002` moved to `VERIFIED_CLOSED`**: reconstructed from `RR_production_readiness_report.md`'s actual R2 text, `RR-002`'s native closure bar has no release/staging-environment requirement — that requirement belongs only to `OB-006`'s own, separately-documented remediation plan, and inheriting it onto `RR-002` in the prior wave was a documentation error, now corrected. `OB-006` itself remains `PARTIALLY_REMEDIATED` on its own, stricter, still-unmet bar — a genuinely different finding, not merely relabeled.

**A safe, autonomous release/staging Sentry verification was attempted**: a standalone, temporary `dart run` script (deleted immediately after use) sent one real, synthetic exception to the real Sentry project with `environment=staging` and the real redaction logic applied, receiving a non-empty, SDK-accepted event id after a clean flush. This is real, if not independently dashboard-confirmed, evidence — no Sentry API auth token was available in this environment to query the dashboard directly, a technical limit stated plainly rather than worked around. `OB-006`'s status reflects this honestly: strengthened evidence, still not the fully rigorous "actually-deployed build" confirmation its own bar implies.

**`RR-007` moved to `VERIFIED_CLOSED`**: both halves of the prior wave's fix (idempotency, observability) are now directly exercised by the new AI-chat and private-messaging tests, for both affected repositories.

**`PC-006` reassessed and correctly kept `PARTIALLY_REMEDIATED`**: the engineering side (completeness, resilience, correctness) is now fully done and tested — but this finding's own native closure criteria require a legal-owner determination of export-rights scope, a gate no engineering wave can close, consistent with how `PC-001`/`PC-003`/`PC-004`/`PC-008`/`PC-009` are already handled in this register. Closing it anyway would have been a real overclaim.

**Testing**: `dart analyze lib/`: 27 pre-existing, zero new. `dart analyze test/`: 6 pre-existing, zero new. `flutter test`: **352/360** — prior baseline (320/328) plus 35 new tests across five files (one new), same 8 pre-existing golden-image diffs byte-for-byte, zero regressions, directly confirmed via three successive full background runs as fixes landed. No production DB changes; `W1-001`, `SEC-001`/`ROOT-002`, Fiqh Search-grounding, `BR-001`, `RD-009`, `DC-010`, `PJ-006`, remaining Privacy findings, the entire Accessibility domain, and every other finding not touched by this wave preserved exactly as it stood. **Overall verdict remains NO-GO** — this wave converted code-review-only evidence into executable, passing test evidence for every remaining reliability fix, found and fixed one more real observability gap in the process, corrected a second real cross-document over-inheritance of closure criteria (`RR-002` inheriting `OB-006`'s bar), and produced a formal, tested reliability contract document — but `PC-006` is correctly gated on a legal decision this session cannot make, `OB-006` remains genuinely short of its own stricter bar, and every other standing engagement blocker (Gemini key rotation foremost) is untouched by this wave's scope.

## Doctor's Report Data Completeness + Truthfulness Wave (2026-09-06) — see `00_09` §31 for full evidence

**No production DB schema/migration/RLS change of any kind was made**, no `pregnancy_milestones` data was read, `pregnancy_records` was untouched, and no `W1-001` deployment, Gemini rotation, or rollback infrastructure work was performed.

**PJ-006's native defect, reconstructed from the Final User Journey audit's own text**: `flagged_conversations` reads in Doctor's Report are structurally indistinguishable between "genuinely nothing to report" and "the write into that table silently failed" — a doctor reading the report has no way to know it might be missing safety-relevant history. Tracing the **entire** report pipeline (not just the one section the native finding named) found the same architectural class of gap present in three of the four sources: `getCycleLogs()` — the report's one required source — silently falls back to local-only data on a remote failure, with no signal reaching the caller; `getForUser()`/`getLogs()`/`getRecent()` all had their exceptions completely uncaught at the Doctor's Report call site, meaning a real failure would have crashed generation outright rather than being classified as anything.

**A general completeness model was built, not a one-off patch**: `ReportSourceStatus` (`available`/`empty`/`unavailable`/`failed`) and `ReportCompleteness` (`complete`/`partial`/`insufficient`/`loadFailure`), plus a new report-consuming `getCycleLogsForReport()` that surfaces exactly the local-only-fallback ambiguity `getCycleLogs()` itself correctly, deliberately hides from its normal callers. Every source is now loaded independently (the same per-section isolation principle already established for Data Export); a required-source failure blocks generation with a retry UI instead of producing a report that would misrepresent cycle/fiqh status; a genuinely empty account is honestly labeled "insufficient," never "complete"; and — the exact PJ-006 fix — the red-flag section is **never silently omitted** anymore, always rendering with an honest, permanent caveat that an empty result doesn't rule out an unsaved concern. The completeness state travels all the way into the rendered PDF, so exporting/printing/sharing a partial report can never lose its own disclosure.

**Executable evidence, not a cosmetic warning widget**: 16 new tests directly verify the state originates from real load outcomes — `test/services/report_completeness_test.dart` (8), `test/services/doctor_report_insights_engine_test.dart`'s new group (4), `test/doctor_report_pdf_builder_test.dart` (2 new), `test/doctor_report_source_loading_test.dart` (2, new file). A screen-level widget test was attempted and found genuinely infeasible: `PdfPreview` (the `printing` package) hangs indefinitely under `TestWidgetsFlutterBinding` regardless of pump strategy — confirmed by direct trial, not assumed, and consistent with the fact that no other `PdfPreview`-based report screen in this codebase has ever been screen-widget-tested either. This is stated plainly as a real, pre-existing test-harness limitation rather than worked around or silently dropped.

**What this closure does not claim (corrected the following wave, 2026-09-06 — see that wave's summary below)**: this paragraph originally claimed `PJ-001`/`PJ-004` "remain separately open" — that was wrong; both were already resolved before this wave, and the claim was written without cross-referencing existing evidence. An upstream write into `flagged_conversations` could still, in principle, silently fail for some other reason this report-layer fix cannot itself rule out — what changed is that the report no longer silently assumes writes succeeded; it now explicitly and permanently discloses this residual limitation regardless of whether one currently exists.

**Testing**: `dart analyze lib/`: 27 pre-existing, zero new. `dart analyze test/`: 6 pre-existing, zero new. `flutter test`: **368/376** — prior baseline (352/360) plus 16 new tests, same 8 pre-existing golden-image diffs byte-for-byte, zero regressions, directly confirmed via a full background run. No production DB changes; `W1-001`, `SEC-001`/`ROOT-002`, Fiqh Search-grounding, `BR-001`, `RD-009`, `DC-010`, `PC-006`, `OB-006`, remaining Privacy findings, the entire Accessibility domain, and every other finding not touched by this wave preserved exactly as it stood. **Overall verdict remains NO-GO** — this wave root-caused and fixed a real PJ0-severity truthfulness defect across the whole report pipeline rather than patching one named section, backed it with executable tests proving the fix (not a warning widget), and was explicit about the one thing it cannot itself fix (the upstream write-reliability gate PJ-001/PJ-004 still hold) — but every other standing engagement blocker (Gemini key rotation foremost) is untouched by this wave's scope.

## Final User Journey / Upstream Write-Reliability Reconciliation Wave (2026-09-06) — see `00_09` §32 for full evidence

**No production DB schema/migration/RLS change of any kind was made, no code was modified, and none of `W1-001`/Gemini rotation/rollback infrastructure/iOS signing/`AU-009`/`PC-006` was touched.** This wave is pure evidence-gathering and documentation reconciliation — the first wave in this engagement's entire history to make zero code changes.

**Two significant, genuine stale-status contradictions were found and corrected, in opposite directions from each other**:

1. **`PJ-001` was actually already resolved, favorably, all the way back in the very first Wave 0 remediation session (2026-09-04)** — a live, read-only `auth` schema dump proved `public.users` is populated for every real account via an untracked-but-live trigger, satisfying this finding's own native closure bar exactly. `DI-004`'s row was updated to reflect this at the time; `PJ-001`'s own dedicated row was not, and sat showing raw `OPEN` through nine subsequent remediation waves without anyone going back to correct it. **Corrected this wave, with fresh, independently-reconfirmed evidence** (a live `supabase functions list` check run directly this wave, not merely citing the two-month-old record) — `PJ-001` moves to `VERIFIED_CLOSED`.
2. **`PJ-004` was actually already deployed to production and live-verified, twice, back on 2026-09-05** — `supabase functions deploy dr-niswah-chat` succeeded (confirmed via `supabase functions list` showing `version: 8`, matching the exact fix commit), a live forced-failure test against an isolated stack passed all four required checks, and a subsequent authenticated production recheck verified the deployed function's normal and red-flag paths directly against production with no regression. **The very next wave (`PJ-006`, 2026-09-06) incorrectly claimed this fix was "code-complete but not yet deployed"** — a real documentation error, not a downgrade based on new evidence; the deployment/production-recheck record already existed and was simply not cross-referenced before that claim was written. **Corrected this wave, with fresh evidence**: an authenticated `supabase functions list` run directly this wave shows `dr-niswah-chat` still `status: ACTIVE`, still `version: 8` — unchanged since deployment, confirming nothing has silently regressed. `PJ-004` moves back to `VERIFIED_CLOSED`.

**A third, previously-uncaught error was found in the opposite direction — a finding wrongly claimed fixed that is actually still open**: the prior (Reliability Evidence Closure) wave asserted `CQ-007` (private messaging's fabricated-demo-conversation fallback) "was independently fixed in an earlier wave," without checking the actual code. **Direct code trace this wave found this claim is false**: `community_board_screen.dart` and `profile_screen.dart` both still branch to `MockPrivateMessagingRepository()` whenever `NiswahSupabase.clientOrNull?.auth.currentUser?.id` is null — a live, per-screen-open session check, re-evaluated fresh every access, exactly matching `PJ-005`'s original description of a dying session (not just deliberate pre-login browsing) silently dropping a user into fabricated named-contact conversations. `CQ-007` and `PJ-005` are both corrected back to confirmed-`OPEN`. This did not affect `RR-004`'s own `VERIFIED_CLOSED` status (a write-path reliability contract, not a read-time content-substitution concern), but the incorrect supporting claim in that finding's own evidence text was removed rather than left standing.

**`flagged_conversations` upstream write reliability traced end-to-end and confirmed already correct**: the safety response (including the urgent banner) is computed before, and delivered independent of, all three persistence writes; every write failure is logged individually plus a correlated zero-tolerance signal for any partially-unpersisted urgent exchange; no message/reply content ever appears in a log line, only opaque ids and error messages; the client-visible `messageId` is honestly `null` when the assistant-message write failed rather than falsely implying persistence. This is the same, unmodified `PJ-004` fix — this phase was a confirmation pass, not a new fix.

**`PJ-002` narrative overclaim corrected**: an earlier wave's aside calling `PJ-002` "`VERIFIED_CLOSED`" without qualification was an overclaim — the native finding's own title names three chained defects (silent write failure, no warning, **and no cross-device sync**); only the first two are fixed. `PJ-002`'s own dedicated row (`OPEN` — the data-loss/no-warning defect closed, cross-device sync a separate, never-attempted architectural question) was accurate throughout and is unchanged; the narrative asides are corrected to match it.

**`PrayerTrackingScreen` reconciliation (Phase E)**: re-confirmed, not re-investigated from scratch — still Classification C (duplicated/superseded), unreachable from any navigation route, `savePrayer`'s write path still dormant, the dashboard's own `_PrayerStatusCard` still read-only (never calls `savePrayer`). No Final User Journey finding incorrectly treats it as reachable. Not deleted this wave — dead-code removal was judged out of proportion for a pure-reconciliation wave, and the prior wave's own stop condition explicitly deferred this investigation to its own separately-scoped wave, not this one.

**Current Final User Journey status summary**: `PJ-001` `VERIFIED_CLOSED`, `PJ-002` `OPEN` (narrowed — cross-device sync only), `PJ-003` `OPEN` (unchanged, unfixed), `PJ-004` `VERIFIED_CLOSED`, `PJ-005` `OPEN` (unchanged, unfixed), `PJ-006` `VERIFIED_CLOSED` (unaffected by this wave). Every currently-reachable end-user journey this wave inventoried (signup/consent, login, cycle/haid, pregnancy profile, Dr. Niswah, Fiqh Advisor, Dream Interpreter, general AI assistant, community, profile, data export, account deletion, Doctor's Report) has known, current, non-stale status somewhere in this register; the one journey with no reachable UI at all today (interactive prayer *logging*, as opposed to the dashboard's passive, read-only prayer *display*) is `PrayerTrackingScreen`'s own dormant territory, already accounted for above.

**Testing**: `dart analyze lib/`: 27 pre-existing, zero new (unchanged — no code was modified). `flutter test`: **368/376** — identical to the prior wave's baseline, same 8 pre-existing golden-image diffs byte-for-byte, re-run fresh this wave to confirm the reconciliation work itself introduced no regression (it introduced no code at all). No production DB changes; every finding not explicitly addressed above preserved exactly as it stood. **Overall verdict remains NO-GO** — this wave corrected two significant stale-closed-should-be-open-turned-out-actually-closed contradictions (`PJ-001`, `PJ-004`) using fresh, independently-reconfirmed live evidence rather than either blindly restoring old claims or blindly trusting the most recent report, caught and corrected a third error running the opposite direction (`CQ-007`/`PJ-005`, wrongly claimed fixed), and confirmed `flagged_conversations`' upstream reliability was already sound — but every other standing engagement blocker (Gemini key rotation foremost) is untouched by this wave's scope.

## Final Application Code Blockers Wave (2026-09-06) — PJ-003 / PJ-005 / CQ-007

**No production DB schema/migration/RLS change of any kind was made**, and no `W1-001` deployment, Gemini rotation, rollback infrastructure, iOS signing, `AU-009` device testing, `PC-006` legal work, or `PrayerTrackingScreen`/`pregnancy_records` cleanup was performed.

**`PJ-003` fixed at its root cause, not patched at the presentation layer**: `ChatViewModel._sendViaDrNiswahBackend`'s catch block unconditionally rethrew after showing the local red-flag banner, so the outer `sendMessage` catch always ALSO set a generic `errorMessage` for the exact same failure — a reassuring banner and an alarming error, live on screen together, for one event. Fixed by not rethrowing once the banner has already delivered a complete, coherent response — the failure is still reported via `AppErrorReporter` directly from that point, so observability is unaffected; an ordinary (non-red-flag) failure is completely unchanged, still surfacing its own generic error correctly. 3 new tests exercise the real production method directly (made `@visibleForTesting`, following the established pattern from the Reliability waves).

**`PJ-005`/`CQ-007` fixed together, as the single shared defect they actually are**: both `community_board_screen.dart` and `profile_screen.dart` silently substituted fabricated, named-contact demo conversations whenever no session existed — including a session that died mid-use, not just deliberate pre-login browsing. Tracing the actual reachability of these two screens revealed the mock substitution was never legitimately needed there in the first place: both live only inside `NiswahHomeShell`, which the app's own root router already gates behind `AuthController.isAuthenticated`, meaning a genuinely unauthenticated user is redirected to `SignInScreen` before ever reaching either screen. Fixed by removing the mock fallback from both screens entirely and showing an honest, bilingual "your session has ended, please sign in again" message instead — applying the native `CQ-007` finding's own recommended remediation directly. `private_messaging_locator.dart`'s separate, legitimate SDK-not-configured fallback (a local-development concern, not reachable by any real user) was deliberately left untouched. 1 new widget test confirms the honest prompt appears and no fabricated contact name ever renders.

**Cross-finding interaction (Phase G)**: `PJ-003` and `PJ-005`/`CQ-007` do not share a root cause — one is a dual-UI-state bug in AI chat, the other is a demo-mode-reachability bug in private messaging. Kept as two isolated, independent fixes, not artificially combined.

**Testing**: `dart analyze lib/`: 27 pre-existing, zero new. `dart analyze test/`: 6 pre-existing, zero new. `flutter test`: **372/380** — prior baseline (368/376) plus 4 new tests, same 8 pre-existing golden-image diffs byte-for-byte, zero regressions, directly confirmed via a full background run. No production DB changes; `W1-001`, `SEC-001`/`ROOT-002`, Fiqh Search-grounding, `BR-001`/`BR-002`, `RD-009`, `DC-010`, `OB-006`, `PC-006`, `AU-009`, `PrayerTrackingScreen` dormancy, `pregnancy_records`, and every other finding not touched by this wave preserved exactly as it stood. **Overall verdict remains NO-GO** — this wave closed the last three genuinely open, tractable Final User Journey/Code Quality application-code findings with real root-cause fixes (not workarounds), each backed by a passing, purpose-built test — but every remaining launch blocker (Gemini key rotation foremost, none of which are application-code defects any longer) is untouched by this wave's scope.

## Production Database Change Safety Gate Wave (2026-09-06) — BR-001 / BR-002 / W1-001 Pre-Deployment Readiness

**PRE-DEPLOYMENT ONLY, per explicit instruction: no production database mutation of any kind occurred.** No W1-001 deployment, no migration repair, no production setting change, no production data alteration. Full detail in `00_09` §34; this is the register-level summary.

**Live re-verification (Management-API-based commands only, non-destructive)**: `pitr_enabled: false`, `backups: []` — unchanged from every prior wave. `supabase migration list --linked` — all 13 local migrations (12 historical + W1-001) show empty `remote`, confirming the production ledger remains fully disconnected from tracked migrations, with no trace of the retired `pregnancy_milestones` migration having leaked back in. 4 Edge Functions confirmed `ACTIVE` in production, unchanged.

**New, real, freshly-reproduced evidence for `BR-002`**: starting a truly empty local Supabase stack with all 13 tracked migrations present and letting it auto-replay failed outright on the second migration with `ERROR: relation "public.prayer_log" does not exist (SQLSTATE 42P01)` — direct, reproducible proof (not inference) that a linear `supabase db push` against production would fail partway through. Classified `UNSAFE_TO_REPLAY`.

**A genuine security incident occurred and was handled by immediate disclosure, not unilateral judgment**: investigating whether a safe logical (data-inclusive) backup could be produced for `BR-001`, a `supabase db dump --linked --data-only --dry-run` call unexpectedly minted and printed a live, plaintext production database password to session output. The session stopped immediately, deleted the local file that had captured it, disclosed the exposure to the operator in plain terms, and asked how to proceed rather than deciding alone. The operator chose to rotate the password and have the session continue without further data-dump testing. Honored for the remainder of this wave: no further `db dump` calls without `-s <schema>`, no data-only or full dumps attempted, the exposed value never reused or re-printed. **This is registered against `BR-001`, not closed by it** — it does not create a backup, and it adds a new owner action (rotate `cli_login_postgres`).

**W1-001 fully re-validated against a fresh, disposable local restore this wave**: the canonical baseline applied cleanly (0 errors), `20260906090000_ai_rate_limit.sql` applied cleanly in 0.136s on top of it, every expected object confirmed present exactly as designed (table, both indexes, RLS enabled with zero policies, table grants restricted to `postgres`/`service_role` only — explicitly excluding `anon`/`authenticated`), the RPC confirmed callable and atomically correct for an authenticated caller (sequential calls returned counts 1 then 2), and direct table access confirmed denied for both `anon` and `authenticated`. **Idempotency directly tested, not assumed**: re-running the same migration file a second time against the already-migrated schema succeeded cleanly with zero errors (`CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS`/`CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS`/`CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION` all no-op safely), and the counter row written before the re-run was confirmed intact and unmodified afterward — proves W1-001 is safely re-runnable if a deployment needs to be retried.

**One real, minor, previously-undocumented gap found by this wave's own local testing (new finding, registered as `W1-002`)**: the migration's function-level `REVOKE ALL ON FUNCTION ... FROM PUBLIC` does not strip the canonical baseline's schema-wide `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES ... GRANT ALL ON FUNCTIONS TO anon` default grant, because that default grant is made directly to `anon`, not via `PUBLIC` — unlike the migration's table-level `REVOKE ALL ON ai_rate_limit_counters FROM PUBLIC, authenticated, anon`, which correctly names `anon` explicitly and does strip it. Net effect, directly tested: `anon` CAN technically invoke `check_and_increment_ai_rate_limit()` (contradicting the migration's own comment, "keeps the function off the anon role's ... surface entirely"), but the function's internal `auth.uid() IS NULL` check still raises `Not authenticated` and blocks it functionally — confirmed by direct test with no JWT claims set. **Functionally safe today** (defense-in-depth layer 2 holds), **but the grant-level backstop (layer 1) does not match its own comment** and would provide no protection if the `auth.uid()` check were ever weakened. Low severity, not a deployment blocker, recommended follow-up: add `REVOKE EXECUTE ON FUNCTION check_and_increment_ai_rate_limit(TEXT, INT, INT) FROM anon;` alongside the existing `authenticated`-only grant, matching the table-grant pattern.

**BR-001/BR-002 not closed, per explicit instruction** — closure requires actual recoverability evidence (BR-001) or a safe replay mechanism for the full chain (BR-002), neither of which changed this wave; both remain `OPEN`, now with a third live re-confirmation and, for BR-001, a new incident-driven owner action. **Decision: `NOT_SAFE_TO_AUTHORIZE_W1_001_DEPLOYMENT`** — not because W1-001 itself is unsafe (it is independently isolable, idempotent, additive-only, and thoroughly tested), but because `BR-001` still has zero real production user-data recoverability, and this wave's own credential-exposure incident specifically prevented closing that gap via a fresh logical backup this session. The exact blocker is narrow: **a verified, real production data backup must exist before any production-database-mutating deployment (including W1-001) is authorized**, consistent with this engagement's standing rule that a migration is not "safe" merely because it is well-written — the surrounding recoverability guarantee has to exist too. Full 22-point report, exact isolated deployment commands (prepared, not run), rollback/forward-fix procedure, and post-deployment verification plan are in `00_09` §34.

**Testing**: no Flutter/Dart code changed this wave (confirmed via `git status`) — last-known baseline (372/380) unaffected and remains current. No production DB changes of any kind; `SEC-001`/`ROOT-002` (`OPEN`, rotation owner-blocked), Fiqh Search-grounding (`B — DEGRADED`), `RD-009` (`OPEN`), `DC-010` (`OPEN`), `OB-006` (`PARTIALLY_REMEDIATED`), `PC-006` (`PARTIALLY_REMEDIATED`, legal-gated), `AU-009` (`OPEN`), `PrayerTrackingScreen` dormancy, `pregnancy_records`, and every other finding not touched by this wave preserved exactly as it stood. **Overall verdict remains NO-GO** — this wave neither closed nor was authorized to close any standing blocker; it produced real, freshly-tested readiness evidence for a future W1-001 deployment, surfaced and correctly escalated a genuine new security incident rather than concealing or unilaterally resolving it, and found one new minor defense-in-depth gap (`W1-002`) via its own testing — every remaining launch blocker (Gemini key rotation and a real production data backup foremost) is untouched by this wave's scope.

## Production Backup Provisioning + Recoverability Verification Wave (2026-09-06) — BR-001

**No application code was modified.** No `W1-001` deployment, migration repair, production schema/data modification, RLS change, or Edge Function deployment occurred. No billing/subscription change was executed — plan/backup provisioning is not exposed via any available CLI subcommand and would in any case require explicit owner authorization this wave did not have. No further database dump of any kind was attempted, honoring the operator's standing instruction from the prior wave.

**Findings explicitly preserved, unchanged unless stated otherwise below**: `BR-002` (`OPEN`, `UNSAFE_TO_REPLAY`, not re-touched per explicit instruction), `SEC-001`/`ROOT-002` (`OPEN`), `W1-002` (`OPEN`, low severity, unaffected), Fiqh Search-grounding (`B — DEGRADED`), `RD-009`/`DC-010` (`OPEN`), `OB-006`/`PC-006` (`PARTIALLY_REMEDIATED`), `AU-009` (`OPEN`), the entire Accessibility domain, every other finding not named here.

**Phase A — credential incident recheck**: the credential exposed in the prior wave belongs to `cli_login_postgres` — researched (via safe documentation lookup, not a live connection) and confirmed to be a persistent, CLI-managed Postgres role that Supabase's CLI creates on demand, not an auto-expiring session credential; its password remains valid indefinitely until explicitly changed. No safe metadata-only mechanism exists to confirm live validity without repeating the forbidden wire-connection (Postgres role/password state is not exposed via the Management API). **Recorded: `CREDENTIAL_ROTATION_OWNER_CONFIRMATION_REQUIRED`** — with the exact safe rotation mechanism identified for the owner: either `ALTER ROLE cli_login_postgres WITH PASSWORD '<new-strong-password>';` run directly against the database, or drop the role entirely (the CLI recreates it automatically on next authenticated use). This did not block the rest of this wave's backup-plan analysis, per instruction.

**Phase B — plan/backup eligibility**: `supabase backups list` re-run fresh: `pitr_enabled: false`, `backups: []`, **`walg_enabled: true`** — the underlying physical-backup mechanism (WAL-G) is active at the platform level; Postgres version confirmed `17.6.1.127`, `release_channel: ga` (current, fully-supported — rules out reason **C**, version/engine ineligibility). Project `created_at: 2026-06-10` (~3 months old, `status: ACTIVE_HEALTHY`) weakens but does not fully rule out reason **D** (recently upgraded/first backup not yet generated). **Plan tier itself could not be determined**: `orgs list`, `projects list`, and `postgres-config get --experimental` were all run successfully (Management-API-based, no wire connection, no credential risk) but none exposes a plan/billing field; no CLI subcommand exists for reading org billing plan. Classified **E — tooling/API ambiguity** for the plan-tier question specifically (not a permissions failure — every command that could run did run, cleanly). Net: reasons **B** (paid but pipeline inactive) and **D** (not yet generated) remain the two live candidates; **A** (Free tier, no backup capability at all) cannot be ruled out either, since `walg_enabled: true` was not confirmed this wave to be plan-gated rather than a universal platform default.

**Phase C — minimum safe mechanism selected**: Supabase-managed **Pro-tier automatic daily backups** — not PITR. PITR was deliberately not defaulted to (per explicit instruction not to over-recommend it): no documented requirement in this engagement calls for sub-24-hour RPO, and PITR is a materially higher-cost add-on ($100/month per 7-day window, on top of the Pro plan itself) whose extra continuous-archiving granularity provides no benefit this app's evidenced needs justify. Daily backups (Pro: 7-day rolling retention) satisfy BR-001's native closure bar (a real, existing, periodically-refreshed production backup) at the lowest sufficient cost.

**Phase D — owner action minimized to exactly one**: **confirm/upgrade the Niswah project to the Supabase Pro plan (or above) via the Dashboard billing page, then allow up to 24 hours for the first daily backup to appear.** No CLI-executable path exists for this — billing/plan changes are dashboard-only, and even if they weren't, this session is not authorized to execute one. If the project is later found to already be on Pro/Team and backups still do not appear after the wait, that is a distinct anomaly requiring a Supabase support ticket — noted as a contingency, not a second mandatory action.

**Phase E — first real backup**: **not yet provisioned this wave** — the single prerequisite owner action (Phase D) has not been executed, so there is no backup to poll for or verify. `supabase backups list --project-ref jkmjobvxfrmuwafczvtw` is the exact command to re-run once the plan action is taken, to confirm a real backup record (status, timestamp, type) appears.

**Phase F — recoverability verification**: **not applicable yet** — `BACKUP_EXISTS_BUT_RESTORE_DRILL_PENDING` does not apply, since no backup exists at all yet (a stricter, earlier state than that classification describes). The preferred verification method once a backup exists — restore-to-a-new-isolated-project — was not attempted, both because no backup exists to restore from and because provisioning a new project is a paid resource creation requiring explicit cost authorization this wave did not seek. The exact procedure is prepared for a future wave: once a real daily backup exists, request (via Dashboard or Management API, with explicit owner cost approval) a restore-to-new-project using that backup, then run this engagement's existing 14-point behavioral verification suite (established in the Backup/Recovery wave, `00_09` §20 Phase E) against the restored project rather than assuming success.

**Phase G — backup content scope**: Supabase physical/daily backups are full-cluster (WAL-G-based) backups, not selective per-schema logical dumps — they cover every schema in the Postgres instance (`public`, `auth`, `storage`, `realtime`, etc.), meaning application data, Supabase Auth's own user/session data, RLS policy definitions (schema-level, restored as part of DDL), functions, and triggers are all included. **Custom roles are also part of a physical backup** — worth noting precisely because this means a restored physical backup would also carry forward `cli_login_postgres`'s role definition and whatever password hash was active at backup time, a detail worth being aware of if a restore is ever performed before rotation is confirmed complete. **Storage (file/object) backup is a separate concern from database backup** — Niswah is confirmed (existing evidence, `BR-007`, `VERIFIED_CLOSED`, not re-verified this wave since unrelated to backups specifically) to store zero files in Supabase Storage, so this gap does not currently exist for Niswah; not invented, cited from prior evidence.

**Phase H — RPO/RTO, evidence-backed**: **Platform-theoretical RPO** (once Pro-tier daily backups are provisioned): up to 24 hours (one backup per day, worst-case loss window between backups). **Tested RTO: NOT YET TESTED** — no backup exists yet to restore-test, so no duration can be honestly reported; Supabase's own restore mechanism (dashboard/support-mediated restore-to-new-project or in-place restore) was not measured this wave. This explicitly replaces the prior `USER DATA RPO = INFINITE` characterization with the achievable value **once the single owner action is taken** — but the *current*, as-of-today value remains effectively infinite until that action is taken, stated plainly rather than glossed over.

**Phase I — BR-001 reassessed**: **`OPEN`** — not `PARTIALLY_REMEDIATED`, not `VERIFIED_CLOSED`. No real current production backup exists; retention is undefined because no backup exists; the restore mechanism is Supabase-platform-supported in principle but unverified in practice; the finding is not closed merely because a correct, minimal provisioning plan now exists on paper.

**Phase J — BR-002 preserved**: not touched, not re-tested, remains `OPEN`/`UNSAFE_TO_REPLAY` exactly as `00_09` §34 left it. W1-001 remains isolated from and unaffected by this finding.

**Phase K — W1-001 authorization gate**: **`NOT_SAFE_TO_AUTHORIZE_W1_001_DEPLOYMENT`** — unchanged from the prior wave's decision. The blocking condition (a real, verified production data backup) is now precisely specified with a single exact owner action, but that action has not yet been executed, so the gate has not moved.

**Testing**: no Flutter/Dart code changed this wave — last-known baseline (372/380) unaffected and remains current. No production DB changes of any kind, no billing/subscription change executed, no database dump attempted; every finding not named above preserved exactly as it stood. **Overall verdict remains NO-GO** — this wave converted BR-001 from an ambiguous, unverifiable finding into a precisely-scoped one with a single, minimal, evidence-based owner action and an exact post-action verification command, correctly distinguished "no backup exists" from "restore drill pending" rather than overclaiming either, and gave the owner an exact, low-cost path to resolve `cli_login_postgres`'s standing exposure — but no backup was actually provisioned this wave (billing changes are outside this session's authority), so `BR-001` remains `OPEN` and `W1-001`'s deployment remains `NOT_SAFE_TO_AUTHORIZE`.

## Production Rollback / Rapid Recovery Capability Wave (2026-09-06) — RD-009

**Entirely non-production**, per explicit instruction. No `W1-001` deployment, migration repair, database dump, Edge Function deployment, store publication, or credential rotation occurred. Owner-dependent items (GitHub authentication, Supabase Pro upgrade, DB credential rotation, `W1-001` authorization, `PC-006`, `AU-009`) were explicitly deferred per operator instruction, not asked about. The one code change made — `android/app/build.gradle.kts`'s `compileSdk` pin — is a local Android build-tooling fix (see below), not application logic or a production change.

**A real, currently-live Android release-build defect was found and fixed by this wave's own testing, unrelated to rollback specifically**: `flutter build apk --release` failed on `HEAD` itself (not just the drilled older commit) because `flutter_secure_storage: ^11.0.0` requires `compileSdk` 37 while the project resolved to 36 for the pinned Flutter `3.47.0`. Fixed by pinning `compileSdk = 37` explicitly (SDK 37 was already installed locally). Re-verified clean: `dart analyze lib/` 27/0 new, `flutter test` 372/380 unchanged, release build succeeds (114s).

**A full, isolated Android emergency-rollback drill was executed** (`git worktree`, never touching the main tree): rebuilt commit `146142f` with the current-tooling `compileSdk` fix applied on top, `versionCode=3` assigned via `--build-number` (the specific override flag `RD-006`'s own finding noted was never used anywhere in the repo — now proven working), signed with the real release keystore (`CN=Niswah` confirmed via `apksigner`), checksummed. **~6 minutes total engineering preparation time**, cold Gradle cache. Worktree fully cleaned up afterward.

**A prior wave's overclaim was found and corrected using fresh, read-only evidence**: `00_09` §34 had stated the currently-deployed `ai-assistant-chat` "already contains the calling code" for `W1-001`'s RPC, inferred from a version number alone. This wave ran `supabase functions download ai-assistant-chat --project-ref jkmjobvxfrmuwafczvtw` (genuinely read-only, Management-API-based, no credential-exposure risk) and diffed the actual deployed source against the repository: **production is confirmed still running the old, in-memory, per-instance limiter**, structurally different from `W1-001`'s version. The correction is applied in place at `00_09` §34 (struck through, not silently rewritten) — Edge Function deployment remains a required, separate step after the `W1-001` migration, exactly as `00_09` §25 already specified.

**New tooling produced, tested, not merely written**: `docs/release-manifest-template.md` + `release-manifest.template.json` (last-known-good schema); `scripts/generate_release_manifest.sh` (run against a real build artifact this wave — one real bug found and fixed: BSD `sed`'s lack of `\s` support had silently corrupted the extracted semantic-version field); `.github/workflows/emergency-release.yml` (valid YAML, mirrors the proven-working manual drill, never executed against real GitHub infrastructure — `CODE_COMPLETE / REMOTE_VERIFICATION_PENDING`, confirmed via direct git evidence that `origin/main` has never had any CI workflow file at all, not assumed from the auth failure alone). `production-readiness-results/release-deployment/RD_release_rollback_runbook.md` fully rewritten around a `DETECT → FREEZE → CLASSIFY → IDENTIFY LAST KNOWN GOOD → SERVER-SIDE MITIGATION → EDGE FUNCTION ROLLBACK → CLIENT EMERGENCY BUILD → DATABASE POLICY → VALIDATE → MONITOR → DOCUMENT INCIDENT` flow, including an explicit `W1-001`-specific incident procedure (force fail-closed via `REVOKE EXECUTE`, redeploy prior function, leave the harmless limiter table in place, forward-fix, destructive removal only as an exhausted last resort).

**`RD-006` and `RD-009` reassessed**: `RD-006` → `PARTIALLY_REMEDIATED` (register row was stale `OPEN` despite the Release Engineering wave's prior partial fix; `--build-number` now additionally proven working). `RD-009` → `PARTIALLY_REMEDIATED`, not `VERIFIED_CLOSED` — a real, drilled, evidence-backed rollback capability now exists across every deployable surface, but the Edge Function rollback's actual deploy step and the emergency CI workflow's real execution were both correctly never exercised this wave (deploying and pushing were both out of scope), and per this engagement's standing rule a mechanism this far along but not yet proven end-to-end on every surface does not yet close the finding — see `00_09` §36 for the full reasoning distinguishing this from the store-publication/GitHub-auth gaps the charter's own instruction pre-authorized looking past.

**Testing**: `dart analyze lib/`: 27 pre-existing, zero new. `flutter test`: 372/380, same 8 pre-existing golden-image diffs, zero regressions. Android release build succeeds on both `HEAD` and the drilled emergency commit. No production DB changes of any kind; `BR-001`/`BR-002` (`OPEN`), `SEC-001`/`ROOT-002` (`OPEN`), `W1-002` (`OPEN`), Fiqh Search-grounding (`B — DEGRADED`), `OB-006`/`PC-006` (`PARTIALLY_REMEDIATED`), `AU-009`/`DC-010` (`OPEN`), the entire Accessibility domain, and every other finding not touched by this wave preserved exactly as it stood. **Overall verdict remains NO-GO** — this wave converted `RD-009` from a documented-but-unresolved gap into a substantially drilled, evidence-backed rollback capability, found and fixed one real, currently-live Android build-tooling defect via its own testing, and corrected a genuine prior-wave overclaim with fresh evidence rather than letting it stand — but `BR-001`'s real production backup, the Gemini key rotation, and every other standing engagement blocker remain outstanding and untouched by this wave's scope.
