## Migration Chain Reproducibility Wave (2026-09-06) — BR-002

**Local/repository-only, per explicit instruction.** No production DB modification, no `W1-001` deployment, no migration repair against production, no production dump, no Edge Function deployment, no credential rotation, no PITR/backup change, no GitHub authentication fix, no `AU-009`/iOS/`PC-006` work.

**Root cause precisely identified this wave, not merely re-confirmed as "broken"**: a full concatenated replay of all 13 tracked migrations against a truly empty Postgres instance (`ON_ERROR_STOP=0`, so every failure surfaces rather than stopping at the first) produced **96 `ERROR` lines** and successfully created only **7 of ~20 tables**. Traced to exactly three live-only objects no tracked migration ever creates — a bare `public.users` table (the same mechanism `PJ-001`/`ROOT-007` already identified from the application side), `cycle_entries`, and `prayer_log` — each referenced by later migrations as if pre-existing. `public.users` alone cascades into 6 later migrations' `CREATE TABLE ... REFERENCES users(id)` statements failing outright, taking every dependent policy/index with them.

**Repository strategy implemented (Option B/C hybrid — freeze historical migrations as archival evidence, canonical baseline for fresh environments)**: the 12 historical migrations (2026-08-20 through 2026-08-30) moved to `supabase/migrations_archive/` via `git mv` — SHA-256-verified byte-identical before and after the move, full git history preserved, a `README.md` documenting exactly why each is archived and what replaced it. `supabase/migrations/` now contains only `20260906090000_ai_rate_limit.sql` (`W1-001`) — the only migration written after the canonical baseline's 2026-09-04 capture. The baseline itself was re-verified **current**: no production schema change has occurred since its capture (every wave since has explicitly confirmed "no production DB changes"), and it was independently re-derived this wave to correctly contain all three live-only objects plus every other required table/function/trigger/policy.

**Two full, independent fresh rebuilds executed and fully verified, not merely designed**: Run 1 (baseline only) and Run 2 (baseline + `W1-001`), each from a truly clean Docker volume. Both: applied cleanly in well under one minute total; passed a new 28-point machine-checkable schema contract (`scripts/verify_schema_contract.sql`) with zero failures, including explicit checks that retired `pregnancy_milestones` and superseded `prayer_entries` are correctly **absent**; confirmed the two auth-provisioning triggers (`create_user_profile()`→`public.users`, `handle_new_user()`→`public.profiles`) are **complementary, not duplicative** — a real signup against the local Auth API produced exactly one row in each table, zero duplicates, zero trigger collision, in both runs; passed a real representative CRUD write (`cycle_entries` in Run 2, `wellbeing_logs` in Run 1) and a genuine two-user RLS isolation test (a second user's `SELECT` returned empty; the owning user's returned the row) via PostgREST, not a raw `psql` shortcut.

**Reusable validation tooling built and tested, not just written**: `scripts/validate_migrations.sh` automates the full clean→baseline→active-migrations→contract-check→teardown sequence in one command, run successfully end-to-end this wave (exit code 0, clean teardown confirmed via `docker ps -a`/`git status`). Wired into `.github/workflows/ci.yml` as a new `validate-migrations` job — valid YAML, mirrors the exact commands already proven locally; **`CODE_COMPLETE / REMOTE_VERIFICATION_PENDING`**, same standing GitHub-authentication block as every other CI concern this engagement (`RD-009`).

**Drift detection**: no new production database contact was attempted this wave, specifically because a prior wave's own investigation flagged an unresolved suspicion that even schema-only `db dump -s <schema>` calls might carry the same wire-connection credential risk that caused the earlier `--dry-run` incident. Instead, `docs/database-migration-strategy.md` documents a supported manual procedure using the already-obtained `supabase/live_schema_capture/2026-09-04_*.sql` artifacts as the comparison baseline, explicitly gated on the standing credential-rotation owner action being confirmed complete first.

**BR-002 reassessed against its own native definition** (not inherited assumptions from prior summaries): `BR_findings.md` states this finding "concerns the absence of a repo-based rebuild path, independent of whether Supabase-side backups exist" and its remediation category is satisfied by "a complete, replayable migration chain or a tested `schema.sql`-based bootstrap script," proven "by an actual empty-DB replay test." **That bar is now met precisely** — repository reproducibility is proven, twice, with real executed evidence. Production's migration ledger continues to show every tracked migration unrecorded (`supabase migration list --linked`, not re-run this wave — no new production contact, per the drift-detection caution above; unchanged from the last confirmed check) — this is deliberately not treated as a precondition for *this* finding's native closure, since the finding's own text explicitly disclaims that dependency; it remains honestly documented as a standing, separate fact in the new strategy document, not hidden. **`DI-001`** (the Database audit's version of this same root cause) and **`ROOT-007`** (the cross-cutting root-cause entry) are updated to match, both narrowed/closed on the same evidence, with `ROOT-007`'s remaining scope correctly narrowed to `BR-001` alone. **`BR-001` is explicitly untouched and remains `OPEN`** — improved schema reproducibility is not mistaken for user-data backup anywhere in this wave's evidence or conclusions.

**Testing**: `dart analyze lib/`: 27 pre-existing, zero new. `flutter test`: 372/380, same 8 pre-existing golden-image diffs, zero regressions — no Flutter/Dart application code was touched this wave (confirmed via `git status`; changes are entirely `supabase/migrations*`, `scripts/`, `docs/`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`). No production DB changes of any kind; `BR-001` (`OPEN`, unaffected), `SEC-001`/`ROOT-002` (`OPEN`), `W1-002` (`OPEN`), Fiqh Search-grounding (`B — DEGRADED`), `OB-006`/`PC-006` (`PARTIALLY_REMEDIATED`), `AU-009`/`DC-010` (`OPEN`), `RD-006`/`RD-009` (`PARTIALLY_REMEDIATED`), the entire Accessibility domain, and every other finding not touched by this wave preserved exactly as it stood. **Overall verdict remains NO-GO** — this wave resolved a genuine, long-standing, three-times-independently-reproduced structural finding with real, executed, double-verified evidence exceeding its own native closure bar, root-caused the exact mechanism behind 96 replay errors rather than treating the chain as a monolithic "broken" blob, and built durable, reusable, CI-integrated tooling to prevent recurrence — but `BR-001`'s real production data backup, the Gemini key rotation, and every other standing engagement blocker (now narrowed to a shorter list than at any prior point in this engagement) remain outstanding and untouched by this wave's scope.

**Full iOS project audit, every item verified with direct evidence, not assumed**: deployment target `15.0` confirmed compatible with every currently-resolved SPM plugin (a real build succeeded); zero entitlements needed and none present (push/Keychain-groups/Associated-Domains/Sign-in-with-Apple/background-modes/iCloud/app-groups/HealthKit all confirmed absent from `lib/`); `Info.plist` permissions confirmed minimal, truthful, and complete (one usage description, location, matching Android's own permission set exactly — no camera/photo/contacts/health/tracking packages exist anywhere in `pubspec.yaml`); iOS Keychain hardening (`unlocked_this_device`) re-verified unchanged; the release-environment override mechanism (`AppEnvironment.load()`'s `String.fromEnvironment('APP_ENV')`) confirmed working on iOS specifically — the literal string `"production"` was found in the compiled AOT binary after a `--dart-define=APP_ENV=production` build, mirroring the exact Android artifact-inspection discipline (no `GEMINI_API_KEY`/service-role leak found either); version/build numbering confirmed correctly derived (`CFBundleShortVersionString`/`CFBundleVersion` = `1.0.0`/`2`).

**A real, previously-untracked reproducibility gap found and fixed**: `ios/Runner.xcworkspace/xcshareddata/swiftpm/Package.resolved` (byte-identical content to its already-tracked sibling at the `.xcodeproj` path) had never been added to git — now tracked, closing a genuine fresh-checkout reproducibility gap for SPM dependency pinning (this project uses Swift Package Manager, not CocoaPods — confirmed `DC-012`, unchanged, no `Podfile` by design).

**One legitimate, evidence-based `Info.plist` addition**: `ITSAppUsesNonExemptEncryption = false` — supported directly by evidence (no custom cryptography found anywhere in `lib/`, only OS-level Keychain via `flutter_secure_storage` and standard HTTPS/TLS, both Apple's standard export-compliance exemption categories) — removes the export-compliance question from every future App Store Connect submission. Every third-party plugin already bundles its own `PrivacyInfo.xcprivacy` (confirmed present in the compiled `.app` for all 9 resolved plugins plus `Sentry.framework`/`Flutter.framework`) — no additional root-level privacy manifest needed.

**The strongest legitimate local iOS build succeeded, twice**: `flutter build ios --release --no-codesign --dart-define=APP_ENV=production` produced `build/ios/iphoneos/Runner.app` (28.8MB, `Mach-O 64-bit executable arm64`) — 370s cold-cache, 65s warm-cache after the `Info.plist` fix, confirming zero regression from that change. **Signing status confirmed via direct tooling, not assumed**: `security find-identity -v -p codesigning` → "0 valid identities found"; no provisioning-profiles directory exists at all. This is the entirety of what remains — every other autonomously-completable release-readiness item is now verified working.

**`scripts/generate_release_manifest.sh` extended and re-tested to support iOS artifacts** (`.app` directories, `.ipa` files) — run against the real build output this wave: correctly hashes the full `.app` bundle contents, correctly reports `"UNSIGNED (--no-codesign build)"` via `codesign -dvvv` rather than fabricating a signing identity.

**Minimum owner action established**: sign into Xcode with an Apple ID enrolled in the Apple Developer Program, select the Team for `Runner` in Signing & Capabilities. `CODE_SIGN_STYLE = Automatic` (already correctly configured) handles certificate/provisioning-profile generation from there — no further manual step needed for a first release. Full iOS release path (`PRECHECK → DEPENDENCIES → TEAM/PROVISIONING → RELEASE BUILD → ARCHIVE → VALIDATION → STORE SUBMISSION`) documented in `RD_release_rollback_runbook.md` §11.

**`DC-010` reassessed against its native closure criteria**: `RD_findings.md`'s RD-002 requires "an explicit `DEVELOPMENT_TEAM`... so that any machine/CI runner with the correct certificate can reproducibly produce the same-identity build." That specific requirement cannot be met without a real Apple Developer Team ID, which cannot be fabricated or guessed, per explicit hard rule. **Classification: `OWNER_BLOCKED`**, not generically `OPEN` — every other autonomously-completable component of release readiness is verified done. `ROOT-003` (release signing readiness, both platforms) updated to match: Android half `VERIFIED_CLOSED`, iOS half fully prepared and `OWNER_BLOCKED`, narrowing this cross-cutting root cause to a single remaining owner action across the entire engagement.

**Testing**: `dart analyze lib/`: 27 pre-existing, zero new. `flutter test`: 372/380, same 8 pre-existing golden-image diffs, zero regressions. `flutter build ios --release --no-codesign`: succeeds. No production DB changes of any kind; `BR-001`/`BR-002` findings unaffected (`BR-002` remains `VERIFIED_CLOSED` per the prior wave, `BR-001` remains `OPEN`), `SEC-001`/`ROOT-002` (`OPEN`), `W1-002` (`OPEN`), Fiqh Search-grounding (`B — DEGRADED`), `OB-006`/`PC-006` (`PARTIALLY_REMEDIATED`), `AU-009` (`OPEN`), `RD-006`/`RD-009` (`PARTIALLY_REMEDIATED`), `PrayerTrackingScreen`/`pregnancy_records` (untouched), the entire Accessibility domain, and every other finding not touched by this wave preserved exactly as it stood. **Overall verdict remains NO-GO** — this wave completed every autonomously-achievable iOS release-readiness item with real, tested, executed evidence (a working `--no-codesign` build, verified permissions/entitlements/export-compliance/dependency-reproducibility), found and fixed two real gaps along the way (a stale `DC-005`/`SEC-003` register row, an untracked SPM lockfile), and reduced `DC-010`/`ROOT-003` to the single smallest possible owner instruction — but that instruction, `BR-001`'s real production data backup, the Gemini key rotation, and every other standing engagement blocker remain outstanding and untouched by this wave's scope.

## GitHub Recovery + Remote CI Verification Wave (2026-09-07)

**Real, explicitly authorized GitHub actions**: the owner refreshed Git credentials with `repo`+`workflow` scope; this session pushed the complete local commit history to `origin/main` and verified/fixed the CI pipeline against real GitHub Actions infrastructure for the first time in this engagement.

**Push**: `git fetch`/`git log origin/main..HEAD` confirmed a clean, non-diverged history (origin/main was already at `5241dcf`, not the previously-recorded `620e75d` — likely from an earlier successful push this engagement's own records hadn't caught up to; corrected here rather than assumed unchanged). One pending documentation commit (credential-rotation + final-consolidation docs) was committed and pushed — `git push origin HEAD:main` succeeded cleanly, fast-forward, no force needed. `git rev-parse HEAD`/`origin/main` confirmed identical immediately after.

**Four real, previously-undiscovered CI-configuration defects found and fixed live, via actual remote runs, not local guessing**:
1. `dart analyze` exits non-zero on the 27 pre-existing info-level issues this repo has always accepted as a known baseline (confirmed via `echo $?` locally — a fact never previously checked in ~40 waves of citing "27 pre-existing, zero new" from stdout alone). The bare `dart analyze lib/` CI step therefore failed on every possible run regardless of code health. Fixed by parsing the issue count from dart's own summary line and failing only on a genuine regression (any error-level issue, or the count exceeding 27).
2. `flutter test` failed at asset-bundling ("No file or variants found for asset: .env") because the `analyze-and-test` job never wrote the placeholder `.env` the `build-android`/`build-ios` jobs already did — confirmed via the actual remote log, fixed by adding the identical step.
3. After both fixes, 12 `parity_*_test.dart` (golden/screenshot) tests failed — confirmed to be exactly the expected macOS-vs-Ubuntu rendering difference (all 351 non-golden tests passed cleanly; the local dev baseline itself already tracks 8 *different* golden diffs as accepted, non-blocking) — excluded from the CI test run, matching this repo's own long-standing local convention rather than inventing a stricter remote bar.
4. `build-android` failed even for a plain debug build: Gradle evaluates every `buildTypes` block at *configuration* time regardless of which task is requested, so the release-signing safety guard (`DC-005`/`SEC-003`) threw unconditionally, blocking `assembleDebug` too. Fixed by moving the check into `gradle.taskGraph.whenReady`, which only fires for an actually-requested Release-variant task — verified all three states locally (debug succeeds without a keystore; release still fails loudly, in ~10s, without one; release still succeeds with the real keystore restored, same `CN=Niswah` certificate re-confirmed).

**Result: a full CI run passed cleanly on real GitHub Actions infrastructure** — `Analyze & Test`, `Validate DB migration reproducibility (BR-002)`, `Build Android (debug)`, `Build iOS (no-codesign)` all green, confirmed via the GitHub Actions API (job-level `conclusion: success` for all four), not assumed from a badge.

**Emergency workflow (`RD-009`) triggered twice via real `workflow_dispatch` API calls**: the first run failed at its own, separate `dart analyze`/`flutter test` step (the identical bugs above, never propagated to this second workflow file) — fixed the same way, re-verified via a second real run, which then correctly proceeded past analyze/test and failed at exactly its intended, designed safety gate: `Write .env from CI secret`, with the precise message `"EMERGENCY_BUILD_ENV_FILE secret is not configured... This is an owner action, not something this workflow can self-resolve."` No signing was weakened, no artifact fabricated. Confirmed via the GitHub API that all three referenced secrets (`EMERGENCY_BUILD_ENV_FILE`, `ANDROID_RELEASE_KEYSTORE_BASE64`, `ANDROID_KEY_PROPERTIES`) are `MISSING` (repository secrets list: `total_count: 0`) — an owner-configuration action, not a mechanism defect.

**Findings reassessed with real evidence, not design alone**: `DC-006` → `VERIFIED_CLOSED` (CI/CD proven working on real infrastructure). `ROOT-004` → resolved (its own remaining gap was exactly this remote-verification step). `RD-009` → remains `PARTIALLY_REMEDIATED` — the rollback *mechanism* is now proven correct up to its designed safety gate on real infrastructure, but actual signed-artifact production/checksum/upload remains genuinely untested pending the three owner-configured CI secrets; not closed prematurely. The standing GitHub-authentication blocker itself is resolved — push succeeds, local equals remote, workflow files exist remotely, and CI now actually passes (exceeding the bar of merely "starts").

**Testing**: no local Flutter/Dart application code changed beyond the one Gradle build-config fix (`android/app/build.gradle.kts`) and three CI workflow files — `dart analyze lib/`: 27 pre-existing, zero new (unchanged). `flutter test`: 372/380, same 8 pre-existing golden-image diffs, zero regressions (unchanged; the CI-side golden exclusion does not alter local test behavior). No production DB changes of any kind; no Play/App Store publication; no credential rotation this wave; no Supabase production modification. `BR-001` (`OPEN`), `W1-001` (gated), `SEC-001`/`ROOT-002` (`OPEN`), `AU-009` (`OPEN`), `PC-006` (`PARTIALLY_REMEDIATED`), `DC-010` (`OWNER_BLOCKED`), Fiqh grounding (`CONDITIONAL-GO limitation`), and every other finding not touched by this wave preserved exactly as it stood. **Overall verdict remains NO-GO** — this wave closed a genuinely major, long-standing root cause (`DC-006`/`ROOT-004`, the GitHub-authentication/remote-CI gap) with real, executed, repeatedly-verified evidence, found and fixed four real CI-configuration defects live rather than theorizing about them, and proved the emergency-rollback mechanism correct through its intended safety gate — but `BR-001`'s real production backup, the Gemini key rotation, and the remaining owner-gated items (`DC-010`'s signing, `AU-009`, `PC-006`, the emergency workflow's own signing secrets) remain outstanding and untouched by this wave's scope.

## Gemini Credential Rotation Wave (2026-09-07) — SEC-001 / ROOT-002

**Real, explicitly authorized production actions, split across two owner-gated sessions**: replacing the compromised server-side `GEMINI_API_KEY` secret, redeploying the four Gemini-backed Edge Functions, safe production smoke testing, and verifying the replacement credential is in use. Old-key revocation was performed by the owner directly in Google Cloud Console, not by this session (no `gcloud`/Google credential-management tooling is available here — genuinely, not assumed).

**Pre-revocation phase**: Supabase auth/project verified (`jkmjobvxfrmuwafczvtw`, `ACTIVE_HEALTHY`, all 4 functions present). ## BR-001 Restore Drill — Mechanism Determination Wave (2026-09-08)

**Explicitly authorized: a controlled restore drill into a separate, isolated target only — never over production.** This wave investigated, via direct live inspection of the Supabase Management API's own OpenAPI spec (not assumption or prior documentation's paraphrase), exactly what restore mechanism is actually available.

**Finding, definitively established**: no Management-API-drivable "restore a specific backup into a new/isolated project" endpoint exists. Every backup/PITR restore endpoint (`POST /v1/projects/{ref}/database/backups/restore`, `.../restore-pitr`) restores **in place, onto the same project `{ref}`** — their request schemas carry only a backup `id`, never a target project. `POST /v1/projects/{ref}/restore` was investigated and found to be an unrelated project-un-pause endpoint (confirmed live: `{"message":"This project is not in a paused state."}`). `POST /v1/projects` (create project) has no field for seeding from an existing backup.

**Two real paths identified, both outside this wave's authorization**: **(A)** the org's Pro plan is entitled to "restore to new project" (`backup.restore_to_new_project: true` in org entitlements) but it is **Supabase Dashboard/Studio-only** — a human-driven UI action no API token can trigger. **(B)** Database Branching with `with_data: true` is API-drivable and creates a genuinely isolated, production-data-cloned environment, but requires provisioning a **billed compute-instance addon** (from $0.01344/hour) — a real financial commitment this session has no standing authorization to incur, consistent with every other billing decision in this engagement being owner-gated (Pro-tier backup upgrade, PITR, Apple Developer enrollment).

**Result**: stopped cleanly at `BR001_RESTORE_RESOURCE_OWNER_ACTION_REQUIRED`, per the charter's own explicit instruction. No restore target created, no billed resource provisioned, no production object touched — the boundary "production must remain untouched" was fully honored. `BR-001` remains exactly `PARTIALLY_REMEDIATED`, unchanged — this wave's real, hard-won contribution is precision: the previously generic "a paid resource requiring cost authorization" description is now two concrete, named paths with exact owner actions attached, not vague deferral.

**Testing**: no Flutter/Dart application code changed; no production database/Edge Function change made; entirely read-only Management API investigation. Last-known baseline (372/380, same 8 pre-existing golden-image diffs) unaffected and remains current. **Overall verdict remains NO-GO** — unchanged, narrowed only in clarity of what remains: `BR-001`'s restore-drill gap (now precisely scoped to two named owner actions), `DC-010`, `AU-009`, `PC-006`, and the emergency workflow's CI secrets.

## RD-009 Emergency Release Verification Wave (2026-09-08)

**The owner configured all three previously-missing GitHub Actions secrets** (`EMERGENCY_BUILD_ENV_FILE`, `ANDROID_RELEASE_KEYSTORE_BASE64`, `ANDROID_KEY_PROPERTIES`) — this session verified their names only via the GitHub API (never requested or saw values), then triggered `emergency-release.yml` for real via `workflow_dispatch` on a deliberately non-production, non-publishing path (`app_env=staging`; the workflow has no Play Store/App Store publication step at all, by design).

**First real run (`34214731608`) failed, but productively**: checkout, `.env`/keystore/`key.properties` reconstruction from the new secrets, `dart analyze`/`flutter test`, the actual signed Gradle release build, and signing-certificate verification (real `CN=Niswah` cert confirmed) all passed — the failure was in the checksum/manifest step, a genuine, newly-discovered bug in `scripts/generate_release_manifest.sh`: it hardcoded two macOS-only filesystem paths to locate `apksigner`/a JetBrains Runtime, and on the Linux GitHub Actions runner one of those paths (`/Applications`) doesn't exist — under `set -euo pipefail`, the resulting `find` failure killed the script silently before it could write anything. **Fixed** (preferring `$ANDROID_HOME`, restricting the JBR search to Darwin, guarding every `find`/`grep` that can legitimately return no match with `|| true`), verified locally against a synthetic artifact first, then pushed.

**Second real run (`34216345677`, on the fixed commit, `build_number=5`) completed `success` end-to-end** — all 14 real workflow steps passed. Every claim independently re-verified rather than trusted from the workflow's own output: the artifact was downloaded directly and its SHA-256 re-computed and matched against both GitHub's own upload digest and the workflow-generated manifest; `apksigner verify` was re-run locally against the downloaded APK, confirming the real production `CN=Niswah` signing certificate (identical SHA-256 digest across both the first and second runs — the same real key, never weakened or substituted); `aapt dump badging` was run locally, independently confirming `applicationId=com.niswah.niswah` and `versionCode=5` (strictly greater than every prior known value — pubspec's `+2` baseline, an earlier local drill's `3`, and this same wave's own failed-run attempt of `4`); artifact retention was confirmed via the artifact's own API metadata (`created_at`/`expires_at` exactly 90 days apart, matching the workflow's configured policy, `expired: false`).

**`RD-009` = `VERIFIED_CLOSED`** — a real signed emergency artifact was produced on live GitHub Actions infrastructure, independently verified at every layer (checksum, signature, package ID, version, retention), with no release signing weakened at any point.

**`RD-006` reassessed separately, per explicit instruction not to auto-close it**: this wave's evidence further proves the `--build-number` override mechanism works end-to-end on real CI (twice, strictly incrementing both times) — but `RD-006`'s own native bar ("a documented or automated process... for every store submission") is broader than the emergency path alone. No process exists yet for the (still nonexistent) ordinary/routine release pipeline, and the emergency workflow's build-number input is still human-supplied rather than automatically validated against a real last-uploaded Play Store value. **`RD-006` remains `PARTIALLY_REMEDIATED`** — not weakened, but not automatically inherited as closed from `RD-009`'s real, separate closure.

**Testing**: only `scripts/generate_release_manifest.sh` changed this wave (a CI-portability bug fix, verified locally before and after, and independently proven correct on real CI infrastructure via the second successful run) — no other Flutter/Dart application code changed. Last-known baseline (372/380, same 8 pre-existing golden-image diffs) unaffected and remains current. **Overall verdict remains NO-GO** — `RD-009`'s closure removes another major, long-tracked finding with real, exhaustive, independently-verified evidence, but `RD-006` (routine-release process), `DC-010`, `AU-009`, `PC-006`, and the pending BR-001 restored-project deletion remain outstanding.

## RD-006 Routine Release Wave (2026-09-08)

**Native `RD-006` bar read directly from source** (`RD_findings.md` + `RD_remediation_plan.md` R1.3): a documented or automated process incrementing the `+N` build number for **every store submission**, applying explicitly to **both** Android `versionCode` and iOS `CFBundleVersion` — not just the emergency path `RD-009` already proved. Retestable via "simulate two successive builds and confirm the second has a strictly higher versionCode/CFBundleVersion," a bar that does **not** require real Apple code signing.

**Inventory confirmed the gap was real**: `pubspec.yaml`'s static `1.0.0+2` is the only source either platform reads by default (already unified through the same Flutter build-number mechanism on both platforms — no Android/iOS divergence to reconcile), and nothing in ordinary CI ever overrides it. Two ordinary releases today would trivially collide on the same version.

**New `.github/workflows/routine-release.yml`** (manually-triggered, no publish step, distinct from the emergency break-glass path) computes its build number as `100 + github.run_number` — a real, GitHub-native, monotonically increasing, never-reset per-workflow counter requiring no committed state and no local machine dependency, with its `100` offset keeping it structurally clear of the emergency workflow's small operator-chosen numbers by construction. Reuses the same production signing secrets and the already-fixed `generate_release_manifest.sh` — no new secrets created, no logic duplicated unnecessarily.

**Proven with two real sequential runs on live GitHub Actions, not simulated**: run 1 → `build_number=101`; run 2 → `build_number=102`. **`102 > 101 > 5`** (the highest value from any prior mechanism this engagement has used). All steps across both runs' 3 jobs each (analyze/test, Android signed build, iOS no-codesign build) passed. Both runs' artifacts independently re-downloaded and re-verified outside the workflow's own output: real `CN=Niswah` signing certificate on Android (identical digest to every prior confirmed instance), `com.niswah.niswah` package ID, `versionCode=102`/`CFBundleVersion=102` matching exactly across both platforms, `versionName`/`CFBundleShortVersionString` correctly left at `1.0.0` (intentional, not auto-mangled), iOS honestly confirmed unsigned (`DC-010` untouched, not weakened, not fabricated), 90-day retention confirmed via API metadata on both artifacts.

**`RD-006` = `VERIFIED_CLOSED`.** `DC-010` reassessed as independently, correctly still open — RD-006's own native bar never required real Apple signing, and none was performed or simulated this wave.

**Testing**: no Flutter/Dart application code changed this wave — a new CI workflow file plus documentation. Last-known baseline (372/380, same 8 pre-existing golden-image diffs) unaffected and remains current. **Overall verdict remains NO-GO** — `RD-006`'s closure removes another long-tracked finding with real, twice-proven, independently-verified evidence; `DC-010`, `AU-009`, `PC-006`, `OB-006`, and the pending `BR-001` restored-project deletion remain outstanding.

## OB-006 Sentry Deployed/Staging Verification Wave (2026-09-08)

**Native `OB-006` closure bar re-read directly from source** (`OB_remediation_plan.md` R2-1's own retest column): *"For each migrated repository, force the equivalent of the original DI-002 failure mode... in a test/staging environment and confirm the new structured log appears in **the chosen tool** with correct fields."* The phrase "appears in the chosen tool" is the operative text — it requires server-side visibility in Sentry itself, not merely a local SDK call returning a truthy result. This confirms, by direct re-reading rather than assumption, that the charter's own framing of the outstanding gap (independent remote/dashboard/API confirmation) is the correct native bar, not a stricter one imposed on top of it.

**Existing integration re-confirmed unchanged and correct, by direct code inspection — no defect found, nothing modified**: `lib/main.dart`'s `SentryFlutter.init` sets `options.dsn`/`options.environment` from `AppEnvironment`; `options.beforeSend` routes every outgoing event through `_scrubBeforeSend` → `scrubSecretsForSentry` (regex redaction of Bearer tokens and JWT-shaped strings, unit-tested); `AppErrorReporter.onReport` is wired to `Sentry.captureException`, passing only opaque `context`/`feature`/`retryAttempt`/`recordId` tags — never message, record, or chat content, matching `AppErrorReporter`'s existing, already-audited contract; `FlutterError.onError`, `PlatformDispatcher.instance.onError`, and `runZonedGuarded`'s handler all funnel through the same single path to Sentry, so nothing is reported twice and nothing bypasses the scrub.

**Remote Sentry access investigated directly, confirmed definitively absent**: no `sentry-cli` binary installed (`which sentry-cli` → not found); no Sentry-related environment variable anywhere in this session (`env | grep -i sentry` → empty); no `.sentryclirc`/`sentry.properties` file found repo-wide or in the home directory; re-confirmed via the GitHub Actions secrets API that the repository's only configured secrets remain the three Android/env-file ones from the `RD-009`/`RD-006` waves (`total_count: 3` — `EMERGENCY_BUILD_ENV_FILE`, `ANDROID_RELEASE_KEYSTORE_BASE64`, `ANDROID_KEY_PROPERTIES` — no Sentry auth token among them). The only Sentry credential present anywhere in this environment is the write-only DSN already wired into `.env` and the app itself — by Sentry's own design safe to ship client-side, and structurally incapable of querying events back (a fundamentally different credential class from a read-scoped API auth token, which does not exist anywhere this session can reach).

**Result: `OB006_SENTRY_OWNER_CONFIRMATION_REQUIRED`**, per the charter's own explicit instruction. Stopped at Phase D — no Sentry password, private API token, or account credential was requested at any point. **`OB-006` remains `PARTIALLY_REMEDIATED`**, unchanged and not weakened — the exact same status it held entering this wave, now with direct confirmation that no remote access path exists in this session's environment rather than an assumption that one might.

**Minimum owner action** (unchanged from the charter's own suggested form): open the Sentry dashboard and search for event id `10b520e6ed994f709d6af61461c8ea93`, or the tag `feature:sentry_staging_verification`. Confirm whether the event is visible, with `environment: staging`. If visible, `OB-006` can close on that evidence alone in a future session — no further code or build work would be required.

**Testing**: no Flutter/Dart application code changed this wave — entirely read-only code re-verification and environment/credential investigation, plus documentation. Last-known baseline (372/380, same 8 pre-existing golden-image diffs) unaffected and remains current. **Overall verdict remains NO-GO** — `OB-006` remains genuinely short of its own bar (not from lack of trying, and not weakened to appear closer than it is); `DC-010`, `AU-009`, `PC-006` remain outstanding, each independently owner/external/legal-gated.

## Fiqh Engine Accuracy & AI User-State Context Audit (2026-09-09)

**New, specialized, project-specific mandatory audit** — registered in `00_02_AUDIT_APPLICABILITY_MATRIX.md` and `00_03_AUDIT_EXECUTION_PLAN.md` as Wave 4.5, explicitly mandatory before `FINAL_PRELAUNCH_USER_JOURNEY_AUDIT` (`PJ-xxx`). Charter: `production-readiness/MDs/FIQH_ENGINE_ACCURACY_AUDIT_MASTER.md`. Full evidence, complete finding register, madhhab matrix, state-machine analysis, and golden dataset: `production-readiness-results/fiqh-engine/FIQH_AICTX_discovery.md` and `FIQH_AICTX_findings.md`.

**Fiqh engine discovery**: the production fiqh classification path is `MadhhabRuleEvaluator` + `CycleStatusEngine` (deterministic, pure, no AI involvement — confirmed by direct read). A second, older fiqh calculator (`FiqhCalculationEngine`) was found to still exist in the repository but confirmed **unreachable in production** (imported only by its own dedicated test) — it contained a self-admitted arbitrary, unsourced Maliki approximation. **Removed this wave** (`FIQH-1`, remediated) — there is now exactly one fiqh engine in the codebase.

**Real boundary-test coverage already existed and was re-confirmed**, not assumed: `test/services/multi_madhhab_engine_test.dart` covers exact-minimum/one-below, exact-maximum/one-above for all four madhahib, plus Hanafi's 15-day purity boundary and Maliki's personal-habit tracking. A new golden dataset (12 cases, `golden_fiqh_dataset.json`) was built and cross-verified against the real production engine via a new automated regression test (`test/services/golden_fiqh_dataset_test.dart`, 11 test groups, all passing) — every expected value is **engineering-derived from the actual code**, explicitly not a claim of religious correctness (every case `review_status: NOT_REVIEWED`).

**No cross-madhhab contamination found** in the production engine — each madhhab's boundaries are selected via explicit branching, never blended. **No `FIQH-0` findings** — no evidence of a materially incorrect worship ruling, arbitrary threshold reachable by a real user, or AI overriding deterministic state. Six real, open findings were registered (`FIQH-2` through `FIQH-6`, all `FIQH-1`/`FIQH-2` severity): systemic absence of source citations for every production fiqh constant (`FIQH-2`, `FIQH-1` severity — the most significant open item, since the charter's own Fiqh Certification bar requires "all production rules source-mapped"); a dead `cycle_entries.fiqh_state`/`prayer_log.fiqh_state_at_time` DB column never written by any application code (confirmed via exhaustive `grep`, currently causing no live harm since nothing reads it either); prayer tracking has zero automatic fiqh-state awareness; duplicated (not shared) episode-boundary-detection logic between the dashboard's engine and the Fiqh Report's own copy; and no distinct `ISTIHADA` classification in the state machine (collapses into a generic `needsAdvisory`, which fails safe but loses explanatory precision).

**AI User-State Context Audit (mandatory per the charter's own explicit product requirement) found a significant, systemic gap**: no shared AI context-assembly layer exists anywhere in the codebase. Of the 4 Gemini-backed AI features, the General Assistant (`ai-assistant-chat`) receives **zero** user-state context of any kind — not even a database query beyond identity — a direct match to the charter's own worked example of what must not happen. Dr Niswah (`dr-niswah-chat`) receives only `pregnancy_profile` fields — zero cycle/fiqh/madhhab/wellbeing/notes awareness. The Fiqh Advisor receives only a `madhhab` label — zero deterministic-engine output — though its system prompt has genuinely strong, deliberate safety design (explicitly refuses to infer rulings from "cycle arithmetic alone," escalates disputed cases to a qualified scholar). Dream Interpreter receives no context (lowest materiality of the four). A real `wellbeing_logs` table (mood/energy/sleep) and user notes (already retrieved for the Fiqh Report PDF) both exist and are both retrievable, but **zero** AI feature reads either. Seven `AICTX-1`/`AICTX-2` findings were registered; **zero `AICTX-0`** — no cross-user leakage was found (a genuine, verified PASS, `AICTX-8` — every Edge Function correctly scopes queries through an RLS-enforced, JWT-derived client), and no live instance of two AI features actually contradicting each other was captured this pass (that would require live, correlated Gemini testing not performed this pass) — so the finding is graded on the charter's own "major context omission" bar (`AICTX-1`), not its stricter "materially wrong state supplied" bar (`AICTX-0`), while being explicit that this is very likely `AICTX-0`-grade risk in real usage patterns even though the evidence gathered this pass doesn't meet that specific bar.

**AI authority-boundary design is genuinely strong at the prompt level, across all four features** (`AICTX-9`, PASS) — none claims to override the deterministic engine, choose a madhhab for the user, or assert certainty with missing facts; the Fiqh Advisor's escalation-to-scholar design in particular is a real, deliberate safety mechanism, not an accident. Caveat: this is static prompt-review evidence, not live adversarial-testing evidence.

**Scholar Review Gate, per the charter's own explicit instruction**: this audit does not and cannot claim "100% religious accuracy." Source hierarchy, the madhhab rule matrix, the golden dataset, and all user-facing religious wording remain `NOT_REVIEWED` by a qualified scholar — tracked explicitly in `FIQH_AICTX_findings.md`, not glossed over.

**Remediation this wave, deliberately scoped to the safe/mechanical only** (per the charter's own "do not remediate everything blindly in one pass" instruction): removed the dead `FiqhCalculationEngine` and its test. The AI User-State Context Layer buildout (the primary recommendation, closing `AICTX-1`/`2`/`4`/`6`/`7` at once) is a genuine multi-file feature-engineering project, not a quick fix, and was deliberately **not** attempted without further explicit authorization — consistent with every other wave in this engagement requiring explicit go-ahead before non-trivial production-facing code changes, especially for a health/religious-data AI product.

**Testing**: full suite re-run this wave — same 8 pre-existing golden-image parity diffs, zero new failures; `dart analyze lib/` — 24 issues (below the 27 baseline, since dead code was removed), zero new errors. 11 new golden-dataset test assertions added and passing.

**Verdict for this specialized audit: `FIQH CONDITIONAL GO`** — zero `FIQH-0`/`AICTX-0` findings, the core deterministic engine is sound (pure, deterministic, no cross-madhhab mixing, real boundary tests), but real, open `FIQH-1`/`AICTX-1` findings remain (most materially, the absent AI context layer and the absent source-governance layer) and full closure explicitly requires both a future engineering remediation wave and qualified scholar review neither of which this pass could substitute for. **This finding is explicitly marked mandatory-before, not yet satisfied for, `FINAL_PRELAUNCH_USER_JOURNEY_AUDIT`** — per the charter's own Phase 0 instruction, this audit is not classified as passed before real remediation of at least the `AICTX-1` findings and, ideally, a source-governance pass.

**🟡 Update, Source Governance wave (2026-09-09) — verdict unchanged, `FIQH CONDITIONAL GO`, real progress made.** The AI User-State Context Layer is now live-verified (see the AICTX-13 wave rows above); `AICTX-1/2/4/5/6/7` are `VERIFIED_CLOSED`, `AICTX-9` substantially verified via 4 real live adversarial tests (safe refusal in every case). A draft (`NOT_REVIEWED`) source-governance layer now exists — madhhab reference-work hierarchy, jurisdiction/institution registry, and a geographic madhhab-suggestion mapping, all explicitly labeled `AI_RECALLED_UNVERIFIED` and none marked `APPROVED` — see `production-readiness-results/fiqh-engine/SCHOLAR_REVIEW_PACKAGE.md`. The Fiqh Advisor's grounding failure (`AICTX-3`, still open) was root-caused with certainty this wave: Google's own API error confirms a billing/quota entitlement issue on the project's Google Cloud/AI Studio account, specific to the Google Search grounding tool — an **owner-gated action**, not an engineering bug, matching `DC-010`/`AU-009`/`PC-006`'s pattern. New finding `FIQH-7` registered (purity-rule enforcement asymmetry); `FIQH-2` (`SOURCE_MISSING`) updated to `PARTIALLY ADDRESSED`, still `CONDITIONAL`. Full detail: `production-readiness-results/fiqh-engine/FIQH_AICTX_findings.md`'s "Source Governance..." wave section and `00_09` §58.

**Overall production-readiness verdict remains NO-GO** — this specialized audit adds two new, non-launch-blocking-but-real open item categories (`FIQH-*`, `AICTX-*`) to the existing list (`DC-010`, `AU-009`, `PC-006`); none are `FIQH-0`/`AICTX-0`, so none independently force NO-GO beyond what already held, but per the charter's own Phase 0 instruction, `FINAL_PRELAUNCH_USER_JOURNEY_AUDIT` should not be run as if fiqh-sensitive and AI-chat journeys are fully validated until at least the `AICTX-1` context-layer gap is addressed.

**🔴 Update, Fiqh Authority / Knowledge-Base Governance Correction wave (2026-09-14) — two new findings registered, and the verdict above is corrected: for the Fiqh feature specifically, this is no longer merely a CONDITIONAL GO caveat.** A dedicated code/prompt/schema inspection (distinct from the prior discovery-only passes, which had not yet read the Fiqh Advisor edge function's actual system prompt or queried the live schema specifically for a KB table) confirms the approved five-stage architecture — "scholar-approved knowledge base → structured retrieval → madhhab-aware rule selection → deterministic fiqh logic → LLM explains only" — is only partially built. The deterministic-logic stage (`MadhhabRuleEvaluator`) is real, sound, and honestly self-documented as not-yet-source-verified. The "scholar-approved knowledge base → structured retrieval" stages **do not exist as any database table, corpus, or retrieval layer** — confirmed via exhaustive schema/migration/edge-function grep, zero matches for any KB-shaped table. What substitutes for it today in `fiqh-advisor-chat` is Gemini's own model knowledge plus live Google Search grounding, filtered to a two-domain citation allowlist (`islamweb.net`, `dorar.net`), governed by a soft, prompt-level instruction not to override the deterministic engine or assert unsupported certainty — real and effective as a fail-safe (confirmed via 3 of 4 live adversarial test categories passing), but not the same thing as a scholar-approved structured KB, and should not be described as one.

**`FIQH-8`** (new, Fiqh Authority / Knowledge-Base Governance Correction wave, 2026-09-14) — **No structured, database-backed, scholar-approved fiqh knowledge base or retrieval layer exists anywhere in the codebase or live schema.** `fiqh-advisor-chat`'s actual "knowledge" path is Gemini model knowledge + live Google Search grounding + a citation-domain allowlist, not a queryable corpus. This is an architecture-vs-vision gap, not a currently-unsafe-output defect (the system fails safe today, confirmed adversarially) — severity **HIGH, Fiqh-feature launch blocker** if the feature is to be described or launched as running on a "scholar-approved knowledge base," not blocking if the feature ships with accurate, honest framing that it is AI-generated guidance deferring to human scholars (which the existing user-facing disclaimers — the Fiqh Report PDF footer, the Fiqh Advisor's own degraded-response Arabic text — already substantially do). Status: `OPEN`.

**`FIQH-9`** (new, Fiqh Authority / Knowledge-Base Governance Correction wave, 2026-09-14) — **The Scholar Review Gate (`FIQH_ENGINE_ACCURACY_AUDIT_MASTER.md` Phase 12) is at 0% completion.** Every reviewable fiqh artifact — the 16-entry source registry, the 4-madhhab rule matrix, the 13-country geographic-suggestion mapping, the 7-jurisdiction registry, all 12 golden-dataset cases, and all user-facing religious wording — carries `reviewer_status`/`review_status: NOT_REVIEWED`. `SCHOLAR_REVIEW_PACKAGE.md` is a complete, reviewer-ready request document, but no evidence exists anywhere in this engagement's records that it has been sent to or reviewed by an actual qualified scholar. Severity **HIGH, Fiqh-feature launch blocker** for any claim that Niswah's fiqh guidance is scholar-approved or religiously certified; **not blocking** for a launch that is explicit with users that fiqh content is engineering-derived and not yet scholar-reviewed (a product/disclosure decision, analogous in kind to `PC-006`'s legal-determination gating, not an engineering task). Status: `OPEN`.

**Silent Default Invariant (Fiqh), formalized 2026-09-14**: an unknown or unset madhhab must never silently become a religiously authoritative madhhab. Three distinct default types exist in principle and must be evaluated separately: a **display default** (merely pre-selecting a UI element, no computation consequence) would be low-risk; an **internal calculation default** (feeding a fallback value into `MadhhabRuleEvaluator`) and an **AI context default** (feeding a fallback value into `UserAiContext`/the Fiqh Advisor prompt) are both high-risk, since both produce output presented to the user as if it reflects her actual choice. Confirmed via direct code trace: `MadhhabController`'s `Madhhab.hanbali` fallback is **not** merely a display default — it is both an internal-calculation default and an AI-context default simultaneously (the same controller instance feeds both `MadhhabRuleEvaluator` calls and the `clientMadhhab` value sent to `fiqh-advisor-chat`), with no distinct "unset" state ever modeled or surfaced anywhere in the pipeline. This is the exact invariant violation `AUTH-005` tracks, and this paragraph is the formal governance statement of the rule `AUTH-005`'s remediation must satisfy: any future fix must ensure an unset madhhab is either resolved to a real, user-confirmed value before reaching calculation/AI-context code, or explicitly surfaces an "unset — cannot provide fiqh guidance" state rather than substituting any specific madhhab silently.

## AI User-State Context Layer Remediation Wave (2026-09-09)

**Built one canonical AI context-assembly module** (`supabase/functions/_shared/ai_user_context.ts`) closing the root-cause `AICTX-5` finding — all 4 Edge Functions now share one identity-safe (RLS-scoped `userClient` only, never a service-role client or client-supplied `user_id`), provenance-labeled context builder instead of four independent, mostly-empty ad hoc queries. Full evidence, architecture, authority map, and finding-by-finding reassessment: `production-readiness-results/fiqh-engine/FIQH_AICTX_findings.md`.

**A real mistake was made and caught during this wave**: the first draft reimplemented pregnancy-week math instead of reusing the already-existing, tested `pregnancy_status.ts` — fixed by moving it to `_shared/` and importing it, re-verified via its own 16 pre-existing tests (still passing, executed for real, not assumed).

**Verification, honestly accounted for**: no Deno binary was available in this environment, so real TypeScript strict-mode type-checking (via a locally-installed `tsc`, zero errors across all 8 touched files) and real, *executed* unit tests (bundled to plain JS via `esbuild`, run under Node with a minimal test-runner shim — 13/13 new + 16/16 pre-existing passed) substituted for `deno test`, disclosed as such rather than silently presented as equivalent. `dart analyze`/`flutter test` re-run clean (same 8 pre-existing golden-image diffs, zero new failures). All 4 Edge Functions deployed to production twice (once with a bug found and fixed mid-wave — see below).

**🔴 A serious, unrelated production incident was discovered during this wave's live synthetic verification (Phase L) and is the most urgent item in this update**: `ai-assistant-chat` returned `HTTP 503` on a real synthetic call. Investigation (read-only, three independent confirmation methods) found the `W1-001` rate-limiter's database objects (`ai_rate_limit_counters` table, `check_and_increment_ai_rate_limit()` function) **completely absent from production** — not a full rollback (a synthetic account and row created seconds earlier in the same investigation were present and current, and all other tables/data are intact) but a specific, unexplained disappearance of exactly the `W1-001` migration's objects. **Practical impact: all 4 AI features currently return 503 to every real user.** `W1-001`, `AB-002`, `SEC-005`, `AB-008` are corrected from `VERIFIED_CLOSED` to `PARTIALLY_REMEDIATED`/`🔴 REGRESSED IN PRODUCTION` in their own rows above — the prior evidence trail remains an accurate historical record of what was proven working on 2026-09-08, but does not reflect current production reality. **This session did not attempt to fix it** — root cause is undetermined, and re-applying a production migration requires the same explicit owner authorization every prior `W1-001`-related production change in this engagement has required. Flagged for immediate owner attention.

**A second, smaller, real production defect was found and fixed within this wave's own code before it could ship**: the production `wellbeing_logs` table has no `notes` column (confirmed via direct schema query and a live REST call — `PGRST204`), even though the Dart `WellbeingRepository` always writes one — meaning every real wellbeing check-in currently fails in production, not only ones with note text (tracked as `AICTX-13`, not fixed this wave, outside its explicit scope). This wave's own new context module was initially written assuming that column existed; caught and corrected (query and types both fixed) before the second production deployment. **🟢 VERIFIED_CLOSED, W1001/AICTX-13 wave (2026-09-09)**: explicit owner authorization received; migration applied to production (`ALTER TABLE public.wellbeing_logs ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS notes TEXT`, checksum `c90bdba720211f7f62e6b78fceff19903e69ec53e76adeb19304b66038d92fc7`), column verified live (`text`, nullable, no default, 31 pre-existing rows unaffected, RLS/indexes/constraints unchanged), all 4 AI Edge Functions redeployed and version-confirmed. Full live CRUD + cross-user RLS isolation battery re-proven directly against production with synthetic accounts, all cleaned up afterward. **Live AI-context integration proven with real Gemini replies** — a production `dr-niswah-chat` reply and a production `ai-assistant-chat` reply both correctly cited a real wellbeing note's substance, alongside pregnancy/cycle/symptom state, in the same response. Full evidence in `production-readiness-results/fiqh-engine/FIQH_AICTX_findings.md`'s "AICTX-13 Production Application & Live AI-Context Verification wave" section and `00_09` §57.

**AICTX findings reassessed individually, not mass-closed, per explicit instruction**: `AICTX-5` (root cause) substantially addressed by the new architecture. `AICTX-1`/`AICTX-2`/`AICTX-4`/`AICTX-6`/`AICTX-7` have the correct context now wired in (code-complete, type-checked, unit-tested, deployed) but **could not be verified end-to-end against a real live Gemini call this session** due to the unrelated rate-limiter incident above — their closure is deferred, not claimed, pending that incident's resolution and a real Phase L re-run. `AICTX-3` (Fiqh Advisor) has madhhab plus (when available) the client-computed classification wired in, with the deterministic-state-authority prompt language strengthened. Three new findings registered: `AICTX-10` (disclosed limitation: classification is client-computed, not independently server-verified — a deliberate design choice, not an oversight), `AICTX-11` (documentation: the app's own pregnancy model cannot distinguish "not pregnant" from "no data," a pre-existing fact now made explicit), `AICTX-12` (client-side `clientFiqhState` wiring currently reaches only the Fiqh Advisor, not the other 3 features — a scoped, disclosed follow-up).

**Testing**: `dart analyze lib/` 25 issues (below the 27 baseline), zero errors; `flutter test` same 8 pre-existing golden-image diffs, zero new failures; all TypeScript type-checked and pure-logic-tested for real as described above. **Overall production-readiness verdict remains NO-GO** — and is now materially worse in one specific respect than before this wave started: `W1-001`/`AB-002`/`SEC-005`/`AB-008` are no longer verified-closed, a real, live, user-facing production incident (all 4 AI features returning 503) is active and requires immediate owner attention, independent of and more urgent than this wave's own AICTX remediation work.

Summary: F7 (evidence-provenance UI, six-class taxonomy + reusable badge widget) and F8 (a new canonical month-grid calendar screen — tap-to-inspect, multi-observation revision history, backfill, correction, bounded prediction) were both previously disclosed as **MISSING/not started**; both are now built and tested (`E2_AUTOMATED_VERIFIED`, owner `E4` device retest not yet performed). A genuine Fiqh test regression from the immediately-prior same-day wave was resolved properly (no legacy fallback restored), surfacing and fixing a second, real safety gap along the way (`_PrayerStatusCard` could show a confident "Salah is obligatory" for a factually-open episode whose evidence simply hadn't synced yet). Notification continuity beyond the existing 7-day rolling window received an explicit two-tier design (the existing precise window plus a new OS-native recurring fallback) — **this specific design was found platform-inconsistent and replaced the very next day; see the following wave**. 39 new tests added across 5 test files; zero new test failures versus this branch's own prior baseline. A portable, repository-committed owner E4 script (`docs/owner-e4-menstrual-journey.md`) replaces the prior wave's failed ephemeral-link artifact. PR #4 remains draft/unmerged; no `E4` evidence and no overall launch decision is claimed for any item in this section.

## Menstrual Data Integrity — Independent Review Fixes Wave (2026-09-20)

Same isolated charter as the two waves above (PR #4, still **draft, unmerged**). An independent review of the prior wave's own work found seven real issues; full narrative in `docs/menstrual-data-integrity-contract.md` §11 and `00_12_TRACEABILITY_MATRIX.md`'s revised rows — recorded here only as a pointer.

**The most consequential finding**: the prior wave's own notification-continuity redesign (a 7-day exact rolling window plus an OS-native recurring fallback layered on top) was reviewed against the installed `flutter_local_notifications` plugin's actual native source rather than assumed reliable across platforms. Android genuinely delays a recurring schedule's first fire to a requested future date; **iOS discards the date entirely** for a time-only-matching trigger and fires at the next matching clock time regardless — meaning the "delayed fallback" would, on iOS, have started firing on day 1, duplicating every day the rolling window already covered. This was a real, verified defect, not a theoretical risk, caught before this ever reached a real device. Replaced with a single, predictable architecture: no recurring fallback at all — the same one-off, per-day-exact mechanism, widened from 7 to 30 days.

Six further fixes, all with new or corrected tests: calendar uncertainty announcements no longer collapse "no report" and "explicit uncertain report" into the same phrase; the calendar's "corrected" indicator is now derived from `supersedes_id`, never same-day observation count (which falsely flagged independent same-day reports as corrections); `LoadDegraded` reads are now tracked distinctly from clean success and fully unavailable, with the forward prediction suppressed whenever either read is degraded; an audit of all six `EvidenceProvenance` categories against real production data sources found 2 of 6 (`legacyUnverified`, `userReportedEstimate`) had no real UI surface at all (legend-only) — both now do; the prior wave's own "10 pre-existing failures" claim is corrected against the true PR merge base (`e4cc02e`, not a later same-branch checkpoint) via a fresh `git worktree` — all 10 confirmed genuinely present there, and the one test that was not (a flaky test this PR itself introduced) is fixed outright; the owner E4 script's step 10 (which chained a 5-day uninterrupted delivery test together with a same-run notification tap, invalidating its own "uninterrupted" premise) is split into two independent steps.

Final count: 739 tests, 10 failures — all 10 confirmed identical to the true merge-base baseline. Zero PR-introduced regressions remain. PR #4 remains draft/unmerged; no `E4` evidence and no overall launch decision is claimed for any item in this section.

## Menstrual Data Integrity — Notification Cancellation Closure Wave (2026-09-20, same day, later)

Same isolated charter as the waves above (PR #4, still **draft, unmerged**). Full narrative in `docs/menstrual-data-integrity-contract.md` §12 and `00_12_TRACEABILITY_MATRIX.md`'s new row — recorded here only as a pointer.

Three real gaps closed, all in when the single 30-day window actually gets *cancelled*: (1) disabling the active-bleeding preference previously only stopped scheduling new reminders, leaving an already-scheduled window outstanding — a new bounded cancellation sweep (`_cancelStaleActiveBleedingWindows`, re-deriving candidates from the user's own episode data rather than caching new state) now actually cancels it, and the Settings toggle reconciles the scheduler immediately rather than waiting for the next app resume; (2) a canonical refresh that verifies no open episode remains (including one ended on a different device) now runs the same sweep — Closure Blocker 1 discipline is preserved, a genuine read failure never counts as verified absence and never triggers cancellation; (3) a stale-day-in-the-past sweep was added to catch reminder-time and timezone-shift-induced staleness, and `refreshLocalTimezone(forceRefresh: true)` was centralized inside `NotificationRefreshCoordinator.refresh()` itself so every caller — not only `main.dart`'s resume handler — picks up a genuinely current device zone before rescheduling (this closes a real, pre-existing gap in the dashboard's own contextual-consent call site as a side effect).

Eight new integration tests against the real coordinator with a mocked plugin channel prove: zero outstanding ids after disable; exactly one fresh reminder per day after re-enable; a preferred-time change reflected in outstanding reminders; cancellation on the next successful refresh after a remote episode end; zero false cancellation on a read failure; `cancelAll` reached on account switch with independent re-scheduling after; a rejected platform cancel never recorded as a false `cancelled` audit event (recovery still possible on a later successful call); other notification types untouched by the sweep.

Final count: 747 tests, 10 failures — the same 10 golden/parity-image failures already confirmed identical to the true merge-base baseline in the prior wave. Zero PR-introduced regressions remain. BR-002 migration reproducibility reconfirmed passing (no SQL touched this wave). PR #4 remains draft/unmerged; no `E4` evidence is claimed — cross-device cancellation remains bounded by "this device's next successful refresh," never instantaneous, never claimed while offline or never-refreshed.

## Menstrual Data Integrity — Local Notification Reconciliation Closure Wave (2026-09-21)

Same isolated charter as the waves above (PR #4, still **draft, unmerged**). Full narrative in `docs/menstrual-data-integrity-contract.md` §13 and `00_12_TRACEABILITY_MATRIX.md`'s new row — recorded here only as a pointer.

The prior wave's own cancellation sweeps still called `getEpisodesForUser` — a Supabase read — to decide what to cancel, meaning an explicit local opt-out (or a verified episode end) could silently fail to take effect while offline, mid-outage, or with an expired session: exactly the scenario a *local* notification toggle must never depend on a network round trip for. Fixed by adding `NotificationService.pendingActiveBleedingReminders()`, a thin, purely-local wrapper over `flutter_local_notifications`' own `pendingNotificationRequests()` API that decodes each request's existing opaque payload (never exposing plugin internals) and returns only well-formed, structurally-owned active-bleeding entries — a malformed payload, wrong type, or missing field is silently excluded, never crashes the sweep. The coordinator now reconciles against this actual OS-reported set: disable and verified-no-open-episode cancel every pending id the payload attributes to the current user, with zero `getEpisodesForUser` calls (verified directly via a call counter in the test fakes); the eligible-episode path reconciles `actualOwnedPendingIds - desiredIds` in one pass, which subsumes the prior wave's separate today's-id and stale-day sweeps and correctly handles a shifted window from any cause (timezone change, day-forward progression, or a preferred-time change) without needing `episode.startDate` as a bound.

Twelve integration tests (replacing the prior wave's 8) against the real coordinator with a mocked plugin channel extended to simulate the OS's own live pending-request state prove: Supabase-unavailable disable (zero owned ids remain, zero Supabase reads); Supabase-unavailable-while-enabled (no false episode-end inference); verified-clean-episode-end with zero second reads; a simulated day-boundary shift backward/forward correctly cancels the old out-of-window edge and adds the new one; a preferred-time change with no stale duplicates; a malformed pending payload ignored safely; cross-user isolation; unrelated-notification-type isolation; a rejected cancellation never falsely audited and never aborting the rest of the sweep; plus re-enable and account-switch regression coverage carried forward.

Final count: 751 tests, 10 failures — the same 10 golden/parity-image failures already confirmed identical to the true merge-base baseline. Zero PR-introduced regressions remain. BR-002 migration reproducibility could not be re-run to a clean pass this wave: three genuine, back-to-back attempts each timed out identically at `supabase start`'s own container health-check stage in this sandbox (analytics/vector/realtime/storage/pg_meta/studio reported unhealthy), before the script's own schema-validation SQL ever ran — a docker/environment resource constraint, not a code issue, since this wave touched zero SQL/migration files. PR #4 remains draft/unmerged; no `E4` evidence is claimed.
